Using 139 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV pp data, it sets 95% CL limits in the λ₁–λ₂ coupling plane for mediator masses 1.5–2.0 TeV, excluding λ₁ > 0.45 (b-tagged dijet) and λ₁ > 0.3 & λ₂ > 0.6 (bottom-squark-like channel).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simultaneously explain the baryon asymmetry of the universe and dark matter relic abundance via a baryon-number-violating model with two scalar mediators, and to improve constraints by leveraging heavy-flavor tagging and jet-rich final states beyond inclusive dijet or standard monotop analyses.

Method: Reinterpretation of ATLAS 13 TeV searches (b-tagged dijets and monotop-alike signatures) using 139 fb⁻¹ of data; mapping experimental upper limits onto the λ₁–λ₂ coupling parameter space of a two-scalar mediator model, assuming decays to down-type quark pairs or top + DM; benchmarking at mediator masses 1.5, 1.75, and 2.0 TeV.

Result: 95% CL exclusion limits derived in λ₁–λ₂ plane: b-tagged dijet analysis excludes λ₁ > 0.45 across all three benchmark masses; bottom-squark reinterpretation excludes λ₁ > 0.3 and λ₂ > 0.6 within validated parameter domains.

Conclusion: The b-tagged dijet and monotop-alike reinterpretations provide complementary, improved sensitivity to the baryon-number-violating two-scalar model compared to inclusive searches, significantly constraining its viable coupling parameter space.

Abstract: A reinterpretation of ATLAS searches targeting $b$-tagged dijet resonances and monotop-alike signatures at a center-of-mass energy of 13 TeV is presented using $pp$ collision data corresponding to integrated luminosities of 139 $\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$. These searches are used to constrain a baryon-number-violating new physics model that introduces two scalar mediators $X_{1}$ and $X_{2}$ to simultaneously address the baryon asymmetry of the universe and the dark matter relic abundance. By exploiting heavy-flavor signatures and allowing for jet-rich final states, this reinterpretation provides complementary sensitivity to the model relative to non-flavor-tagged inclusive dijet searches and conventional monotop reinterpretations. Assuming the mediators decay either into a pair of down-type quarks or into a top quark and a dark matter candidate, we translate the experimental upper limits into the model's parameter space. 95% confidence level exclusion limits are derived in the $λ_1$-$λ_2$ coupling plane for benchmark mediator masses of 1.5, 1.75, and 2.0 TeV. For all three benchmark masses, the $b$-tagged dijet reinterpretation excludes $λ_1 > 0.45$, while the bottom-squark reinterpretation excludes the region with $λ_1 > 0.3$ and $λ_2 > 0.6$, within the respective validated parameter domains.

</details>


### [20] [Dijet acoplanarity as a function of flattenicity in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04097)
*Jesus Eduardo Muñoz Mendez,Antonio Ortiz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates event classification biases in LHC pp collision analyses searching for QGP signatures; it finds multiplicity-based classifiers introduce spurious azimuthal broadening, whereas flattenicity-based triggering eliminates this bias and thus offers a cleaner probe for jet quenching in small systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ambiguity surrounding apparent QGP-like effects in pp collisions—whose origin is unclear due to known selection biases from multiplicity-based event estimators.

Method: Comparative analysis of event classifiers (including multiplicity-based and flattenicity) using PYTHIA 8 simulations of 13 TeV pp collisions; evaluation via dijet acoplanarity and azimuthal broadening.

Result: Multiplicity-based classifiers induce artificial azimuthal broadening even in absence of jet quenching (PYTHIA 8), while flattenicity-triggered events show no such effect—indicating reduced or absent bias.

Conclusion: Flattenicity is a robust, bias-mitigating event classifier that enables more reliable jet-quenching searches in small systems and should be adopted in LHC analyses targeting QGP formation in pp collisions.

Abstract: One of the most important discoveries at the LHC is the presence of effects associated with quark-gluon plasma (QGP) formation in proton-proton (pp) collisions. The origin of these observations remains unknown. Since event selection in many studies relies on multiplicity-based estimators known to introduce selection biases, alternative approaches must be explored to elucidate the origin of these phenomena.
  This contribution presents a comparison of different event classifiers used in LHC experiments, aiming to assess their mutual compatibility. Results for pp collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV simulated with PYTHIA 8 indicate that the different classifiers introduce biases that can obscure any evidence of QGP formation in small systems. However, these biases are significantly reduced or absent in events triggered with flattenicity. This finding motivates the application of flattenicity in dijet acoplanarity studies used to search for jet quenching effects. While multiplicity-based estimators produce an azimuthal broadening in high-multiplicity compared with minimum-bias pp collisions, even in PYTHIA 8 that does not incorporate jet quenching, events triggered with flattenicity show no such broadening. This suggests that this novel tool could be exploited by LHC experiments to refine jet-quenching searches in small systems.

</details>


### [21] [Energy shift and decay width of kaonium from coupled-channel one-loop chiral amplitudes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04120)
*Alireza Beygi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the energy shift and decay width of kaonium (a hypothetical $K^+ K^-$ Coulomb-bound atom) using two one-loop chiral coupled-channel amplitudes; it finds larger ground-state widths (1.67 and 0.92 keV) than prior predictions, showing sensitivity to both real and imaginary parts of the $K^+ K^-$ scattering amplitude near threshold, while confirming level separation for lowest s-wave states and negligible impact on photon-fusion line shapes after experimental resolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies in theoretical predictions of kaonium’s decay width and clarify the role of real vs. imaginary parts of the $K^+ K^-$ scattering amplitude—including Coulomb corrections—in determining its strong-interaction width.

Method: Uses two independently fitted coupled-channel one-loop chiral perturbation theory amplitudes to compute the strong-interaction energy shift and decay width of the kaonium ground state; incorporates Coulomb corrections and compares with previous LO and non-perturbative results; analyzes s-wave level spacing and photon-fusion line shape including Belle detector resolution.

Result: Ground-state widths of 1.67 keV and 0.92 keV—both significantly larger than the 0.30 keV LO prediction; the two amplitudes yield scattering lengths with nearly equal Im but opposite-sign Re parts, causing opposing Coulomb corrections; first three s-wave levels remain resolved; intrinsic width increase has negligible effect on photon-fusion line shape after convolution with Belle’s experimental resolution.

Conclusion: Kaonium’s width is sensitive to both real and imaginary parts of the near-threshold $K^+ K^-$ amplitude; higher-order chiral dynamics substantially enhance the width over LO estimates; despite this, kaonium remains spectroscopically resolvable in s-wave, and its experimental signature in photon fusion is robust against width changes.

Abstract: Using two fitted coupled-channel one-loop chiral amplitudes, we investigate the strong-interaction energy shift and decay width of the hypothetical Coulomb-bound $K^+ K^-$ hadronic atom, kaonium. We obtain ground-state widths of 1.67 and 0.92 keV, which are both larger than previous kaonium predictions, including the 0.30 keV result obtained from a non-perturbative treatment based on leading-order chiral perturbation theory. Although the $K^+ K^-$ scattering lengths obtained from the two one-loop amplitudes have nearly equal imaginary parts, their real parts have opposite signs; thus, the leading absorptive contributions to the widths are similar, while the Coulomb corrections act in opposite directions. The kaonium width therefore depends on both the real and imaginary parts of the near-threshold strong amplitude. Despite the increased ground-state width, the first three $s$-wave levels remain separated. In photon fusion, at fixed pole area and resonance position, the increase in the intrinsic width has a negligible effect on the line shape after including Belle's experimental resolution.

</details>


### [22] [Spontaneous Spin Polarization in Dense Quark Matter Reexamined](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04160)
*Carlisson Miller,Rodolfo Casana,Manoel M. Ferreira,Gastão Krein*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the quark spin-polarized (QSP) phase in cold, dense quark matter using the two-flavor NJL model with tensor interactions, comparing two regularization schemes—TRS and MSS—and finds that MSS yields physically robust, causal, monotonic behavior with persistent spin polarization at high density, potentially impacting neutron star magnetism and structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in the phase structure of high-baryon-density QCD matter, particularly the stability and physical viability of the quark spin-polarized (QSP) phase, and assess its implications for neutron star magnetic fields and equation of state.

Method: Analyzes the two-flavor Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model with tensor interactions; compares two regularization schemes—traditional regularization scheme (TRS) with sharp cutoff on both vacuum and medium terms, and medium separation scheme (MSS) isolating divergent vacuum contributions while keeping medium phase space cutoff-free; computes condensates, thermodynamics, causality constraints, spontaneous magnetization, and constructs hybrid stars.

Result: TRS yields nonmonotonic QSP condensate evolution, thermodynamic instabilities, and potential causality violations at high chemical potential; MSS yields monotonic QSP growth, causal equation of state, increasing spontaneous magnetization, and qualitatively distinct hybrid-star mass-radius relations and stability trends.

Conclusion: The medium separation scheme (MSS) provides a physically consistent treatment yielding a robust, high-density spin-polarized quark phase; such a persistent QSP phase may contribute microscopically to strong magnetic fields in compact stars—including magnetars—and significantly alter neutron star structure and stability.

Abstract: The phase structure of strongly interacting matter at high baryon density remains an open problem in QCD, with important implications for the internal structure and magnetic properties of neutron stars. We reexamine the emergence of a quark spin-polarized (QSP) phase in cold, dense quark matter within the two-flavor Nambu-Jona-Lasinio model with tensor interactions, focusing on its dependence on regularization. We compare the traditional regularization scheme (TRS), where a sharp momentum cutoff is applied to both vacuum and medium contributions, with the medium separation scheme (MSS), where ultraviolet-divergent vacuum terms are isolated while finite medium-dependent phase-space contributions remain cutoff free. The two prescriptions lead to qualitatively different results. In TRS, the QSP condensate eventually collapses at high chemical potential, leading to nonmonotonic thermodynamic behavior and, for some couplings, violations of causality. In MSS, the QSP condensate instead increases monotonically with density, the equation of state remains causal, and the associated spontaneous magnetization grows toward high density. The persistence of spin polarization at high density may provide a microscopic contribution to the strong magnetic fields of compact stars, including magnetars. An exploratory hybrid-star construction further shows that the QSP phase can significantly modify the equation of state, stability trends, and mass-radius relations. These results highlight the importance of a consistent treatment of medium phase space and demonstrate the potential impact of a persistent spin-polarized quark phase on neutron-star physics.

</details>


### [23] [A Composite Three-Body-Holonomy Toy Framework for Dynamical Electroweak-Scale Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04202)
*Tadashi Yoshikawa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a three-state hidden sector model where the number three emerges from a fundamental three-body holonomy in a relativistic 1+1D Dirac system, not from an imposed gauge symmetry; strong SU(3)_H dynamics generates a composite condensate whose cyclic orientation is selected by the holonomy; an anomalous cubic determinant interaction yields a holonomy-dependent potential and dimensional transmutation sets the composite scale; the radial excitation S₃ couples to the Higgs via a holonomy-dependent CP-even portal, enabling dynamical electroweak symmetry breaking and generation of the electroweak scale from the hidden-sector scale in a classically scale-invariant setup.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the origin of the number three in particle physics (e.g., three generations, three colors) not as an ad hoc gauge symmetry but as a consequence of a more primitive topological structure — specifically, a genuine three-body holonomy arising from a relativistic (1+1)-dimensional Dirac system.

Method: Constructs a phenomenological toy framework elevated to a minimal three-state internal sector in (3+1)D, realized via composite strong dynamics of an asymptotically free hidden SU(3)_H gauge theory with three SM-singlet Dirac flavors; incorporates anomalous cubic determinant interaction induced by the holonomy; employs dimensional transmutation and holonomy-dependent effective potentials; introduces a CP-even portal coupling between the radial composite scalar S₃ and the SM Higgs doublet.

Result: The holonomy selects the cyclic internal orientation of the composite condensate; the cubic determinant interaction generates a holonomy-dependent potential; dimensional transmutation establishes a composite scale; S₃ couples to the Higgs and shifts its quadratic term, triggering electroweak symmetry breaking; in the classically scale-invariant limit, the electroweak scale is dynamically generated from the hidden-sector scale via dimensionless portal couplings.

Conclusion: The number three can emerge organically from a topological three-body holonomy rather than being imposed by gauge symmetry; this provides a unified origin for multiple 'threefold' structures in particle physics and offers a natural, scale-invariant mechanism for electroweak symmetry breaking driven by strong hidden dynamics.

Abstract: We propose a phenomenological toy framework promoted to a minimal three-state internal sector in $(3+1)$ dimensions and realized by composite strong dynamics. It is in a genuine three-body holonomy which originates in a relativistic $(1+1)$-dimensional Dirac system. The hypothesis is that the number three is not imposed first as a gauge symmetry, but is inherited from the primitive closed cyclic structure of the three-body holonomy. This threefold structure subsequently reappears as a three-state internal space, the three-site loop, a three-flavor hidden sector, and a cubic determinant interaction.
  We introduce an asymptotically free hidden $SU(3)_H$ gauge theory with three Standard-Model-singlet Dirac flavors. Strong hidden dynamics generates a composite condensate, while the inherited holonomy selects its cyclic internal orientation. The anomalous cubic determinant interaction produces a holonomy-dependent potential, and dimensional transmutation generates the characteristic composite scale.
  The radial composite excitation along the cyclic direction, denoted by $S_3$, couples to the Standard-Model Higgs doublet through a holonomy-dependent CP-even portal. The resulting condensate shifts the Higgs quadratic term and can trigger electroweak symmetry breaking. In the classically scale-invariant reference limit, the electroweak scale is induced from the dynamically generated hidden-sector scale through dimensionless portal couplings.

</details>


### [24] [Pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone Boson Motion from Thermal Thresholds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04265)
*Mussawir Khan,Ali Muhammad,Tianjun Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel finite-temperature mechanism for coherent motion of pseudo-Nambu–Goldstone bosons (pNGBs), where differential thermal evolution of misaligned symmetry-breaking sources rotates the effective vacuum direction—inducing field motion even from an initially stationary minimum; demonstrated in the Majoron sector of Type-I seesaw, with implications for spontaneous leptogenesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain coherent motion of light scalar fields (e.g., pNGBs) in the early Universe without invoking ad hoc initial conditions (displacement or velocity) or temperature-dependent potentials—instead seeking a microscopic, thermally driven origin rooted in multi-scale symmetry breaking.

Method: Analyzes finite-temperature effective potential of a pNGB (Majoron) coupled to heavy neutrinos with non-degenerate thermal decoupling temperatures in a Type-I seesaw framework; computes thermal evolution of the effective vacuum angle and field dynamics via tracking of the time-dependent minimum in the thermal potential.

Result: Demonstrates that differential thermal freeze-out of heavy neutrinos rotates the thermal vacuum direction by |Δθ_T| ≈ 0.76 rad, driving the Majoron to peak velocity |θ̇|/H ≈ 0.62 while remaining within ~1 mrad of the instantaneous minimum—confirming robust adiabatic tracking.

Conclusion: Establishes a generic, microscopic finite-temperature origin for pNGB dynamics, applicable beyond Majorons to any system with multiple non-aligned, hierarchically thermalizing symmetry-breaking sources—offering new pathways for spontaneous leptogenesis and early-universe scalar coherence.

Abstract: Coherent motion of light scalar fields in the early Universe is typically attributed to an initial displacement, a primordial velocity, or a prescribed temperature-dependent potential. We identify a distinct microscopic mechanism in which differential thermal evolution of multiple non-aligned symmetry-breaking sources rotates the effective vacuum direction of a pseudo-Nambu--Goldstone boson (pNGB), driving motion from an initially stationary minimum. The mechanism is realized in the Majoron sector of a Type-I seesaw mechanism, where heavy neutrinos with separated thermal thresholds supply the required differential thermal evolution. For a representative benchmark, the thermal source direction rotates by $|Δθ_T|\simeq0.76$~rad and drives the Majoron to a peak velocity $|\dotθ|/H\simeq0.62$, while the field tracks the moving minimum to sub-milliradian accuracy. This establishes a microscopic finite-temperature origin for pNGB motion, with implications for spontaneous leptogenesis and, more generally, for any system with multiple non-aligned symmetry-breaking sources that evolve differently with temperature.

</details>


### [25] [Amplitude-level NLO predictions for exclusive $J/ψ+γ$ production in ultraperipheral collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04293)
*Xi-Jie Zhan,Tai-Fu Feng,Xing-Gang Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines exclusive γγ → J/ψ + γ production in the color-singlet channel at leading order in heavy-quark velocity, highlighting that including the full one-loop squared amplitude (Q) — beyond conventional NLO (B+V) — ensures positivity of the differential cross section, improves scale stability, and significantly modifies integrated rates and kinematic distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve unphysical negativity in the conventional NLO (B+V) differential cross section at high p_T and to assess the impact of retaining the complete one-loop squared term (Q) on theoretical reliability, scale dependence, and phenomenological predictions for ultraperipheral collisions.

Method: Leading-order NRQCD-based calculation of γγ → J/ψ + γ in the color-singlet channel; comparison of conventional NLO (Born + virtual interference, B+V) versus extended scheme (B+V+Q), where Q is the full one-loop amplitude squared; evaluation across photon-photon energies (W = 80, 160 GeV) and hadronic collision systems (pp at 14 TeV, Pb–Pb at √s_NN = 5.52 TeV); renormalization scale variation over μ_R = {W/2, W, 2W}.

Result: The conventional B+V spectrum turns negative at high p_T for W = 80–160 GeV even at μ_R = W, while B+V+Q remains non-negative; total partonic cross section exceeds Born due to positive virtual contributions from other scattering directions; inclusion of Q increases integrated ultraperipheral cross sections by 11.6%–18.4% and reduces scale uncertainties by 36%–67%; differential distributions (e.g., p_T spectra) are quantitatively reshaped.

Conclusion: Retaining the full one-loop squared term Q is essential for physical consistency (positivity), improved perturbative stability (reduced scale dependence), and accurate prediction of both integrated yields and differential observables in exclusive J/ψ+γ photoproduction.

Abstract: Exclusive $γγ\to J/ψ+γ$ production is studied in the color-singlet channel at leading order in the heavy-quark velocity. The conventional next-to-leading-order (NLO) result $B+V$, comprising Born production and its interference with the renormalized one-loop amplitude, is compared with $B+V+Q$, where $Q$ retains the complete one-loop square. At photon-photon energies $W=80~\mathrm{GeV}$ and $160~\mathrm{GeV}$, the conventional spectrum becomes negative over finite high-$p_T$ intervals already at renormalization scale $μ_R=W$, whereas the complete square remains non-negative. In contrast, the total partonic cross section exceeds Born at the larger energies in the energy scan, owing to positive virtual contributions from other scattering directions. For $p$-$p$ collisions at $14~\mathrm{TeV}$ and $Pb$-$Pb$ collisions at $\sqrt{s_{NN}}=5.52~\mathrm{TeV}$, $Q$ raises the central integrated ultraperipheral rates by $11.6\%$--$18.4\%$. Their absolute scale widths over $μ_R=\{W/2,W,2W\}$ decrease by $36\%$--$67\%$. Retaining the complete one-loop square changes both the predicted integrated rates and the shapes of the differential distributions.

</details>


### [26] [Angular Observables in the $Λ_{b}\to Λν\barν$ Decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04309)
*Wolfgang Altmannshofer,Shibasis Roy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes angular observables in the rare baryonic decay Λ_b → Λ(→ pπ⁻)νν̄ to probe chiral and CP-violating structures of b → s νν̄ transitions beyond what mesonic decays (e.g., B → K(*)νν̄) can access; it identifies a T-odd CP asymmetry A₅ sensitive to Im(C_L C_R*), provides SM predictions using lattice QCD form factors, and assesses FCC-ee sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve parameter degeneracies and fully characterize the chiral (C_L vs C_R) and CP-violating structure of new physics in b → s νν̄ transitions — capabilities lacking in conventional mesonic decays.

Method: Analysis of the five independent angular observables in the polarized Λ_b → Λ(→ pπ⁻)νν̄ decay at the Z pole; derivation of sensitivity to |C_R|² − |C_L|² and Im(C_L C_R*); computation of SM predictions using state-of-the-art lattice QCD form factors; projection of new physics reach at FCC-ee.

Result: Four of the five angular observables require initial Λ_b polarization; A₅ is identified as a T-odd CP asymmetry directly probing Im(C_L C_R*); SM branching ratio and all angular observables are predicted; FCC-ee offers high sensitivity to chiral and CP-violating NP scenarios.

Conclusion: Baryonic angular observables in Λ_b → Λνν̄ are indispensable for disentangling Wilson coefficients C_L and C_R and their CP phases — complementing and extending the physics reach of mesonic b → s νν̄ probes.

Abstract: We study the rare decay $Λ_b \rightarrow Λ(\rightarrow pπ^-)ν\barν$ of polarized $Λ_b$ baryons produced at the $Z$ pole. We find that the differential decay distribution is characterized by five independent angular observables, four of which require initial $Λ_b$ polarization. While standard observables in mesonic $b \rightarrow s ν\barν$ decays ($B \rightarrow K^{(*)} ν\barν$, $B_s \rightarrow φν\barν$) probe only two combinations of short-distance Wilson coefficients, $|C_L + C_R|$ and $|C_L - C_R|$, the angular observables in $Λ_b \rightarrow Λ(\rightarrow pπ^-)ν\barν$ provide access to the orthogonal combinations $|C_R|^2 - |C_L|^2$ and $\text{Im}(C_L C_R^*)$. In particular, we identify a $T$-odd CP asymmetry, $A_5$, that directly probes CP-violating phases in the short-distance effective Hamiltonian. We present Standard Model predictions for the branching ratio and all angular observables using lattice QCD form factors, and evaluate the projected new physics sensitivity at the FCC-ee. Our results demonstrate that baryonic angular observables are essential for resolving parameter degeneracies and for completely characterizing the chiral and CP structure of possible new physics in $b \rightarrow s ν\barν$ transitions.

</details>


### [27] [Chiral Vortical Transport under Minimal-Length Deformations in Rotating Relativistic Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04324)
*Sameer Ahmad Mir,Behnam Pourhassan,Nawal H. Siddig,Euaggelos E. Zotos,Laila Abdulaziz Al-Essa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how a minimal length scale modifies the chiral vortical effect (CVE) — a rotation-induced current in chiral fermionic matter — by isotropically deforming the momentum-space density of states (DoS). It finds that leading CVE contributions (linear in μ and T) remain unchanged at first order in DoS deformation, while minimal-length effects enter only via higher-order thermal and chemical-potential terms, some tied to anomaly structures and others independent; coefficients depend on the specific DoS deformation. For the Kempf-Mangano-Mann model, a positive minimal length always suppresses the DoS contribution to vortical conductivity, and Planck-scale corrections are negligible under heavy-ion freeze-out conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum-gravitational minimal-length scales—encoded via modified momentum-space density of states—affect chiral transport phenomena, specifically the chiral vortical effect, which is linked to fundamental anomalies but may receive novel corrections beyond standard effective field theory.

Method: Analytic computation of the vortical conductivity from the static, long-wavelength current–momentum density correlation function, using an isotropic deformation of the momentum-space density of states. The analysis starts with a single right-handed Weyl fermion and generalizes to massless chiral systems with commuting conserved charges; explicit evaluation for the Kempf-Mangano-Mann minimal-length deformation is performed.

Result: 1) Leading-order CVE contributions (∝ μ and ∝ T²) are robust against isotropic DoS deformations at first order; 2) Minimal-length effects appear only in higher-order terms in μ and T, including combinations matching gauge/mixed gauge-gravitational anomalies and a new independent combination; 3) Coefficients of these corrections are deformation-dependent—not fixed by 4D anomaly structure; 4) For Kempf-Mangano-Mann deformation, positive minimal length monotonically reduces the DoS contribution to vortical conductivity; 5) Planck-suppressed corrections are numerically negligible (~10⁻²⁰) under RHIC/LHC chemical freeze-out conditions.

Conclusion: Isotropic minimal-length modifications do not alter the leading anomaly-driven chiral vortical response within the DoS sector, but introduce deformation-dependent higher-order corrections—some anomaly-related, some novel—demonstrating that quantum-gravity effects can selectively modify subleading transport without violating anomaly constraints.

Abstract: We study how a minimal length can affect the chiral vortical effect, in which rotation produces a current in matter made of chiral fermions. We focus on an isotropic modification of the momentum-space density-of-state (DoS) and determine the vortical conductivity from the static, long-wavelength correlation between the current and momentum density. The analysis is carried out first for a single right-handed Weyl fermion and is then extended to general massless chiral fermion systems with mutually commuting conserved charges. We find that, for a broad class of analytic isotropic DoS modifications, the familiar leading contributions from chemical potential and temperature remain unchanged at first order within the DoS sector. Minimal-length effects instead appear through higher-order thermal and chemical-potential contributions. Some of these corrections contain the same chiral charge combinations that appear in gauge and mixed gauge-gravitational anomalies, while another contains an independent higher-order charge combination. Their coefficients depend on the form of the DoS modification and are therefore not fixed by the usual four-dimensional anomaly structure. For the Kempf-Mangano-Mann deformation, the exact result shows that a positive minimal-length parameter always reduces the DoS contribution to the vortical conductivity. We also find that a Planck-suppressed correction is extremely small under standard heavy-ion chemical freeze-out conditions. These results show that isotropic minimal-length modifications can change higher-order contributions to chiral vortical transport while leaving the familiar leading anomaly-related response unchanged within the DoS sector considered here.

</details>


### [28] [Freeze-in Asymmetric Dark Matter with a Dual-Role Scalar: CP Violation and Symmetric-Component Depletion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04354)
*S. Peyman Zakeri*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a minimal freeze-in model for asymmetric fermionic dark matter, where DM asymmetry arises from CP-violating decays of a scalar mediator φ, aided by interference with a heavier scalar S; the model naturally reproduces the observed relic density and evades current experimental constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a predictive, minimal, and testable framework for asymmetric fermionic dark matter within the freeze-in paradigm, addressing the origin of DM asymmetry and efficient symmetric-component annihilation without conflicting with observational bounds.

Method: Extends the SM with a Dirac DM fermion ψ, auxiliary fermion χ, real scalar mediator φ, and heavier real scalar S, all protected by Z₂ × Z₂′ symmetry; solves coupled Boltzmann equations for asymmetric (Y₋) and total (YΣ) yields; derives and numerically verifies scaling relations for relic abundance.

Result: Analytic scaling Y₋^∞ ∝ μ²ε/mφ and Ω_DM h² ∝ mψ μ²ε/mφ confirmed; correct relic density (Ω_DM h² ≈ 0.12) achieved for benchmark parameters (ε = 10⁻⁶, mφ = 200 GeV, μ ≈ 2.7×10⁻⁹ GeV, mψ ≈ 40 GeV); all predicted signals (direct/indirect detection, collider, self-interaction) lie below current experimental sensitivities.

Conclusion: The model provides a self-consistent, predictive, and experimentally viable realization of asymmetric fermionic dark matter in the freeze-in regime, with testable signatures potentially accessible in next-generation experiments.

Abstract: We present a minimal, predictive scenario for asymmetric fermionic dark matter (DM) generated through freeze-in. The standard model (SM) is extended by a Dirac fermion $ψ$ that serves as the DM candidate, an auxiliary Dirac fermion $χ$, a real scalar mediator $φ$, and a heavier real scalar $S$, all stabilized by a $Z_2 \times Z_2'$ discrete symmetry. The DM asymmetry is generated through the CP-violating out-of-equilibrium decays of the mediator $φ$, while the heavier scalar $S$ plays a dual role: it generates the physical CP-violating phase required for the asymmetry through its interference with $φ$, and it mediates the annihilation of the symmetric component of the DM population. We solve the coupled Boltzmann equations for the asymmetric yield $Y_-$ and the total yield $Y_Σ$, and identify the scaling relations $Y_-^\infty \propto μ^2 \varepsilon / m_φ$ and $Ω_{\rm DM} h^2 \propto m_ψ\, μ^2 \varepsilon / m_φ$, which we verify numerically. The observed relic abundance $Ω_{\rm DM} h^2 \simeq 0.12$ is naturally reproduced for a wide range of parameters; in particular, for $\varepsilon = 10^{-6}$ and $m_φ= 200$~GeV, the correct relic density is obtained for $μ\approx 2.7 \times 10^{-9}$~GeV and $m_ψ\approx 40$~GeV. We further confront the model with current experimental constraints, including direct detection, indirect detection, collider searches, and DM self-interactions, and show that the predicted signals lie far below the sensitivity of current experiments. This scenario provides a self-consistent and testable realization of asymmetric fermionic DM within the freeze-in framework.

</details>


### [29] [Pion gravitational form factors in an algebraic rainbow-ladder model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04386)
*Jiayin Kang,Lei Chang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the pion's gravitational form factors within the rainbow-ladder truncation of QCD, decomposing contributions from quark, dressed-quark gluon, and binding gluon components; it yields A(0)=1, D(0)=−1 in the chiral limit and momentum fractions ⟨x⟩_q=0.896, ⟨x⟩_g=0.104, with D-term partition mirroring the momentum fraction split.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle quark and gluon contributions to the pion's gravitational form factors—key probes of hadron mass structure and mechanical properties—within a controlled QCD-based framework.

Method: Uses rainbow-ladder truncation of the Dyson–Schwinger equations combined with an algebraic quark model featuring a dynamical basis to compute the gravitational form factors A(t) and D(t), and to separate contributions by physical origin (quark, dressing gluon, binding gluon).

Result: Obtains A(0) = 1 and D(0) = −1 in the chiral limit; momentum fractions are ⟨x⟩_q = 0.896 and ⟨x⟩_g = 0.104; the D-term satisfies the same quark–gluon partition at the model scale.

Conclusion: The pion’s mass and mechanical structure are dominantly quark-driven at the model scale, but gluons—both as dressings and binders—play quantitatively significant roles, especially in the D-term which encodes internal pressure and shear forces.

Abstract: Within rainbow-ladder truncation, we identify three contributions to the pion's gravitational form factors---graviton coupling to the quark, to the gluon dressing the quark, and to the gluon binding the pion---and separate them into quark and gluon parts. Using an algebraic quark model with a dynamical basis, we obtain $A(0)=1$, $D(0)=-1$ in the chiral limit, and momentum fractions $\langle x\rangle_q=0.896$, $\langle x\rangle_g=0.104$. The D-term obeys the same partition at the model scale.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>

Method: Survey and comparative analysis of experimental approaches: fixed-target neutrino and electron scattering, atomic parity violation in heavy atoms, and planned parity-violating electron scattering (PVES) experiments at JLab (e.g., MOLLER, P2) and Mainz (P2 experiment), all operating at Q² ≪ M_Z² to probe radiative corrections and potential BSM effects.

Abstract: We report on the status and prospects of precision weak neutral current interaction measurements in fixed target scattering, atomic parity violation, as well as future collider measurements at momentum transfer $\sqrt{Q^2} \ll M_Z$, including the experiments in parity-violating electron scattering under construction at Jefferson Laboratory and at Mainz that are expected to commence data collection in the very near future.

</details>


### [31] [Violation and Restoration of Probability Positivity in Perturbative Quantum Field Theory: From Quantum Mechanics to Quantum Chromodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04557)
*Bin Gong,Xu-Dong Huang,Jian-Xiong Wang,Chao Zeng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper addresses the long-standing issue of negative decay/production rates in exclusive QCD processes at higher orders (NLO/NNLO) due to improper truncation of squared amplitudes; it proposes amplitude-level NRQCD factorization—where infrared cancellations occur per amplitude (not per squared amplitude)—to restore manifest positivity and improve convergence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Negative predictions for exclusive decay and production rates in QCD at NLO and NNLO arise from applying infrared-truncation practices inherited from inclusive QED, where such truncation is justified by small coupling and KLN theorem; but in exclusive colorless/neutral processes, IR divergences cancel amplitude-wise, making full modulus-squared truncation both valid and necessary for positivity.

Method: The authors formulate amplitude-level NRQCD factorization, ensuring IR safety at the amplitude level (not just the cross section), derive a generic effective representation for truncated observables as the modulus square of a complex perturbative series, and validate it on complete NNLO calculations for several exclusive charmonium and bottomonium processes (e.g., J/ψ→3γ, e⁺e⁻→J/ψ+J/ψ, eight B-factory charmonium production channels).

Result: The amplitude-level prescription yields strictly positive decay and production rates at NNLO, eliminates unphysical negative values, and improves perturbative convergence compared to conventional squared-amplitude truncation.

Conclusion: For exclusive color-singlet (QCD) or electrically neutral (QED) processes, positivity is restored by squaring the fully IR-finite truncated amplitude—not by truncating the squared amplitude—making amplitude-level factorization the physically correct and systematically improvable framework.

Abstract: In quantum mechanics the modulus square of the wave function is the probability of an event --- positivity is self-evident. Quantum field theory corresponds to quantum mechanics one-to-one: the wave function corresponds to the matrix element, so the probability of a process should be the modulus square of the matrix element, positive by construction. In the founding era of perturbative quantum field theory, however, Feynman, Schwinger and Tomonaga treated inclusive processes in quantum electrodynamics, where the truncation of the squared amplitude was forced by infrared cancellations and rendered harmless by the small coupling $α\simeq1/137$. Carried over to exclusive processes in quantum chromodynamics, where the coupling is large, this practice produced negative decay and production rates already at NLO, persisting at NNLO. We point out that for exclusive processes, whose initial and final states are colorless for QCD and electrically neutral for QED, the infrared divergences cancel amplitude by amplitude and the KLN constraint is absent, so the complete modulus square of the truncated amplitude is the proper, manifestly positive definition of the probability. We formulate amplitude-level NRQCD factorization as the systematic prescription that restores positivity, show that every amplitude-level truncated observable admits the effective representation $Γ^{\mathrm{amp}}=Γ_{\mathrm{LO}}\bigl|1+\frac{α_s}π(c_1+ic_2)+\frac{α_s^2}{π^2}(c_3+ic_4)\bigr|^2$, and demonstrate, with the complete NNLO QCD corrections to $J/ψ\to3γ$, $Υ\to3γ$, $e^+e^-\to J/ψ+J/ψ$ and the eight exclusive charmonium production channels at $B$ factories, that the prescription yields positive and better-converging predictions.

</details>


### [32] [Boosted top tagging via lepton-in-jet topology for vector-like quark searches in FCC-hh](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05104)
*Sanchari Bhattacharyya,Biplob Bhattacherjee,Kirana K. K.,Swagata Mukherjee*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a machine-learning framework using both physics-motivated variables (DNN) and constituent-level modeling (Particle Transformer) to tag highly boosted leptonic top quarks (t→bℓν) at the 100 TeV FCC-hh, where decay products are collimated and the lepton is embedded in the jet; it applies the tagger to a B′→tW search, achieving 5σ discovery reach up to ~10 TeV depending on B′ width.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: At FCC-hh (√s = 100 TeV), highly boosted top quarks from t→bℓν decays produce non-isolated leptons embedded in jets, challenging conventional constituent-based tagging; it is unclear whether such taggers retain discrimination power, necessitating dedicated development and benchmarking.

Method: Two complementary ML approaches: (i) a deep neural network (DNN) trained on physics-motivated high-level observables (e.g., substructure, lepton-jet compatibility), and (ii) a Particle Transformer (ParT) operating directly on jet constituents; both optimized for non-isolated lepton signature; integrated into an event-level search for vector-like B′→tW.

Result: Both DNN and ParT taggers achieve comparable performance distinguishing leptonic top jets from hadronic top, b-, and c-jets; the B′ search yields 5σ discovery reach of ~7 TeV for Γ_B′/m_B′ < 25% (under stat-only and 10% syst uncertainty), extending to ~10 TeV when width放宽 to ≲50%.

Conclusion: Constituent-based (ParT) and variable-based (DNN) taggers remain effective for leptonic boosted top identification at FCC-hh energies; the proposed framework enables sensitive B′→tW searches, demonstrating feasibility of discovering heavy vector-like quarks up to ~10 TeV.

Abstract: In this paper, we present a machine-learning-based framework for identifying leptonically decaying, highly boosted top quarks, $t\to b\ellν$, at the Future Circular Collider: hadron-hadron mode (FCC-hh) operating at a center-of-mass energy of $\sqrt{s}=100$~TeV. At these energies, the large Lorentz boost causes the top-quark decay products to become highly collimated, with the lepton embedded within the resulting top-quark jet. In this regime, it is not a priori clear whether constituent-based jet taggers, which rely on information from individual jet constituents, retain the high discrimination power achieved at LHC energies. To answer this question, we employ two complementary approaches: a variable-based tagger trained with a deep neural network (DNN) using physics-motivated observables and a constituent-based Particle Transformer (ParT) acting on the jet constituents. Both approaches are optimised for the characteristic non-isolated lepton signature of boosted leptonic top decays and achieve comparable performance in discriminating leptonic top jets from hadronic top jets and heavy-flavor $b$- and $c$-jet backgrounds. We then incorporate the tagging framework into an event-level search for single production of a vector-like $B^\prime$ quark decaying through $B^\prime\to tW$, and use it to evaluate sensitivity to $B^\prime$ in the range of $3$ to $10$~TeV. For the reference $B^\prime$ benchmark, the proposed search strategy achieves a $5σ$ discovery reach of approximately $7$~TeV under the width requirement $Γ_{B^\prime}/m_{B^\prime}<25\%$, in both the statistics-only and $10\%$ background systematic uncertainty scenarios. Relaxing the width requirement to $Γ_{B^\prime}/m_{B^\prime}\lesssim 50\%$ extends the discovery reach to approximately $10$~TeV.

</details>


### [33] [Mixed-state quantum geometry of dense two-color QCD matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04848)
*Kouji Kashiwa,Riki Oshima,Hiroaki Kouno*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper applies information geometry—specifically the Bures metric and quantum Fisher information (QFI)—to thermal states in a two-flavor NJL model of dense QCD, revealing strong geometric signatures near the color-superconducting phase boundary but weak or absent features at the chiral crossover and BEC–BCS crossover.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To detect and characterize quantum phase transitions and crossovers in dense QCD using information-geometric tools that are sensitive to underlying quantum correlations and state distinguishability, beyond conventional thermodynamic observables.

Method: Constructs thermal (Gibbs) states from mean-field Bogoliubov–de Gennes Hamiltonians in the two-flavor NJL model; computes the Bures metric and quantum Fisher information matrix on the (T, μ) thermodynamic parameter manifold; analyzes trace of QFI and metric-volume density numerically.

Result: Pronounced enhancements of both QFI trace and metric-volume density near the color-superconducting phase boundary; only mild signatures across the chiral crossover; no discernible feature at the BEC–BCS crossover.

Conclusion: Information geometry—particularly QFI and Bures metric—provides a sensitive probe for the color-superconducting transition in dense QCD, distinguishing it from chiral and BEC–BCS crossovers, thereby supporting its utility as a diagnostic tool for strongly correlated quantum phases.

Abstract: We investigate an information-geometric approach to dense two-color QCD using thermal states defined as normalized Gibbs states in the two-flavor Nambu--Jona-Lasinio model at the mean-field level. The Bures metric and quantum Fisher information are constructed from thermal states associated with effective Bogoliubov--de Gennes Hamiltonians reproducing the mean-field quasiparticle spectrum. On the thermodynamic parameter manifold $(T,μ)$, where $T$ is temperature and $μ$ is quark chemical potential, we analyze information-geometric quantities including the trace of the QFI matrix and the metric-volume density. The numerical results show pronounced enhancements of both quantities near the color-superconducting phase boundary, whereas only weaker signatures are observed across the chiral crossover. By contrast, neither observable exhibits a distinct feature associated with the BEC--BCS crossover.

</details>


### [34] [A strategy for early discoveries at LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05889)
*Alain Le Yaouanc,François Richard*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper argues that the 650 GeV excess at LHC could be a Randall–Sundrum Kaluza–Klein graviton resonance—contrary to the common assumption that such states are too heavy for LHC—and proposes revised search strategies (e.g., including h(95)h(125), h(95)h(95), semi-leptonic/hadronic ZZ/WW) to enhance detection and confirm a KK graviton tower up to ~3 TeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reinterpret LHC excesses—especially the 650 GeV bump—as light Kaluza–Klein gravitons in a Randall–Sundrum warped extra-dimension model, challenging the prejudice that such resonances must be >2 TeV, and to address methodological gaps in current searches that assume scalar (not spin-2) resonances.

Method: Critical reanalysis of LHC search strategies; identification of selection biases against spin-2 resonances (e.g., wrong mass window assumptions, neglect of non-resonant backgrounds, exclusion of h(95)h(125)/h(95)h(95) final states); proposal of optimized analyses for ZZ, WW, hh (including mixed-mass Higgs pairs), using both leptonic and hadronic decay channels.

Result: Demonstration that the 650 GeV excess is compatible with the lightest KK graviton; explanation of why standard searches miss it; quantitative expectation that improved analyses—particularly in ZZ, hh, and h(95)h(125) channels—could yield statistically significant evidence (potentially discovery-level) with existing LHC data.

Conclusion: A light RS KK graviton is a viable and testable explanation for the 650 GeV excess; current exclusions below 2 TeV are invalid for this scenario due to inappropriate analysis choices; adopting spin-2–aware selections—including exotic Higgs pair channels and full kinematic reconstruction in semi-leptonic/hadronic ZZ/WW—will enable discovery and reveal a KK graviton tower up to ~3 TeV.

Abstract: Evidence for excesses observed at LHC tend to cluster mostly around 95 GeV and 650 GeV. We claim that a consequent interpretation of the 650 GeV and some additional indications calls for a Randall Sundrum sequence of Kaluza Klein graviton resonances. A common prejudice claims that such resonances should be very heavy, out of reach for LHC. We argue that this is no so. Although present LHC searches seem to exclude these particles below 2 TeV, we explain why this 650 GeV resonance could be one of them. We also explain why ongoing analyses ignore this scenario and apply selections which are only valid for scalar resonances. This could also apply to ongoing searches for Higgs pairs where the search for resonances decaying into SM Higgs pairs ignores the KK graviton scenario. We expect that with a correct treatment, one could very significantly increase the detection efficiency for ZZ and hh channels and achieve, with present data, a convincing proof of our expectations. Channels like h(95)h(125) and h(95)h(95) should be added to present selections. Such improvements should allow to reach a discovery. For the ZZ and WW channels, one can use semi-leptonic and purely hadronic final states, to confirm the tower of KK resonances indicated up to 3 TeV.

</details>


### [35] [Exploring the Supersymmetric Grand Unified Theories with Gauge Mediation at the Future Proton-Proton Colliders and Hyper-Kamiokande Experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04954)
*Waqas Ahmed,Tianjun Li,Shabbar Raza*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how future experiments—Hyper-Kamiokande and a 100 TeV pp collider—can test supersymmetric GUTs with gauge mediation under the grand desert hypothesis, linking proton decay bounds and sparticle mass limits to GUT scale and SUSY-breaking parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test supersymmetric Grand Unified Theories (SUSY GUTs) with gauge mediation in the 'grand desert' scenario, where no new physics exists between TeV and GUT scales, using upcoming high-sensitivity experiments.

Method: Analytical calculation of dimension-six proton decay lifetime via heavy gauge boson exchange; derivation of sparticle mass bounds (gluino, squarks, neutralino/NLSP) from GUT-scale constraints and renormalization group evolution; consistency checks with Hyper-K and FCC-hh/SppC sensitivities.

Result: Proton decay lifetime < ~5×10³⁴ yr for M_GUT ≤ 0.84×10¹⁶ GeV, testable at Hyper-K; for M_GUT ≥ 0.84×10¹⁶ GeV, lightest neutralino becomes NLSP and gluino/squark mass upper bounds are 8.64 TeV and 11.82 TeV—within reach of 100 TeV colliders; full mass spectra for colored sparticles, sleptons, pseudoscalar Higgs, and electroweakinos are presented.

Conclusion: Future experiments can jointly probe SUSY GUTs with gauge mediation: Hyper-K tests low-M_GUT via proton decay, while 100 TeV colliders test high-M_GUT via NLSP-driven sparticle production; discovery would constrain messenger scale, effective SUSY-breaking scale, and RG evolution of soft terms.

Abstract: As a continuation of our previous work, with the grand desert hypothesis, we prove that we can probe the Supersymmetric Grand Unified Theories (GUTs) with gauge meidation at the future proton-proton collider and Hyper-Kamiokande experiment. For the GUT scale $M_{\rm GUT}$ less than or equal to $0.84\times10^{16}$~GeV, the proton lifetime for dimension-six proton decay via heavy gauge boson exchange is less than or around $5\times10^{34}$ years, and thus we can probe it at the Hyper-Kamiokande experiment. For $ M_{\rm GUT} \ge 0.84 \times 10^{16}$ GeV, the lightest neutralino is the Next to the Lightest Supersymmetric Particle (NLSP), as well as the upper mass bounds on the gluino and first two generation squarks are 8.64 TeV and 11.82 TeV, respectively. Therefore, the gluino and first two generation squarks can be probed at the future 100 TeV proton-proton collider such as ${\rm FCC}_{\rm hh}$ and SppC. Moreover, we discuss the mass spectra for the colored sparticles, sleptons, pseudo-scalar Higgs and electroweakinos. If the sparticles were discovered, it might give us hint on the messenger scale, the effective supersymmetry breaking scale, and the renormalization group evolution of the supersymmetry breaking soft terms.

</details>


### [36] [Learning Astrophysical Uncertainties in Dark Matter Direct Detection with Simulation-Based Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06159)
*Felix Kahlhoefer,Niklas Reus*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates neural ratio estimation (NRE), a simulation-based inference method, for WIMP dark matter direct detection using XENONnT data; it shows robustness to astrophysical halo model uncertainties and yields exclusion limits consistent with expected sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a scalable, simulation-based inference alternative to profile likelihood methods for WIMP direct detection, especially as experiments approach the neutrino fog where traditional frequentist approaches become computationally prohibitive and statistically challenging.

Method: Neural ratio estimation (NRE) using a binary classifier trained on simulated parameter-observation pairs (WIMP mass $m_χ$, spin-independent proton coupling $c_p$) generated via the fuse detector simulation of XENONnT; multiple astrophysical halo models—including extreme ±5σ Standard Halo Model variations—are jointly included in training to implicitly marginalize over astrophysical uncertainties.

Result: NRE yields well-calibrated Bayesian posteriors under severe halo model misspecification; posteriors from realistic halo models are nearly indistinguishable; the signal-plus-background classifier produces an exclusion limit consistent—both qualitatively and quantitatively (accounting for frequentist–Bayesian differences)—with XENONnT’s expected sensitivity.

Conclusion: NRE is a viable, scalable, and robust complement to profile likelihood ratio methods for next-generation direct detection experiments operating in the neutrino fog.

Abstract: We present a proof-of-concept study of neural ratio estimation (NRE) - a simulation-based inference algorithm - applied to WIMP direct detection using the XENONnT experiment as an example. A binary classifier is trained on simulated parameter-observation pairs to approximate the likelihood ratio for the WIMP mass $m_χ$ and spin-independent proton coupling $c_p$, using realistic scintillation and ionization signals produced by the fuse detector simulation. We demonstrate that astrophysical uncertainties from halo model variations are marginalized over implicitly and at no additional inference cost by including multiple halo models in the training data. Using extreme $\pm5σ$ Standard Halo Model variations, we show that training on a combined dataset recovers well-calibrated posteriors even under severe misspecification, while more realistic halo models produce nearly indistinguishable results. The signal-plus-background classifier yields an exclusion limit that is both qualitatively and (after accounting for differences between frequentist and Bayesian approaches) quantitatively consistent with the XENONnT expected sensitivity. These results establish NRE as a scalable complement to the profile likelihood ratio approach, with particular advantages as experiments probe deeper into the neutrino fog.

</details>


### [37] [Probing anomalous $Wtb$ couplings through $bW$ spin correlations in top-quark pair production at the HL-LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04971)

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a spin-density-matrix framework to probe anomalous $Wtb$ couplings in $t\bar{t}$ production via $b$-quark and $W$-boson polarizations and their spin correlations, using $\Lambda_b$ semileptonic decay and leptonic $W$ decay. It derives the $bW$ spin density matrix under anomalous couplings, constructs angular observables with linear sensitivity, and demonstrates significantly improved constraints at HL-LHC — e.g., 8× better sensitivity to $V_R$, ~6× to $g_L$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve experimental sensitivity to anomalous $Wtb$ interactions — key probes of physics beyond the Standard Model — by leveraging spin information from both the $b$ quark and $W$ boson in top-quark pair production, which is underutilized in conventional analyses.

Method: Develops a $bW$ spin density matrix formalism incorporating anomalous $Wtb$ couplings ($V_R$, $g_L$, etc.); maps $b$-quark spin via angular distributions in $\Lambda_b \to \Lambda_c \ell \nu$ (after fragmentation) and $W$ polarization via leptonic decay angles; constructs CP-conserving and CP-violating angular observables linear in the anomalous couplings; performs statistical sensitivity projections for HL-LHC.

Result: At HL-LHC (14 TeV, 3000 fb$^{-1}$), the method achieves up to 8× improved constraint on $V_R$ and ~6× on $g_L$ compared to current limits; other anomalous couplings also see enhanced sensitivity due to linear dependence and reduced systematic uncertainties from spin-correlation observables.

Conclusion: The $bW$ spin density matrix framework provides a theoretically robust, experimentally feasible, and highly sensitive probe of anomalous $Wtb$ couplings, outperforming existing methods by fully exploiting spin entanglement and polarization information in $t\bar{t} \to b W^+ \bar{b} W^-$ events.

Abstract: We propose a spin-density-matrix framework for probing anomalous $Wtb$ interactions in top-quark pair production by exploiting the polarizations of the $b$ quark and the $W$ boson and their spin correlations. The $b$-quark spin is analyzed through angular distributions in semileptonic $Λ_b$ decay following fragmentation, while the leptonic $W$ decay serves as a $W$-polarization analyzer. We derive the $bW$ spin density matrix in the presence of anomalous $Wtb$ couplings and show that its polarization and spin-correlation parameters provide linear sensitivity to these couplings. We construct experimentally accessible angular observables associated with these parameters and evaluate their statistical sensitivities. For the HL-LHC at $\sqrt{s}=14~ \mathrm{TeV}$ with an integrated luminosity of $3000~ \mathrm{fb}^{-1}$, the proposed method yields improved constraints on most anomalous $W t b$ couplings, with the sensitivity to $V_R$ enhanced by a factor of 8 and that to $g_L$ by a factor of about 6.

</details>


### [38] [Novel dependence between neutrino mass splittings strongly supported by initial JUNO results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06738)
*I. Alikhanov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reports experimental validation of an empirically predicted relation between neutrino mass-squared differences, Δm²₂₁ and Δm²₃₁, using JUNO’s high-precision data; the measured ratio matches √2 with 0.27% relative precision and <0.03σ deviation, suggesting a hidden symmetry in the neutrino mass spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test a recently proposed empirical relation among neutrino mass-squared differences — motivated by possible underlying symmetries beyond the standard three-flavor oscillation framework.

Method: Analysis of JUNO Collaboration's latest high-precision measurements of Δm²₂₁ and Δm²₃₁ to compute and statistically evaluate the ratio (√Δm²₃₁ + √Δm²₂₁)/(√Δm²₃₁ − √Δm²₂₁).

Result: The experimentally derived ratio is 1.4143₋₀.₀₀₃₈⁺⁰.₀₀₃₆, matching √2 ≈ 1.4142 with 0.27% relative precision and a deviation of <0.03σ.

Conclusion: The excellent agreement supports the existence of a nontrivial constraint among neutrino mass parameters, hinting at an undiscovered symmetry governing the neutrino mass spectrum.

Abstract: In the standard three-flavor paradigm, two of the three neutrino mass-squared differences are independent. The existence of an additional dependence between these oscillation parameters was recently predicted on empirical grounds, taking the form $$\frac{\sqrt{Δm^2_{31}}+\sqrt{Δm^2_{21}}}{\sqrt{Δm^2_{31}}-\sqrt{Δm^2_{21}}}=\sqrt{2}.$$ The JUNO Collaboration has now reported the most precise measurements to date of both $Δm^2_{21}$ and $Δm^2_{31}$. We demonstrate that this relation is in excellent agreement with the experimental data, which yield $1.4143_{-0.0038}^{+0.0036}$ corresponding to a relative precision of $0.27\%$. Remarkably, the central value differs by less than $0.03$ standard deviations from the prediction ($\sqrt{2}\approx1.4142$). This provides strong experimental support for the novel constraint and thereby suggests an underlying symmetry governing the neutrino mass spectrum, rather than purely independent parameters.

</details>


### [39] [The decay $η_c\to p\bar pη$ with meson--baryon final-state interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05209)
*Jing Song,Yu-Shan Ren,Eulogio Oset*

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the role of baryon resonances—particularly the dynamically generated N*(1535) and possible N*(1895)—in the charmonium decay η_c → p̄pη, and to test the chiral unitary framework’s ability to describe final-state interactions in the pη and p̄η subsystems.

Conclusion: The decay η_c → p̄pη provides a clean probe of ηN interactions and S₁₁ baryon resonance structure; results validate the chiral unitary description of N*(1535) and motivate further search for S₁₁ states near 1.9 GeV.

Abstract: We study the decay \(η_c\to p\bar{p}η\) by combining the SU(3) flavor structure of the primary production with final-state interactions in the \(pη\) and \(\bar{p}η\) subsystems, using the chiral unitary approach. The low-energy \(S_{11}\) dynamics, including the dynamically generated \(N^*(1535)\), is incorporated into the complete coupled-channel amplitude, and a phenomenological \(N^*(1895)\) contribution is included coherently. The \(pη\) and \(\bar{p}η\) invariant-mass distributions are well described, and the \(p\bar{p}\) distribution is predicted as a kinematic reflection of the \(ηN\) dynamics. The results support the dynamical interpretation of the \(N^*(1535)\) and suggest possible additional \(S_{11}\) strength near \(1.9\)~GeV.

</details>


### [40] [Exploring Anomaly-Free Dark Photon Models Through Solar Neutrino-Electron Scattering in Ton-Scale Dark Matter Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05377)
*Mehmet Demirci,Gürsel Kazıl,M. Fauzi Mustamin*

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain low-mass dark photon models—particularly anomaly-free U(1)_X extensions with kinetic and mass mixing—using high-statistics, low-energy solar neutrino-electron scattering data, complementing existing dark sector searches.

Method: Analysis of solar neutrino-electron elastic scattering data from PandaX-4T and XENONnT; derivation of exclusion limits in parameter space for six benchmark anomaly-free U(1)_X models; separate treatment of pure kinetic and pure mass mixing for model-independent constraints.

Result: Novel, improved exclusion limits for sub-MeV dark photon mediators; limits shown to scale with theoretical leptonic charge assignments; stronger constraints than CHARM-II, TEXONO, and GEMMA in the relevant mass range.

Conclusion: Solar neutrino-electron scattering provides a powerful probe of low-mass dark photons; results enhance global sensitivity to dark portals and underscore the importance of anomaly cancellation and charge structure in model building.

Abstract: We study the phenomenological implications of anomaly-free dark photon models using low-energy solar neutrino-electron elastic scattering data from the ton-scale PandaX-4T and XENONnT experiments. Focusing on $U(1)_X$ gauge extensions that incorporate both kinetic and mass mixing, we derive novel exclusion limits across the parameter spaces of six benchmark models. Additionally, we provide model-independent limits by isolating the pure kinetic and pure mass mixing scenarios. In the sub-MeV mediator mass regime, our results meaningfully improve upon existing limits from previous neutrino-electron scattering experiments, such as CHARM-II, TEXONO, and GEMMA. Ultimately, these findings yield improved limits on low-mass dark photon models, demonstrating their complementarity with other ventures in the global search for dark portals.

Abstract: We present a likelihood analysis of observables in deeply virtual exclusive $π^o$ electro-production off the proton. We use experimental data for the cross section as well as the beam and target spin asymmetries measured in various longitudinal polarization configurations at Jefferson Lab Hall A and Hall B. Performing a local extraction, for each kinematic bin, we derive a joint likelihood for the various helicity amplitudes defining the different polarization configurations. We subsequently extract the Compton form factors that parameterize the underlying QCD scattering amplitudes in the chiral-odd sector. Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) methods are employed to explore the full parameter space, extracting the parameter uncertainties, correlations and credible intervals. The joint likelihood analysis of the cross-section and asymmetry data constrains the parameter space indicating that a larger kinematic coverage and additional polarization configurations, {\it e.g.} in the transverse sector, will improve the precision of the parameters. We also report a determination of the proton tensor charge and tensor anomalous magnetic moment extracted in a Bayesian statistical framework using deeply virtual exclusive scattering data.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Conclusion: The finite-size quark-diquark framework provides quantitatively reliable benchmark predictions for multiply heavy baryon spectroscopy and weak decays; residual hyperfine discrepancies point to needed refinements in spin-dependent interactions; recoil dynamics play a critical, calculable role—especially in charm decays—and must be included for precision studies.

Abstract: We investigate the mass spectra and two-body nonleptonic weak decays of ground-state doubly and triply heavy baryons in a nonrelativistic quark-diquark model. The resulting Schr{ö}dinger equations provide the baryon masses and wave functions used consistently in the decay calculation. The predicted masses differ from existing lattice QCD results by less than $1.5\%$, and our calculated mass of $Ω_{cc}^{+}$ is consistent with recent LHCb observations. The calculated hyperfine splittings are systematically slightly smaller than the lattice values, indicating a residual uncertainty in the spin-dependent interaction. Within the factorization approximation, we evaluate the $c\to s$ and $b\to c$ transitions with pion or kaon emission. At leading nonrelativistic order, the vector contribution vanishes for the $3/2^+\to1/2^+$ transitions, while both vector and axial contributions enter the $3/2^+\to3/2^+$ channels. The $b\to c$ spatial overlaps are substantially smaller than their $c\to s$ counterparts. Together with the Cabibbo-Kobayashi-Maskawa hierarchy, this reduces the corresponding $b \to c$ width by approximately two orders of magnitude. In the $3/2^+\to1/2^+$ charm transitions, the leading $|\bm{k}|^3$ dependence enhances the suppression of kaon emission. In bottom transitions with a larger energy release, the smaller relative phase-space difference brings the ratios close to the scaling determined by the Cabibbo-Kobayashi-Maskawa elements and meson decay constants. These results provide benchmark estimates for future studies of multiply heavy baryons and quantify the role of recoil dynamics within the finite-size quark-diquark framework.

</details>

TL;DR: This paper investigates current-component independence for pseudoscalar-to-pseudoscalar semileptonic form factors in light-front dynamics using the Bakamjian–Thomas (BT) construction, benchmarking against a solvable Bethe–Salpeter model; it shows that BT-based LF quark models with on-shell spinors and consistent invariant mass projections yield identical $f_-(q^2)$ from $(+,\perp)$ and $(+,-)$ current components, analytically proving independence for rotationally invariant wave functions, and introduces a mass-weighted transverse contour for direct, parametrization-free evaluation of $D\\to K$ form factors across the full timelike range including zero recoil.

Conclusion: The BT construction—when realized with on-shell spinors, consistent invariant mass projections, and rotationally invariant wave functions—ensures current-component independence for $f_-(q^2)$ in pseudoscalar semileptonic transitions; this clarifies the dynamical origin of zero-mode assignments and provides a robust, parametrization-free framework for computing form factors directly from light-front wave functions.

Abstract: We examine the role of the Bakamjian--Thomas (BT) construction in achieving current-component independence for pseudoscalar-to-pseudoscalar semileptonic form factors in light-front (LF) dynamics. An exactly solvable covariant Bethe--Salpeter (BS) model provides an off-shell benchmark. Its transverse-current zero-mode contribution is calculated directly from the shrinking nonvalence region and reproduced by an effective operator in the valence convolution. In the BT-based LF quark model, the one-body current kernel is constructed from on-shell constituent spinors, while interactions are encoded in the mass operator and wave functions. In this realization, the $(+,\perp)$ current-component extraction is zero-mode free and is saturated by the on-shell overlap. Using the free constituent invariant masses consistently in the internal projections yields the same $f_-(q^2)$ from the $(+,\perp)$ and $(+,-)$ current-component extractions after integration. We establish this equality analytically for rotationally invariant radial wave functions with the LF Jacobian and the adopted pseudoscalar spin coupling, provided the reduced overlaps converge. The difference from the minus-current projection using physical meson masses defines an interaction-dependent BT completion, distinct from the BS endpoint contribution. A mass-weighted complex transverse contour permits direct evaluation of the Gaussian $D\to K$ overlaps throughout the physical timelike interval, including zero recoil, without a form-factor parametrization or extrapolation. These results establish current-component independence within the specified BT realization and clarify the dynamical dependence of zero-mode assignments.

</details>


### [44] [Vector Dark Matter from $d\geq 2$ Extra Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05851)

  <summary>Details</summary>

Method: Constructs d ≥ 2 extra-dimensional gravitational models; performs Kaluza-Klein decomposition to identify vector towers; derives field content and interactions; analyzes unitarization via extra-dimensional geometry; computes freeze-in relic abundances for the multi-component KK vector spectrum using Boltzmann equations and gravitational production rates.

Result: Demonstrates that KK vectors constitute viable, natural dark matter with gravitational-only couplings to the SM; shows geometry unitarizes cosmologically relevant processes; obtains viable parameter space for d = 2 benchmark where relic density matches Planck observations via freeze-in, governed predominantly by a single new scale—the compactification radius.

Conclusion: Kaluza-Klein vector towers from ≥2 extra dimensions provide a well-motivated, minimal, and testable dark matter framework rooted in quantum gravity considerations; their gravitational portal nature and multi-component structure lead to distinctive cosmological signatures distinct from WIMPs or axions.

Abstract: In this work, we propose a new natural dark matter candidate: Kaluza-Klein towers of vectors from $d\geq 2$ extra-dimensional models. Their naturalness follows from two key features: (i) they arise unavoidably from the Kaluza-Klein decomposition of the higher-dimensional metric, and (ii) if the Standard Model is localised on a brane, they do not couple directly to it and interact only gravitationally through the other components of the higher-dimensional metric. Remarkably, the rich particle content comes with as few as a single extra parameter. We first explain the general idea for $d\geq 2$, then focus on the $d=2$ case as our benchmark scenario. We derive the field content and interactions among the model particle content, and show how the geometry of the extra dimension unitarises the relevant processes for cosmology. We then compute the multi-component relic abundances of the vectors from freeze-in and discuss the available parameter space.

</details>

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile quasi-Dirac Higgsino dark matter with stringent astrophysical constraints—particularly solar neutrino limits from capture-annihilation in the Sun—while preserving testable signals in direct detection and indirect gamma-ray channels, by leveraging environment-dependent, axion-mediated annihilation.

Method: Analytical and phenomenological calculation of velocity-dependent annihilation cross sections for quasi-Dirac Higgsinos coupled to a subthreshold-mass axion-like particle; comparison across cosmological reannihilation, solar capture-annihilation equilibrium, and Galactic halo environments; evaluation of neutrino, gamma-ray (continuum and line), direct detection (inelastic scattering), and dark radiation signatures; exploration of UV completions including generalized mirage mediation, hidden strong dynamics, and extended Higgs sectors.

Result: Axion-mediated annihilation is enhanced at low velocities (e.g., Sun, halo) due to subthreshold propagator behavior and transition from coannihilating multiplet to lightest neutral state; solar equilibrium allows only a few percent of total DM abundance without violating IceCube neutrino bounds, yet yields observable high-recoil signals in LZ; Galactic halo annihilation produces detectable gamma-ray lines (near current sensitivity for cuspy profiles) and continuum; dark photons contribute to ΔN_eff, offering a cosmological probe.

Conclusion: Quasi-Dirac Higgsinos coupled to a light ALP provide a viable, testable dark matter scenario where environment-dependent annihilation decouples astrophysical constraints from terrestrial and indirect detection prospects—bridging theoretical naturalness with near-future observational reach.

Abstract: We study environment-dependent annihilation of quasi-Dirac Higgsino dark matter coupled to an axion-like particle with a mass slightly below the Higgsino-pair threshold. Axion-mediated annihilation becomes more important relative to electroweak annihilation in the Sun and Galactic halo than during cosmological reannihilation following late nonthermal production. This separation arises from both the low-velocity enhancement of the subthreshold propagator and the change from a coannihilating Higgsino multiplet to the lightest neutral state. In solar capture-annihilation equilibrium, a dominant annihilation channel into massless dark photons suppresses the high-energy neutrino signal while leaving capture efficient. Higgsinos can consequently account for a few percent of the total dark matter abundance while satisfying solar-neutrino constraints and retaining an appreciable terrestrial inelastic-scattering rate, with implications for the LZ high-recoil candidate. In the Galactic halo, the enhanced cross section instead produces correlated continuum and line gamma-ray signals, with the lines potentially close to the present sensitivity for a cuspy Galactic dark-matter profile. We discuss supersymmetric realizations based on generalized mirage mediation and hidden strong dynamics, as well as an extended Higgs-sector alternative. The dark photons also contribute to dark radiation, providing a complementary cosmological probe.

### [46] [Phenomenological study of diquark effects in $\bar{B}_{(s)}^0 \to D_{(s)}^{(*)+} L^-$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05958)

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Deviations between experimental data and Standard Model predictions for branching fractions of B̄_(s)^0 → D_(s)^(*)+ L^- decays (with L = π, K, K*, ρ) motivate exploration of new physics, specifically the type-IV scalar diquark as a potential explanation.

In the two-coupling case, viable parameter space exists if Δ_{d(s)}^{LL} ≠ 0. LHCb’s R_{π/μν_μ} measurement tightens constraints on Δ_d^{LL} but not on Δ_d^{RL}. Left-handed couplings are phenomenologically favored.

Conclusion: A type-IV scalar diquark with dominant purely left-handed couplings Δ_{d(s)}^{LL} provides the most plausible NP explanation for the observed B̄_(s)^0 → D_(s)^(*)+ L^- anomalies; predictions for branching fractions and ratios R_{(s)L}^{(*)} are updated for experimental verification at LHCb and Belle II.

Abstract: Motivated by the deviations between experimental data and SM predictions for the branching fractions of $\bar{B}_{(s)}^{0} \to D_{(s)}^{(*)+}L^-$ decays with $L=π,K^{(*)},ρ$, we investigate whether the type-IV scalar diquark can offer a plausible explanation for them. By matching the renormalized amplitudes in the full theory onto those in the low-energy effective theory formulated in the diquark operator basis, we extract the corresponding Wilson coefficients up to NLO in $α_s$ at the NP scale $μ_φ=M_φ$. We then rewrite the obtained operators in the SM basis and apply the renormalization-group running to evolve the Wilson coefficients down to the hadronic scale $μ_b=m_b$. For the hadronic matrix elements of the relevant NP operators, we employ the QCD factorization approach to evaluate the non-factorizable one-loop vertex corrections, and confront our theoretical predictions to the latest data to constrain the diquark parameter space. In the single-coupling case, we find that the purely left-handed coupling $Δ_{d(s)}^{LL}$ can reconcile the discrepancies at $1σ$, while the mixed right-left coupling $Δ_{d(s)}^{RL}$ remains compatible with data only at about $2σ$. Extending to the two-coupling case, we find viable parameter regions as long as $Δ_{d(s)}^{LL}\neq 0$. Incorporating the LHCb measurement of the ratio $R_{π/μν_μ}$ further reduces the allowed range of $Δ_{d}^{LL}$, but does not yield meaningful extra restriction on $Δ_{d}^{RL}$. Hence, while either $Δ_{d(s)}^{LL}$ or $Δ_{d(s)}^{RL}$ can separately provide an acceptable NP interpretation, our phenomenological results favor a scenario dominated by purely left-handed diquark-quark couplings. Finally, we present updated predictions for the branching fractions and the ratios $R_{(s)L}^{(*)}$, which can be probed by forthcoming LHCb and Belle II measurements.

</details>


### [47] [Hidden-heavy Pentaquarks in the Born-Oppenheimer Approximation for QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06009)

  <summary>Details</summary>

Conclusion: The diabatic Born-Oppenheimer potential framework provides a coherent, QCD-motivated explanation for the spectrum and quantum numbers of hidden-heavy pentaquarks, yielding distinctive and testable predictions—especially the $\frac{5}{2}^-$ assignment for $P_{c\bar{c}}(4457)^+$ and the quartet structure—that differentiate it sharply from conventional molecular models.

Abstract: Hidden-heavy pentaquarks can be identified as bound states in diabatic Born-Oppenheimer potentials that are repulsive color-Coulomb potentials at short distance, cross below a heavy-hadron-pair threshold, and then approach that threshold at long distance.
