We predict that their $J^P$ quantum numbers are $\frac{1}{2}^-$, $\frac{3}{2}^-$, $\frac{3}{2}^-$, and $\frac{5}{2}^-$, respectively. Our prediction that there are only four hidden-charm pentaquarks differs qualitatively from that of simple molecular models that there should be seven. We also present model-independent predictions for pentaquark decays into charmonium plus a light baryon and into a charm baryon and a charm meson.

</details>


### [48] [A non-invertible Fibonacci selection rule for two-Higgs-doublet models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06014)

  <summary>Details</summary>

Motivated by string compactifications, we instead impose a non-invertible Fibonacci selection rule at a matching scale $Λ$ for that purpose. This rule also forbids the independent mass parameter that normally allows the additional Higgs particles to become arbitrarily heavy, while renormalization-group evolution regenerates it below $Λ$. An allowed $\mathbb{Z}_2$-breaking quartic interaction simultaneously avoids stable cosmological domain walls. In the exact alignment limit, requiring perturbative unitarity and boundedness from below up to the matching scale bounds the masses of the three non-SM Higgs states; for $Λ=10^{19}\,\mathrm{GeV}$, they lie below about $150\,\mathrm{GeV}$. The scanned parameter space predicts a suppressed Higgs diphoton signal strength within the projected reach of the HL-LHC. This illustrates how a stringy non-invertible selection rule can leave testable signatures in Higgs physics.

</details>


### [49] [NNLO $N$-jettiness soft function for processes with massless and massive partons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06040)

The cancellation of all divergencies is demonstrated analytically. The finite remainder of the soft function is calculated numerically without an infrared regulator. The calculation employs the recently proposed method that uses the so-called inclusive $N$-jettiness variable to simplify the computation of the non-iterative contribution to the soft function.

</details>


### [50] [Tomography of light hadron structures: From LCDAs to iTMDs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06079)
*Shan Cheng,Jian Chai,Ji-Xin Yu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces intrinsic transverse momentum distribution functions (iTMDs) to improve k_T factorization for exclusive QCD processes, enabling better description of soft transverse dynamics; applied to pion and proton electromagnetic form factors, the iTMD-enhanced pQCD framework agrees well with data and lattice results, and reveals the essential role of higher-twist (especially subleading-twist) contributions in intermediate-Q² scaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the limitation of standard k_T factorization in exclusive QCD processes — namely, its neglect of soft transverse dynamics — by incorporating intrinsic transverse momentum effects beyond leading twist.

Abstract: To account for the soft transverse dynamics overlooked by the $k_T$ factorization formalism of exclusive QCD processes, we propose introducing the intrinsic transversal momentum distribution functions (iTMDs), in conjunction with the light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs), to define the probability amplitude for finding a hadron state where partons move fast along the longitudinal axis while undergoing gentle oscillations in the transverse plane. We implement this framework in the calculation of the electromagnetic form factors of the pion and the proton. Our iTMDs-improved perturbative QCD predictions show good agreement with available experimental data and lattice QCD results. More importantly, we find that higher-twist, especially subleading-twist contributions, are indispensable for explaining the scaling behavior of both pion and proton form factors at intermediate momentum transfers. Furthermore, the inclusion of iTMD effects significantly improves the perturbative QCD predictions for the pion form factor in the few GeV$^2$.

</details>


### [51] [Revisiting neutrino-assisted self-interacting dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06151)
*Luqi Wang,Shu-Yuan Guo,Xuewen Liu,Bin Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines whether neutrino-assisted dark matter self-scattering can explain astrophysical small-scale structure anomalies in two scalar-mediator models (t-channel and s-channel); it finds that, under thermal relic assumptions and current observational constraints, the neutrino force is insufficient for astrophysically relevant self-interactions in both cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether neutrino-mediated forces can generate sufficient dark matter self-scattering to resolve small-scale structure problems (e.g., core-cusp, too-big-to-fail) while remaining consistent with relic abundance and experimental/cosmological bounds.

Method: Combined mediator-resolved Yukawa-type potentials, numerical quantum scattering calculations (including Sommerfeld enhancement), thermal relic density computation (including kinetic decoupling effects), and application of laboratory (e.g., direct detection, beam-dump) and cosmological (CMB, BBN, LSS) constraints.

Result: In the t-channel model, neutrino-force contributions to self-scattering are suppressed by existing bounds at relic-abundance points; self-interactions are tree-level dominated and insufficiently enhanced. Sommerfeld and early kinetic decoupling alter Ωh² by ≤50% only for light mediators. In the s-channel model, freeze-out fixes couplings, yielding self-scattering cross sections >11 orders of magnitude below the SIDM benchmark (~1 cm²/g). An analytic upper bound confirms this shortfall.

Conclusion: Neutrino-assisted self-scattering cannot account for astrophysically relevant dark matter self-interactions in either t- or s-channel scalar-mediator realizations, given standard thermal relic production and current observational constraints.

Abstract: We assess neutrino-assisted dark matter self-scattering in two scalar-mediator realizations by combining mediator-resolved potentials, numerical scattering calculations, thermal relic production, and the adopted observational constraints. In the $t$-channel case, the adopted laboratory and cosmological bounds suppress the neutrino force contribution at points reproducing the relic abundance and yielding sizable self-interactions, which are dominated by tree-level mediator exchange. Sommerfeld enhancement and early kinetic decoupling can change the relic abundance by up to 50\% in the light mediator regime. In the $s$-channel case, thermal freeze-out fixes the coupling, leaving self-scattering well below the order of magnitude required for explanation of small-scale structure of the universe. An analytic upper envelope explains this shortfall of more than eleven orders of magnitude relative to the self-interacting dark matter benchmark. These results establish that, under the adopted constraints and thermal relic assumptions, the neutrino force cannot account for astrophysically relevant dark matter self-scattering in either realization.

</details>


### [52] [Functional renormalization group study of the quark-meson model beyond the local potential approximation: Kurganov--Tadmor versus the grid method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06162)
*Zsolt Szép,György Wolf*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper solves the functional renormalization group (FRG) flow of the two-flavor quark-meson model at finite temperature and chemical potential using Litim's regulator, comparing the high-resolution Kurganov–Tadmor central scheme against the standard grid method in LPA and LPA′ truncations; it finds agreement between methods at fine resolution, highlights challenges from wave function renormalization at finite density related to expansion-point choice, and confirms persistence of low-temperature backbending in the first-order phase boundary even in LPA′.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess numerical reliability and physical robustness of FRG calculations for the quark-meson model at finite density and low temperature—particularly where sharp Fermi-surface features challenge numerical stability—and to clarify the impact of including momentum-dependent wave function renormalizations (LPA′) on phase structure, especially the debated low-T backbending of the chiral first-order transition line.

Method: Solves the FRG flow equation for the two-flavor quark-meson model using Litim’s regulator; employs two numerical schemes: (i) the high-resolution Kurganov–Tadmor central scheme (as benchmark), and (ii) the standard grid method; performs calculations in both local potential approximation (LPA) and LPA′ (including running Z-factors for σ, π, and quark fields); examines dependence on expansion point (fixed vs. running) for field parametrization and flow evolution.

Result: Grid method converges to Kurganov–Tadmor results at sufficient resolution in both LPA and LPA′; inclusion of wave function renormalizations introduces numerical and conceptual difficulties tied to expansion-point choice, especially at finite μ; the low-temperature backbending of the first-order chiral phase boundary—previously seen in LPA—persists in LPA′ regardless of whether the expansion point is fixed or running.

Conclusion: The backbending feature is robust beyond LPA and not an artifact of truncation-level insufficiency; however, consistent treatment of wave function renormalizations at finite density requires careful handling of field parametrization, suggesting that current LPA′ implementations may still lack full momentum and scale dependence needed for quantitative precision near the critical endpoint.

Abstract: We solve the functional renormalization group flow of the two-flavor quark-meson model at finite temperature and quark chemical potential with Litim's regulator, using the Kurganov--Tadmor central scheme and the standard grid method, both in the local potential approximation (LPA) and in an LPA$'$ truncation with running wave function renormalization factors for the pion, sigma, and quark fields. Using the Kurganov--Tadmor scheme as a high-resolution benchmark down to very low temperatures, where the Fermi surface produces the sharpest fronts in the field derivative of the potential, we find that, with sufficiently fine resolution, the grid method gives virtually identical results in both truncations. We discuss the difficulties that the wave function renormalization factors bring into the parametrization of the model and into the flow at finite density, related to the choice of the expansion point. Contrary to some expectations, the peculiar backbending of the first-order phase boundary at low temperature, known from LPA, persists in our implementation of the LPA$'$ truncation, both with a fixed and with a running expansion point.

</details>


### [53] [Polarised cross sections for loop-induced processes with SHERPA+RECOLA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06698)
*Mareen Hoppe,Marek Schönherr,Frank Siegert*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends SHERPA’s polarisation framework to include polarised loop-induced processes at LO and LO+PS, enabling more accurate vector boson polarisation measurements for LHC/HL-LHC precision tests of the electroweak sector.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current parton-shower Monte Carlo simulations lack polarised templates for loop-induced processes, causing significant modelling uncertainties that limit the LHC's sensitivity to vector boson polarisation—especially critical for HL-LHC electroweak symmetry-breaking and gauge-sector tests.

Method: Extends SHERPA with polarised amplitudes from RECOLA; implements fixed-order LO and LO+parton-shower matched simulations for loop-induced processes; systematically studies polarisation definitions and QCD/QED resummation effects.

Result: First implementation of polarised loop-induced processes in a parton-shower generator; quantitative assessment of polarisation effects in inclusive and jet-associated loop-induced channels (e.g., gg→VV, Vγ); identification of key sensitivities to polarisation definitions and resummation.

Conclusion: The extension significantly improves theoretical modelling accuracy for vector boson polarisation observables, reducing dominant systematic uncertainties and enhancing the LHC’s capability to probe BSM physics via electroweak gauge couplings.

Abstract: Studying vector boson polarisation has become an integral part of the LHC physics programme in recent years, as it provides an independent test of the electroweak symmetry-breaking mechanism and the gauge sector of the electroweak Standard Model. These measurements require polarised templates from parton-shower Monte Carlo simulations. Their accuracy does not yet meet the precision requirements of upcoming measurements, leading to significant modelling uncertainties in experimental analyses that will constrain the LHC's polarisation sensitivity at latest in the HL-LHC phase. Polarised loop-induced processes have so far not been included in most parton-shower event generators, despite contributing significantly to processes of interest. In this work, we present the extension of the polarisation framework in the Monte Carlo event generator SHERPA to polarised loop-induced processes at fixed LO and LO matched to parton shower. In addition to a description of the methodology based on polarised amplitudes from the RECOLA matrix element generator, we discuss polarisation effects in relevant inclusive, loop-induced processes and their jet-associated counterparts, several of them for the first time. Both the influence of different polarisation definitions and of QCD and QED resummation effects are investigated.

</details>


### [54] [One Event, Two Frontiers: From LZ to LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06756)
*Md Sadique Anwar,Nivedita Ghosh,Sahabub Jahedi,Tarak Nath Maity*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines whether the 248 keV nuclear-recoil event observed by LZ could be due to dark matter elastic scattering, identifies compatible DM parameter space, and shows that LHC monojet constraints—especially for momentum-dependent EFTs like ℒ₁₀—exclude most sub-TeV DM interpretations, limiting future direct detection sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the 248 keV nuclear-recoil event in LZ can be interpreted as dark matter–nucleon elastic scattering, and to test its viability against collider constraints—particularly for momentum-dependent effective operators not previously constrained at LHC.

Method: Systematic analysis of effective Lagrangians for DM–nucleon elastic scattering; calculation of local significance (≥3σ) for LZ; recasting of ATLAS monojet + MET search (13 TeV, 139 fb⁻¹) to derive LHC exclusion limits; validity assessment within EFT regime.

Result: LHC constraints exclude the DM interpretation of the LZ event for DM masses ≲ TeV for most effective interactions; first LHC limits derived for momentum-dependent Lagrangians including ℒ₁₀; many sub-TeV parameter regions are inaccessible even to future direct detection experiments.

Conclusion: The LZ 248 keV event is highly disfavored as a dark matter signal under standard EFT assumptions due to stringent LHC constraints, especially for momentum-dependent couplings; results imply fundamental limitations on probing such DM scenarios via direct detection alone.

Abstract: The 248 keV nuclear-recoil event observed by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment could potentially arise from dark matter (DM) interactions with nucleons. We investigate elastic scattering interpretation, focusing on Lagrangians that yield a local significance of $3σ$ or higher for some DM mass. We identify DM parameter space compatible with the LZ event and assess its consistency with collider constraints by recasting the ATLAS monojet plus missing-transverse-momentum search at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV, based on $139~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ of data. For most of the effective interactions, we find that LHC constraints exclude the DM interpretation of the LZ event for DM masses below $\lesssim\,$TeV subject to the effective field theory (EFT) validity. In particular, we derive, for the first time, LHC constraints on several explicit momentum-dependent Lagrangians, including $\mathcal{L}_{10}$. For many interactions, these constraints are sufficiently strong that the corresponding parameter space below DM mass $\lesssim\,$TeV may be challenging to probe even with future direct detection experiments. Our results, therefore, have broader implications beyond the interpretation of the LZ event itself.

</details>

### [55] [Time-dependent integrable quantum field theories with dynamical boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03870)
*Pradip Kattel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs time-dependent integrable chiral quantum field theories on a finite interval using scattering and reflection data from autonomous integrable models; spectral labels evolve affinely in time, linking bulk and boundary dynamics via boundary quantum Knizhnik–Zamolodchikov (BqKZ) equations; the local Hamiltonian is reconstructed from factorized S- and R-matrices, and the wavefunction is obtained via characteristic pullback of the BqKZ solution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically generate time-dependent integrable chiral QFTs on bounded domains by leveraging the factorized scattering structure of autonomous models, while ensuring consistency between bulk dynamics and boundary reflections through geometric constraints on spectral coordinates.

Method: Imposes affine dependence of spectral labels on characteristic coordinates to enforce position-independent scattering at fixed time; derives boundary quantum Knizhnik–Zamolodchikov (BqKZ) equations from round-trip consistency of spectral translations; reconstructs the local time-dependent Hamiltonian by solving one- and two-particle matching conditions using the bulk S-matrix and boundary reflection matrix; obtains the many-body wavefunction via characteristic pullback of the BqKZ solution.

Result: A unified framework where the same autonomous factorized data (S- and R-matrices) fully determine both the boundary qKZ transport problem and the local driven Hamiltonian; explicit constructions for rational SU(N) and O(3)-invariant models with dynamical boundary impurities, yielding driven Kondo-type interactions with couplings governed by a common affine spectral coordinate.

Conclusion: Time-dependent integrability on finite intervals arises naturally from affine spectral parametrization of scattering and reflection data; BqKZ flatness encodes global consistency, while Yang–Baxter and reflection equations guarantee local compatibility; the construction bridges integrable boundary QFT, quantum transport, and driven many-body dynamics.

Abstract: We present a method for constructing time-dependent integrable chiral quantum field theories on a finite spatial interval from the scattering and reflection data of an autonomous integrable model. Each particle carries a spectral label that is constant along its free trajectory. Requiring a right--left scattering event at a fixed time to be independent of its position forces the map from characteristic coordinates to spectral labels to be affine, with the same slope for both chiralities. As a result, all bulk scattering events at a given time sample one common spectral argument, and the two boundary reflections sample the corresponding endpoint arguments. The bulk and boundary drives are not independent time-dependent deformations: their time dependence is fixed by the same affine spectral coordinate. Successive reflections at the two endpoints translate the spectral label by a finite amount. Following a particle through a complete round trip then produces a difference transport whose consistency gives the boundary quantum Knizhnik--Zamolodchikov equations. The Yang--Baxter and reflection equations ensure local compatibility, while BqKZ flatness ensures compatibility of complete round trips. We then reconstruct the local Hamiltonian by inverting the one- and two-particle matching problems. The bulk matrix fixes the contact interaction, while the reflection matrix fixes the corresponding boundary coupling. The same autonomous factorized data determine the boundary qKZ transport problem and the local driven Hamiltonian. Once the boundary qKZ system is solved, its characteristic pullback gives the corresponding time-dependent many-body wavefunction. We illustrate the construction with rational $SU(N)$ models and an $O(3)$-invariant model with dynamical boundary impurities, obtaining driven Kondo interactions whose couplings are fixed by the same affine spectral coordinate.

</details>


### [56] [Quenched Growth of Normal Matrices and Harmonic Measure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03912)
*Oleg Alekseev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the conditional distribution of additional eigenvalues of large normal random matrices given a fixed 'frozen' set, proving convergence to harmonic measure on the boundary of the spectral droplet under specific scaling regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how new eigenvalues distribute conditionally on a frozen configuration in large normal random matrices, and to relate this to harmonic measure and orthogonal polynomial ensembles.

Method: Analyzes the conditional law of M additional eigenvalues given N frozen eigenvalues using projection onto vanishing polynomials; employs asymptotic analysis of polynomial ensembles, harmonic measure theory, and orthogonal polynomial techniques for regular analytic spectral droplets.

Result: Proves that the spatial distribution of the M new eigenvalues converges to the harmonic measure on the boundary of the original spectral droplet when M/log N → ∞ and M/N → 0; establishes an exact finite-rank identity linking polynomial moments of the conditional mean to fluctuations of the frozen spectrum.

Conclusion: The conditional eigenvalue distribution in the described scaling limit is universally governed by harmonic measure, independent of microscopic details of the frozen configuration (with high probability), and connects macroscopic electrostatics with orthogonal polynomial asymptotics.

Abstract: The eigenvalues of a large normal random matrix form a two-dimensional Coulomb gas. At fixed weight, the spectral droplet grows with the matrix rank, and its semiclassical density increment is harmonic measure. We freeze an $N$-eigenvalue configuration and sample $M$ further eigenvalues using the projection onto polynomials of degree less than $N+M$ that vanish at the frozen points. Their conditional law is an exact polynomial ensemble in the potential of the frozen charges. For a regular analytic droplet, we prove that the spatial distribution of the new eigenvalues converges to harmonic measure on the old boundary when $M/\log N\to\infty$ and $M/N\to0$. This convergence holds conditionally on the frozen spectrum, outside a set of frozen configurations whose probability tends to zero. The equilibrium process formed from orthogonal polynomials of degrees $N,\ldots,N+M-1$ has the same limit. An exact finite-rank identity relates polynomial moments of the conditional mean to fluctuations of the frozen spectrum, with a remainder from polynomial modes of degree $N+M$ and above.

</details>


### [57] [Branching Gaps, Coexistence, and Boundary Influence for the Ising Model on Spherically Symmetric Trees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03941)
*Farrukh Mukhamedov,Otabek Khakimov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes Gibbs uniqueness and phase coexistence for the ferromagnetic Ising model on spherically symmetric rooted trees with variable branching (1 or q children per level), linking structural properties—especially patterns of nonbranching (one-child) levels—to thermodynamic behavior under nonzero external field and finite temperature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how tree geometry—specifically the distribution and length of nonbranching (one-child) levels—affects Gibbs uniqueness, phase coexistence, and boundary influence decay in the Ising model, beyond classical regular or periodic tree settings.

Method: Analytical study using recursive distributional equations, fixed-point analysis of tree-level transfer operators, Schwarzian derivative criteria for monotonicity and bifurcation, and explicit construction of trees with prescribed gap sequences; comparison with periodic trees via uniform domination arguments.

Result: Unbounded runs of one-child levels imply uniqueness at all nonzero fields and finite β; sufficiently long nonbranching stretches control uniqueness thresholds; for density-one trees with vanishing gap sequences, boundary influence can decay arbitrarily slowly; bounded eventual gaps guarantee coexistence in an explicit field interval; eventually periodic gaps reduce uniqueness to fixed-point uniqueness of the full-period return map, with a complete classification via negative Schwarzian derivative revealing three fixed points (two nondegenerate folds at coexistence endpoints, one outside).

Conclusion: Tree geometry—encoded in the sequence of branching levels—directly governs the existence and structure of the nonzero-field coexistence region; boundedness (not periodicity) of gaps is the essential condition for finite-temperature, nonzero-field phase coexistence; the critical behavior at coexistence endpoints is universally governed by square-root bifurcations due to fold singularities.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the ferromagnetic nearest-neighbor Ising model on rooted spherically symmetric trees with one or \(q\) children per vertex, according to a prescribed sequence of branching levels. Unbounded runs of one-child levels force uniqueness at every nonzero homogeneous field and every finite inverse temperature. We quantify this effect through the locations of sufficiently long nonbranching stretches. Conversely, uniqueness need not entail any uniform rate: for each prescribed vanishing sequence, we construct a density-one tree whose finite-volume boundary influence decays more slowly along a subsequence. The tree retains branching number \(q\), although its nonzero-field coexistence region disappears. For eventually bounded gaps, we prove an optimal uniform comparison with the periodic tree having the largest allowed gap and obtain an explicit interval of coexistence fields. Thus bounded gaps are equivalent, within this family, to coexistence at some finite temperature and nonzero field. For eventually periodic gaps, Gibbs uniqueness is equivalent to fixed-point uniqueness of the full-period return map. A negative Schwarzian derivative yields a complete classification: in the supercritical regime the map has three fixed points inside a closed coexistence interval, two at its endpoints, and one outside. The nonzero endpoints are automatically nondegenerate folds, with square-root splitting of the merging branches.

</details>


### [58] [A Self-Generated Local Structural Prior for Resolving Target Merging in Electrical Impedance Tomography (EIT)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04394)
*Junkang Liu,Yizhuang Song*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a self-generated local structural prior to improve spatial separability in Electrical Impedance Tomography (EIT) reconstruction, preventing merging of neighboring conductivity targets by leveraging eigenvector-based structural information from an initial reconstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional EIT suffers from ill-posedness and nonuniform spatial sensitivity, causing adjacent conductivity targets to merge into a single region—degrading spatial separability and diagnostic utility.

Method: The method comprises: (1) obtaining an initial EIT reconstruction; (2) building a Gaussian similarity matrix based on pixel intensity similarity in that image; (3) computing the covariance matrix and selecting an adaptive eigenvector to capture dominant structural features; (4) constructing a localized structural mask from this eigenvector and integrating it as a prior into a refined EIT reconstruction.

Result: Numerical simulations and water tank experiments show clear separation of previously merged neighboring conductivity targets, outperforming conventional EIT methods in preserving spatial distinctness.

Conclusion: The self-generated local structural prior effectively enhances target separability in EIT without requiring external anatomical or functional priors, offering a practical, data-driven improvement to reconstruction fidelity.

Abstract: Electrical impedance tomography (EIT) is an ill-posed inverse imaging technique with limited and spatially nonuniform resolution. Conductivity targets located in regions with degraded spatial sensitivity may be reconstructed as a single connected region, resulting in the loss of spatial separability between neighboring structures. In this work, we propose a self-generated local structural prior for suppressing target merging in EIT reconstruction. First, an initial conductivity image is obtained from the measured boundary data using an existing EIT reconstruction method. This reconstruction is subsequently used as a reference image to construct a Gaussian similarity matrix according to the intensity similarity between image pixels. The adaptively chosen eigenvector of the covariance matrix is then employed to extract the principal structural information of the reference image. A localized structural mask is constructed and incorporated into a subsequent EIT reconstruction to preserve the spatial separability of neighboring targets. Numerical and water tank experimental results demonstrate that the proposed method effectively separates neighboring conductivity targets that are merged by conventional EIT reconstruction methods.

</details>


### [59] [Supersymmetric semi--infinite cohomology and $N = 2$ superconformal structures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04434)
*Thomas Creutzig,Uhi Rinn Suh,Sangwon Yoon*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs supersymmetric semi-infinite cohomology with $N=2$ superconformal structure via BRST reduction of an $N=\frac{3}{2}$ superconformal structure on a super-affine vertex algebra linked to a super Manin pair satisfying the Kazama–Suzuki condition; it also realizes super coset models using relative supersymmetric semi-infinite cohomology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous algebraic framework for $N=2$ superconformal symmetry in supersymmetric sigma models and coset constructions, particularly by extending semi-infinite cohomology to the supersymmetric setting and connecting it to geometric constraints (Kazama–Suzuki condition) and BRST quantization.

Method: BRST reduction applied to an $N=\frac{3}{2}$ superconformal structure on a super-affine vertex algebra associated to a super Manin pair; use of relative supersymmetric semi-infinite cohomology to realize super coset models.

Result: Construction of supersymmetric semi-infinite cohomology endowed with an $N=2$ superconformal vertex algebra structure; realization of super coset models as relative cohomology modules compatible with this structure.

Conclusion: The $N=2$ superconformal structure emerges naturally from BRST reduction of an $N=\frac{3}{2}$ structure on a super-affine vertex algebra tied to a super Manin pair obeying the Kazama–Suzuki condition; relative supersymmetric semi-infinite cohomology provides a unified algebraic description of super coset models.

Abstract: We study supersymmetric semi-infinite cohomology and its $N = 2$ superconformal structure via the BRST reduction of an $N = {\frac{3}{2}}$ superconformal structure on the super-affine vertex algebra associated to a super Manin pair arising from the Kazama--Suzuki $N = 2$ condition. Moreover, we realize super coset models in terms of relative supersymmetric semi-infinite cohomology.

</details>


### [60] [Covariant Euler-Poincaré Reduction for Field Theories on Centered Semidirect Products](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04628)
*Miguel Ángel Berbel,Leonardo Colombo,Álvaro Rodríguez Abella*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a geometric reduction framework for covariant field theories on principal bundles with structure group given by a centered semidirect product $G \Join V$, yielding decomposed Euler-Poincaré and Lie-Poisson field equations, multisymplectic reduction, and balance laws; it distinguishes left/right actions via centered variables (heart/diamond operators), and applies the theory to matrix microstructure models and jet-group field theories, including exact solutions and numerical validation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend geometric field-theoretic reduction beyond standard semidirect products by incorporating commuting left and right linear representations—capturing two-sided symmetry data—and to resolve ambiguities in physical modeling (e.g., nematodynamics) through a centered, decomposition-aware formalism.

Method: Hamiltonian and Lagrangian reduction of covariant field theories on principal $G\Join V$-bundles using multisymplectic geometry; employs centered variables, heart ($\heartsuit$) and diamond ($\diamond$) operators to separate left/right contributions; derives reduced field equations, multisymplectic form formula, and Noether/energy-momentum balance laws; applies to matrix representation and jet-group examples with analytical and numerical verification.

Result: Reduced Euler-Poincaré and Lie-Poisson field equations split into $\mathfrak{g}$- and $V$-components; explicit reduced multisymplectic form; generalized Noether and energy-momentum balance laws; decomposition of $\operatorname{Mat}(3)$ under conjugation clarifies director/alignment-tensor relation without collapsing to nematodynamics; for $G^2_1$ on $(1+1)$-Minkowski space, obtains semilinear symmetric-hyperbolic system with exact traveling conversion front and conserved positive energy for linearized perturbations; numerical reconstruction confirms consistency via mesh refinement and wave-map comparison.

Conclusion: The centered semidirect product framework provides a canonical geometric setting for two-sided symmetry in field theories, cleanly separating left/right dynamics and enabling physically faithful reductions—especially in microstructural and jet-based models—where standard semidirect products obscure structural distinctions.

Abstract: Let $G$ be a Lie group equipped with commuting left and right linear representations on a vector space $V$, and let $G\Join V$ be the associated centered semidirect product. We develop a reduction scheme for covariant field theories on principal bundles with structure group $G\Join V$ from both Lagrangian and Hamiltonian perspectives, obtaining the reduced Euler-Poincaré and Lie-Poisson field equations decomposed into their $\mathfrak g$- and $V$-components, where $\mathfrak g$ is the Lie algebra of $G$. We also derive the reduced multisymplectic form formula, as well as the Noether and energy-momentum balance laws. Our reduction results are compared with those for principal bundles with standard semidirect-product structure groups, and the role of the centered variables is investigated: they retain the original two-sided geometric data and separate the left and right contributions via the heart and diamond operators. The framework is then applied to a two-sided matrix microstructural model and to the second-order jet group on $\mathbb R^r$, $G_r^2$. For the matrix model, the conjugation representation decomposes $\operatorname{Mat}(3)$ into scalar, vector, and symmetric-traceless sectors, clarifying its precise relation to director and alignment-tensor variables without identifying it with standard nematodynamics. For the one-dimensional jet group on a $(1+1)$-dimensional Minkowski base, we obtain a semilinear symmetric-hyperbolic system and an exact traveling conversion front with explicit energy-momentum exchange between the linear- and quadratic-jet sectors. Its hyperbolic-target interpretation yields a conserved positive energy for the linearized perturbations. A nonlinear numerical experiment illustrates the reduced evolution and reconstruction of the group-valued field, with consistency assessed through mesh refinement and an independent wave-map computation.

</details>


### [61] [Dynamical quasi-symmetry of the classical Kepler-Coulomb problem and the conservation of the Laplace-Runge-Lenz vector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04650)
*Radosław Szmytkowski*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies a new dynamical quasi-symmetry for the Kepler–Coulomb problem in ℝ^N (N ≥ 2) via a projective radius transformation and time reparametrization, mapping Newton’s equation to a modified Coulomb-like equation with a sign factor; it establishes a novel link between energy conservation in the transformed system and Laplace–Runge–Lenz vector conservation in the original system, complementing the known SO(N+1)/SO(N,1) dynamical symmetries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover new dynamical structures beyond the well-known SO(N+1) and SO(N,1) symmetries of the Kepler–Coulomb problem, specifically seeking transformations that preserve the form of motion up to a controlled modification—i.e., quasi-symmetries—and to explore their geometric and conserved-quantity implications.

Method: Applies an extended-configuration-space transformation: projective spatial map r → R = r/(1 + c·r) combined with differential time scaling dt → dT = dt/(1 + c·r)², where c is a constant inverse-length vector; analytically derives the transformed equation of motion, analyzes trajectory mappings (conics and hodographs), and proves equivalence between energy conservation in the transformed system and Laplace–Runge–Lenz vector conservation in the original system.

Result: The transformation yields a dynamical quasi-symmetry: the transformed dynamics obey a Coulomb-type equation with a sign factor sgn(1 − c·R); non-degenerate conic sections map bijectively under this transformation; velocity hodographs are correspondingly mapped; crucially, energy conservation in the (R,T)-system is shown to be equivalent to conservation of the Laplace–Runge–Lenz vector in the original (r,t)-system.

Conclusion: A previously unrecognized dynamical quasi-symmetry exists for the Kepler–Coulomb system, distinct from but complementary to its standard Lie-group dynamical symmetries; this symmetry encodes the Laplace–Runge–Lenz conservation law geometrically through energy conservation in a reparameterized space-time, offering a deeper structural understanding of hidden symmetries in classical central-force problems.

Abstract: The paper studies a classical non-relativistic particle moving in $\mathbb{R}^{N}$, $(N\geqslant2)$, in the presence of the Kepler-Coulomb force $\boldsymbol{F}(\boldsymbol{r})=α\boldsymbol{r}/r^{3}$, $(α\neq0)$. It is shown that the extended-configuration-space transformation involving the projective transformation of the radius vector $\boldsymbol{r}\to\boldsymbol{R}=\boldsymbol{r}/(1+\boldsymbol{c}\cdot\boldsymbol{r})$ and the differential time scaling $\mathrm{d}t\to\mathrm{d}T=\mathrm{d}t/(1+\boldsymbol{c}\cdot\boldsymbol{r})^{2}$, where $\boldsymbol{c}$ is a real vector-valued parameter with dimensions of inverse length, constitutes a dynamical quasi-symmetry in the sense that it maps Newton's equation of motion $m(\mathrm{d}^{2}\boldsymbol{r}/\mathrm{d}t^{2})=α\boldsymbol{r}/r^{3}$ into the equation $m(\mathrm{d}^{2}\boldsymbol{R}/\mathrm{d}T^{2})=(α\boldsymbol{R}/R^{3}) \operatorname{sgn}(1-\boldsymbol{c}\cdot\boldsymbol{R})$. This quasi-symmetry is independent of, yet complementary to, the well-known Kepler-Coulomb dynamical symmetry, which is SO($N+1$) for negative energies and SO($N,1$) for positive energies. A detailed analytical and graphical analysis of induced mappings between non-degenerate conic trajectories associated with the original and transformed systems is presented. Mappings between velocity hodographs are also briefly discussed. It is then proved that the conservation of energy in the transformed motion implies the conservation of the Laplace-Runge-Lenz vector in the original dynamics.

</details>


### [62] [Alternative Hamiltonian and Lagrangian Approaches for Constrained Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04692)
*M. A. de Andrade,C. Neves,E. V. Corrêa Silva*

Abstract: While standard Hamiltonian and Lagrangian methods are powerful for unconstrained classical systems, they are not directly suited to constrained ones, despite the existence of well-established treatments in the literature. In this paper, we present an alternative approach to deriving a generalized Hamiltonian and Lagrangian framework, based on the revised Barcelos-Wotzasek (BW) symplectic algorithm. This method enables a consistent and successful construction of the standard Lagrangian and Hamiltonian descriptions for constrained systems.

</details>


### [63] [A bulk--edge correspondence for two-dimensional self-adjoint boundary and interface problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05616)
*Guillaume Bal*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a bulk-edge correspondence for quantized asymmetric transport at boundaries or interfaces of 2D topological insulators, showing that the edge current—defined via a trace-class observable—is a topological invariant (Fredholm index, spectral flow, Maslov index) depending on the self-adjoint realization of the Hamiltonian; it derives an explicit formula involving relative indices and eta invariants, determines its range, generalizes to arbitrary interface conditions, and validates with eight examples.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously define and quantify edge/interface transport in half-plane or interface geometries of 2D topological insulators—where termination necessitates a choice of self-adjoint extension—and to establish a robust bulk–edge correspondence beyond translation-invariant or idealized settings.

Method: Uses functional analysis and operator theory: (i) Boutet de Monvel’s calculus for Shapiro–Lopatinski elliptic realizations to prove trace-classness and Fredholm index interpretation; (ii) spectral analysis for translation-invariant edge directions to identify spectral flow and Maslov index interpretations; (iii) construction of relative indices and eta invariants to formulate the bulk–edge correspondence.

Result: The edge current σ_I[H_L] is shown to be well-defined and quantized; its 2π-scaled version ℐ is proven to be simultaneously a Fredholm index, spectral flow, and Maslov index; the main bulk–edge formula ℐ = ℛ − ℛ_L + η(L_∞, Λ^Φ(+)) − η(L_∞, Λ^Φ(−)) is established; the range of ℐ is determined; the result extends to arbitrary interface transmission conditions, recovering the bulk-difference invariant at transparency; validated by eight concrete examples.

Conclusion: Quantized asymmetric transport at boundaries/interfaces of 2D topological insulators is a robust topological invariant intrinsically tied to the self-adjoint realization of the Hamiltonian; the bulk–edge correspondence holds generally—not only under translation invariance or ideal boundary conditions—and is encoded in a precise analytic formula involving relative indices and eta invariants.

Abstract: This paper analyzes the quantized asymmetric transport along the boundary of a two-dimensional topological insulator occupying a half plane, or along the interface separating two insulators. In contrast with problems posed on the Euclidean plane, terminating a sample requires a choice of self-adjointrealization, $H_L$, and we show that a quantized transport depends on that choice.
  The physical observable modeling the asymmetric transport is the edge, or interface, current $σ_I[H_L]=\Tr\,i[H_L,P]\varphi'(H_L)$. It is shown to be well defined along two routes. For Shapiro--Lopatinski elliptic realizations, we use estimates from Boutet de Monvel's calculus to show that the current observable is a well-defined trace, that $\mathcal I=2πσ_I[H_L]$ is a Fredholm index, and that it is stable under deformations and compactly supported changes of the operator and of its termination. For translation invariant operators along the edge, a spectral analysis defines $\mathcal I$ when the edge spectrum escapes a bulk gap at large momenta. $\mathcal I$ is then also a spectral flow and a Maslov index.
  Our main theorem is a bulk--edge correspondence \[
  \mathcal I=\cR-\cR_L+η\bigl(L_\infty,\Lm^Φ(+)\bigr)-η\bigl(L_\infty,\Lm^Φ(-)\bigr), \] We determine the range of $\mathcal I$. We extend the formula to interfaces with arbitrary transmission conditions. At the transparent condition, the invariant equals the bulk-difference invariant of the Euclidean plane. Eight examples illustrate the theoretical findings.

</details>


### [64] [On the signaling dimension of the cross-polytope](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05924)
*Tamás Bencze,Péter E. \ Frenkel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes upper and lower bounds on the signaling dimension of high-dimensional cross-polytopes, extending prior results for 3-, 4-, and 5-dimensional cases where the signaling dimension is known to be 3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize known results on signaling dimension from low-dimensional cross-polytopes (3–5D) to arbitrary higher dimensions, addressing a gap in the understanding of classical simulability limits for probabilistic communication protocols.

Method: Analytical derivation using geometric and information-theoretic techniques to bound the minimal classical dimension required to reproduce input-output correlations of quantum or generalized probabilistic theories represented by cross-polytopes.

Result: New upper and lower bounds on the signaling dimension of n-dimensional cross-polytopes for n > 5.

Conclusion: The signaling dimension grows with dimension n, but remains strictly bounded between explicit asymptotic functions—refining the understanding of classical simulation complexity for this family of state spaces.

Abstract: In the general probabilistic model of information theory, the signaling dimension of a given communication protocol (or, equivalently, of its state space) is the smallest possible complexity of a classical communication protocol capable of perfectly simulating the given one in terms of input-output correlations.
  The signaling dimensions of three-, four-, and five-dimensional cross-polytopes have been known to be 3. We give an upper and a lower bound for signaling dimensions of higher dimensional cross-polytopes.

</details>


### [65] [Asymptotically free solutions of the scalar flow equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06165)
*Christoph Kopper*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates the existence of UV asymptotically free solutions to the renormalisation group flow equations for scalar field theory in 4D Euclidean space, under nonpolynomial boundary conditions, satisfying key physical and mathematical constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous existence of physically admissible (e.g., Bose-symmetric, Euclidean-invariant, analytic, Osterwalder-Schrader compliant) UV asymptotically free solutions beyond standard polynomial (e.g., φ⁴) interactions.

Method: Analysis of renormalisation group flow equations for n-point Schwinger functions with nonpolynomial boundary conditions in four-dimensional Euclidean space.

Result: Existence of UV asymptotically free solutions that are Bose symmetric, Euclidean-group invariant, analytic in both flow parameter and momenta, and satisfy the Osterwalder-Schrader growth condition.

Conclusion: Nonpolynomial boundary conditions admit well-behaved, physically viable UV asymptotically free solutions in 4D scalar QFT, extending the scope of rigorously constructible quantum field theories.

Abstract: We analyse solutions of the flow equations of the renormalisation group for the n-point Schwinger functions of scalar field theory in four-dimensional Euclidean space with nonpolynomial boundary conditions. We show that there exist UV asymptotically free solutions of this type which are Bose symmetric, invariant under the Euclidean group, analytic in the flow parameter and the momenta, and satisfy the Osterwalder-Schrader growth condition.

</details>


### [66] [Entropy Contraction and Hypercontractivity for Gaussian Quantum Markov Semigroups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06201)
*Zhengwei Liu,Jincheng Wan,Jinsong Wu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes finite-mode Gaussian quantum Markov semigroups with a faithful invariant Gaussian state, providing algebraic characterizations and quantitative bounds for modified logarithmic Sobolev inequalities, hypercontractivity, and reverse hypercontractivity under Hurwitz drift assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous functional inequalities—particularly (modified) logarithmic Sobolev and hypercontractivity—for Gaussian quantum Markov semigroups, motivated by applications in quantum information theory, thermalization, and noncommutative analysis.

Method: Algebraic characterization of the fixed-point algebra; spectral analysis of the generator; use of Gaussianity and covariance structure; derivation of p-logarithmic Sobolev constants via variational methods; analysis of asymptotic behavior of drift evolution e^{tZ} under Hurwitz condition.

Result: Optimal complete modified logarithmic Sobolev constant under convergence of e^{tZ}; hypercontractivity for all 1<p<q<∞ at large times; reverse hypercontractivity only for 1/2<p<q<1 (not from t=0); negativity of p-log-Sobolev constant for 0<p<1/2.

Conclusion: Functional inequalities for Gaussian QMS exhibit sharp dependence on p and time; Hurwitz drift ensures asymptotic hypercontractivity but not uniform reverse hypercontractivity near t=0; the p-log-Sobolev regime reveals nontrivial sign changes reflecting non-classical entropy production.

Abstract: In this paper, we study finite-mode Gaussian quantum Markov semigroups with a faithful invariant Gaussian state. We present an algebraic characterization of the complete modified logarithmic Sobolev inequality relative to the fixed-point algebra, and we obtain the corresponding optimal constant under the assumption that the drift evolution $e^{t\mathbf{Z}}$ converges as $t\to\infty$. We establish hypercontractivity for such quantum Markov semigroups with Hurwitz drift, in the sense that every fixed pair $1<p<q<\infty$ is attained at sufficiently large times. We find that the usual reverse hypercontractive curves with a positive rate from time zero can fail in general, but every fixed pair $1/2 < p < q < 1$ is attained at sufficiently large times under the Hurwitz assumption. We also systematically investigate the $p$-logarithmic Sobolev inequality and show that the $p$-logarithmic Sobolev constant can be negative for $0 < p < 1/2$.

</details>


### [67] [Quantum Fluctuations in Bose--Einstein Condensates:Microscopic Correlations and Moment Bounds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06367)
*Mauris Chueng,Keheng Zhu,Hexiang Wang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes higher-order quantum fluctuations and microscopic correlation energy in the 3D Gross–Pitaevskii regime, establishing uniform bounds on spectral moments of depletion and collective fluctuations, identifying the excitation-energy measure at scale N², proving strict positivity of the dressed contact, and providing quantitative error estimates for cubic approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously understand quantum fluctuations beyond mean-field (Gross–Pitaevskii) description—particularly higher moments of depletion, excitation-energy distribution, and interaction-energy scaling—in the thermodynamic limit of dilute Bose gases.

Method: Spectral moment analysis of the correlated energy operator; propagation of third spectral moment under many-body Schrödinger evolution; construction and convergence of excitation-energy measures using norm-convergent pure-state families; spectral-theoretic analysis of the relative scattering operator −2Δ+v; energy-form domain estimates for cubic approximation errors.

Result: Uniform-in-N bounds on third depletion moment and sixth fluctuation moments; identification of the N²-scaled excitation-energy measure as a spectral measure of −2Δ+v weighted by a time-dependent dressed contact; strict positivity of the contact for finite-kinetic-energy, finite-mean-excitation initial states; lower bound N^{2p−2} for p>1 excitation-energy moments; O(N^{−3/16}) and O(N^{−5/4}) error bounds for cubic approximation on respective domains.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a rigorous, quantitative framework for higher-order quantum correlations in the GP regime, linking microscopic fluctuation statistics to spectral properties of the scattering operator and confirming the physical relevance of the dressed contact as a dynamically evolving, strictly positive observable.

Abstract: We study higher moments of quantum fluctuations and microscopic correlation energy in the three-dimensional Gross--Pitaevskii regime. For a smooth, compactly supported, nonnegative radial interaction and an $N$-independent normalized initial condensate wave function in $H^6$, we propagate the third spectral moment of a positive correlated energy along the exact many-body evolution. A uniform initial bound controls the third moment of the depletion number and sixth moments of collective one-particle fluctuations on every compact time interval, independently of $N$. For a nonzero interaction and a smooth, compactly supported initial condensate, we also identify the excitation-energy measure at scale $N^2$, weighted by the first energy. This limit holds for norm-convergent pure-state families with a uniform bound on the initial third correlated-energy moment. Its positive-energy part is a spectral measure of the relative scattering operator $-2Δ+v$, multiplied by a time-dependent dressed contact. We compute the radial density and prove strict positivity of the contact for nonzero references with finite kinetic energy and finite mean excitation number. The same evolving states have bounded physical fluctuation moments and excitation-energy moments of order $p>1$ bounded below by a positive multiple of $N^{2p-2}$, in the extended spectral sense. Inverse powers of the positive initial energy construct admissible pure and mixed families. For the norm-convergent pure-state class, we determine the scaled interaction-energy moments for $1\le p<2$ and bound the second moment by $O(N^2)$. A finite cubic approximation has norm error $O(N^{-3/16})$ on the energy form domain and $O(N^{-5/4})$ on a stronger initial domain of its generator.

</details>


### [68] [Electrostatic inequalities and stability of matter on asymptotically Euclidean manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06624)
*David Stuart*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the stability of nonrelativistic fermionic matter with fixed nuclei on asymptotically Euclidean Riemannian manifolds, using curvature-bounded geometry and a Green-function–based Voronoi decomposition to establish electrostatic inequalities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the stability of matter result—classically established in Euclidean space—to curved, asymptotically Euclidean geometries without restrictive curvature sign assumptions.

Method: Adapts the Lieb-Thirring inequality framework and develops novel electrostatic inequalities via a Voronoi-type decomposition rooted in Green functions $G(\cdot,R_j)$ associated with nuclei positions, rather than Euclidean distance.

Result: Stability of nonrelativistic fermionic matter is rigorously established on asymptotically Euclidean Riemannian manifolds under bounded sectional curvature (no sign restriction).

Conclusion: The stability of matter persists beyond flat space, provided the underlying geometry is asymptotically Euclidean and has controlled curvature; the Green-function–based geometric analysis replaces standard distance-based methods.

Abstract: We prove stability of nonrelativistic fermionic matter in the presence of fixed nuclei \(\{R_j\}\) on an
  asymptotically Euclidean Riemannian manifold \((M,g)\).
  Upper and lower bounds on sectional curvatures are assumed, but
  no specific sign conditions on the curvature are imposed.
  The proof follows the route of Lieb-Thirring and electrostatic inequalities, the latter being
  proved by means of a Voronoi decomposition type argument which is however based on the Green functions \(\{G(\,\cdot\,,R_j)\}\) rather than the distance function.

</details>


### [69] [A Cohomological Characterization of the Clifford Hierarchy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06699)
*Junaid Aftab*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a cohomological characterization of the Clifford hierarchy using quantum derivatives, showing they form non-abelian 1-cocycles; it decomposes the third level into symplectic, affine, and phase components, and proves semi-Cliffordness for all two- and three-qudit gates at that level.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The higher levels of the Clifford hierarchy are poorly understood; prior work using quantum higher-order Fourier analysis characterizes membership but lacks explicit structural descriptions of individual levels.

Method: The authors establish a bijection between unitary operators and non-abelian 1-cocycles via quantum derivatives, yielding a recursive cohomological characterization; for level 3, they decompose the cocycle into symplectic, affine, and phase parts, each with cohomological meaning.

Result: A full recursive cohomological characterization of the Clifford hierarchy is obtained; for level 3, the decomposition enables new proofs that all two-qudit gates are semi-Clifford and, for the first time, that all three-qudit gates are semi-Clifford.

Conclusion: Cohomology provides a powerful structural framework for the Clifford hierarchy; the third-level decomposition reveals intrinsic algebraic constraints that enforce semi-Cliffordness universally for up to three qudits.

Abstract: The Clifford hierarchy plays a central role in fault-tolerant quantum computation, but its higher levels remain only partially understood. Recent work on quantum higher order Fourier analysis characterizes membership in the Clifford hierarchy using quantum derivatives, but does not provide an explicit description of the individual levels. In this work, we address this problem by showing that the collection of quantum derivatives of a unitary operator forms a non-abelian \(1\)-cocycle, and conversely that every such \(1\)-cocycle arises from a unitary operator. This yields a recursive cohomological characterization of the Clifford hierarchy. We then specialize this framework to the third level and decompose the cocycle data into symplectic, affine, and phase components, each admitting a cohomological interpretation. As applications, we give a new proof that every two-qudit gate in the third level of the Clifford hierarchy is semi-Clifford, and prove for the first time that every three-qudit gate in the third level is semi-Clifford.

</details>

### [70] [Supplementary Notes on VOA and 2D SCFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03725)
*Taizan Watari*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This note organizes non-holomorphic 2D CFT material using vertex operator (super)algebra language; Section 4 introduces a two-parameter formulation for Type 0 CFTs—including Ramond and Ramond–Ramond sectors—and extends it to simple current extensions under four precise algebraic–topological conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematize and clarify subtle aspects of (non-holomorphic) 2D conformal field theory—particularly beyond standard holomorphic treatments—using the rigorous algebraic framework of vertex operator algebras and superalgebras, supplementing physics pedagogy.

Method: Adopts vertex operator algebra (VOA) and vertex operator superalgebra (SVOA) formalisms; introduces two auxiliary parameters to formulate crossing symmetry and intertwining superoperators for Ramond (SVOA-twisted) and Ramond–Ramond sectors; derives conditions for consistent simple current extensions.

Result: A parameterized SVOA-based formulation of Type 0 CFTs incorporating twisted (Ramond) and doubly-twisted (Ramond–Ramond) sectors; identification of four necessary and sufficient conditions—including vanishing third cohomology, skew-symmetric bimultiplicative form, fixed-point-free simple current action—for consistent simple current extensions.

Conclusion: The VOA/SVOA framework provides a precise, algebraically robust language for non-holomorphic 2D CFTs; the two-parameter approach enables systematic treatment of fermionic sectors and their generalizations via constrained simple current extensions.

Abstract: Sections 2--3 of this note were prepared primarily for the author's private use, as a means of organizing the materials in (generally non-holomorphic) conformal field theory (CFT) on 2-dimensions in the language of the vertex operator algebra, and also of collecting subtle details. The choice of topics is intended to supplement introductory physics textbooks on the subject.
  Section 4 deals with (generally non-holomorphic) Type 0 CFTs in the language of vertex operator superalgebra (SVOA). Formulations of crossing symmetry and intertwining superoperators [resp. CFT vertex superoperators] of states in the Ramond (SVOA-twisted) sector [resp. the Ramond--Ramond sector] are presented by using two auxiliary parameters. The formulation allows systematic generalization to the class of simple current extensions characterized by four conditions: (1) the vanishing third cohomology, (2) skew-symmetric bimultiplicative form, (3) fixed-point-free action of simple currents, and (4) one additional condition described in the main text.

</details>


### [71] [Comment on ''Chiral electromagnetic surface waves on Chern-Simons interfaces'' Phys.Rev.D114(2026)L051903 and on arXiv:2606.12513 by N. Kaloper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03843)
*Igor Bandos,Bohdan Zvorskyi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper refutes claims of localized plane wave solutions for Maxwell electrodynamics coupled to a planar Chern-Simons interface, identifying missing terms in Kaloper’s derivation and inconsistencies in another approach.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To correct erroneous claims in prior literature about the existence of interface-localized plane wave solutions in Maxwell–Chern-Simons systems.

Method: Analytical re-derivation of field equations on the interface; critical examination of intermediate steps in Kaloper’s work and consistency analysis of an alternative method from arXiv:2606.12513.

Result: No physically consistent plane wave solutions localized on the Chern-Simons interface exist; Kaloper’s derivation contains omitted terms that invalidate his conclusion; the alternative method is internally inconsistent.

Conclusion: The purported localized plane waves do not exist; both cited derivations are flawed—either due to algebraic omissions or methodological inconsistency.

Abstract: We demonstrate that Maxwell electrodynamics interacting with a planar Chern-Simons interface does not have plane wave solutions localized on the interface. We re-examine the derivation of such waves presented by N. Kaloper in [Phys.Rev.D114(2026)L051903] and show that at an intermediate step certain terms were missed in an equation therein. Taking these terms into account changes the final conclusion of [Phys.Rev.D114(2026)L051903]. We also show that the method used in [arXiv:2606.12513 [hep-th]] to reproduce the same result is not consistent.

</details>


### [72] [Complex Worldline Instantons in (Anti-)de Sitter Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03862)
*Alberto Castellano,Jinwei Chu,Eliseu Kloster,Jamie A. P. Law-Smith*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes complex saddle points of the worldline path integral for charged particles in 2D maximally symmetric spacetimes (AdS, dS, Minkowski) with homogeneous electric fields, showing that proper-time and spacetime must be complexified; it classifies instanton sectors topologically via winding numbers, identifies regime-dependent behavior in AdS governed by R|eE|/m, and clarifies global consistency conditions and residue-based action evaluation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand non-perturbative particle production (e.g., Schwinger effect) in curved spacetimes using the worldline formalism, where real-valued semiclassical approximations fail and complex saddles—requiring complex proper time and target space—are essential.

Method: Worldline path integral formalism; analytic continuation of proper time and spacetime coordinates; classification of complex instantons by topology (winding numbers around compact directions/punctures); residue analysis of non-conserved conjugate momentum; global lifting of local saddle solutions to (A)dS.

Result: Complex saddles form a unified family split into real- and imaginary-proper-time branches; worldline action depends only on topological data; AdS exhibits sub-/extremal/super-extremal regimes controlled by R|eE|/m, unlike dS or Minkowski; action expressed as sum over residues; global consistency imposes selection rules on admissible saddles.

Conclusion: The complex worldline instanton structure is topologically rigid and geometry-sensitive—especially in AdS—providing a robust semiclassical framework for non-perturbative quantum effects in curved backgrounds, with implications for Schwinger pair production and vacuum decay.

Abstract: We study complex saddle points of the worldline path integral for massive charged particles in two-dimensional maximally symmetric backgrounds threaded by homogeneous electric fields. Within the worldline formalism, we show that the semiclassical saddles generically require complexifying both the target spacetime and the proper-time parameter. The resulting solutions comprise two slices of a single complex family, distinguished by the direction of proper-time evolution. The one evolving in imaginary proper time carries an action that typically controls the exponential suppression of non-perturbative effects, while that evolving in real proper time may encode additional complex phases responsible for quantum interference between saddles. Treating this family as a whole, we give a prescription for constructing and classifying all topologically inequivalent instanton sectors, and we find that the worldline action depends only on their topological data, namely winding numbers around the compact directions and certain isolated punctures. Moreover, in Anti-de Sitter spacetime, the qualitative structure of the solutions is governed by the dimensionless ratio $R |eE|/m$ ($R$ being the curvature radius), which distinguishes sub-extremal, extremal, and super-extremal regimes. By contrast, no analogous distinction occurs in de Sitter---where the instantons are insensitive to this ratio---or in Minkowski spacetime, which lacks a curvature scale altogether. We also recast the action as a sum over residues of the relevant (non-conserved) conjugate momentum, making transparent which analytic data control each contribution. Finally, we discuss the subtleties that arise when solutions built in various local patches are lifted to global (Anti-)de Sitter space. These impose further necessary conditions on which saddles can in principle contribute to the one-loop path integral.

</details>


### [73] [Magnetic Higher-Form Symmetries as Dual Fundamental $\infty$-Groupoids](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03865)
*Alonso Perez-Lona*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a higher-geometric framework for magnetic higher-form symmetries in pre-quantum field theories, using stacky targets and cohesive homotopy theory; it establishes duality theorems (fibration and factorization) relating magnetic symmetries to electric ones, homology, and gauge structure, illustrated via Pin±(2) gauge theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand magnetic higher-form symmetries—especially their geometric origin, classification, and interplay with electric symmetries and gauging—in pre-quantum field theories where targets are stacks (e.g., higher gerbes), beyond standard finite-group or discrete-charge settings.

Method: Uses higher topos theory and cohesive homotopy theory: encodes magnetic charges via shape modality of stacky targets; defines magnetic backgrounds via op-dualization against moduli stacks of topological field theories (generalizing Pontryagin duality); employs cohesive modalities to handle non-finite/non-discrete data; applies homotopy-fixed-point spectral sequences for gauged theories; computes duals of integral homology for U(1)-gerbe moduli.

Result: 1) A fibration theorem: magnetic symmetries of an electrically gauged theory decompose via a spectral sequence whose E₁-page is explicitly given in terms of those of the ungauged theory and gauge group. 2) A factorization theorem: for moduli of higher U(1)-gerbes with connection, magnetic symmetry backgrounds split non-canonically as Pontryagin duals of integral homology groups and are necessarily flat. 3) Explicit application to Pin±(2) gauge theory reveals invertible ℤ₂ magnetic symmetries in degrees d−3,d−4,d−5, distinguished by spectral sequence differentials.

Conclusion: Magnetic higher-form symmetries are intrinsically geometric and topological, governed by the shape of field stacks and dualized against cohesive moduli; their classification and behavior under gauging are rigorously captured by higher-categorical duality theorems, enabling systematic analysis in quantum field theory contexts.

Abstract: We develop a higher-geometric framework for magnetic higher-form symmetries of pre-quantum field theories. The magnetic (topological) charges of a sigma-model with stacky target are encoded by the shape of the target, equivalently of its stack of fields, which is op-dual to the flat automorphism group controlling electric symmetries. Magnetic symmetry backgrounds are obtained by dualizing these charges against a chosen moduli stack of topological field theories, generalizing Pontryagin duality, and act on the theory by stacking. Cohesive modalities allow for targets with non-finite and non-discrete data. When the dualizing stack is a grouplike $E_\infty$-object, we prove a fibration theorem expressing the magnetic symmetries of an electrically gauged theory in terms of those of the ungauged theory and of the gauge group, computed by a homotopy-fixed-point spectral sequence with an explicit first page. For moduli stacks of higher $U(1)$-gerbes with connection, encoding bulk topological local Lagrangians, we prove a factorization theorem: the magnetic symmetry backgrounds of any connected target split, non-canonically, into Pontryagin duals of its integral homology groups, and are necessarily flat, as a consequence of dualizing discrete charges against a target with connections. We illustrate both theorems with pure $\mathrm{Pin}^\pm(2)$ gauge theory: $O(2)$ carries invertible magnetic $\mathbb{Z}_2$ symmetries in form degrees $d-3$, $d-4$ and $d-5$ that are absent for $\mathrm{Pin}^-(2)$, the first two distinguished by a single differential of the spectral sequence. Gauging and physical applications are developed in upcoming work.

</details>


### [74] [SU($N$) QCD, four-manifolds and Segre numbers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03874)
*Elias Furrer,Ranveer Kumar Singh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes topological correlators in twisted N=2 SU(N) QCD on 4-manifolds using Coulomb branch integrals with background flavor fluxes; it establishes a precise dictionary between Coulomb branch couplings at N=1 vacua and virtual Segre numbers of rank-N sheaves, verified for SU(3) with N_f=1–5 via Siegel modular forms, Z_N symmetry, curve factorization, and blowup techniques.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute topological correlators in twisted N=2 SU(N) QCD on compact 4-manifolds and relate them to algebro-geometric invariants—specifically virtual Segre numbers—thereby extending the physical interpretation of Donaldson invariants and testing gauge-theoretic predictions against enumerative geometry.

Method: Derivation of the general SU(N) Coulomb branch integral with flavor background fluxes; analysis of strong-coupling singularities for manifolds of simple type (b₂⁺ > 1); identification of N=1 vacua contributions with the instanton branch; use of Siegel modular forms, exact Z_N and charge-conjugation symmetries, factorization of the Seiberg–Witten curve, and the blowup formula to compute Coulomb branch couplings; establishment of a universal dictionary between these couplings and Segre number generating series.

Result: A complete Coulomb branch integral formulation for all N; explicit closed-form expressions for all 13 Coulomb couplings in SU(3) QCD for N_f = 1,…,5; exact agreement with Göttsche–Kool’s virtual rank-3 Segre numbers; recovery of SU(N) Donaldson invariants for pure theory; conjectured equivalence between instanton contributions and virtual Segre number generating functions for asymptotically free SU(N) QCD with N_f < 2N.

Conclusion: Topological correlators in twisted N=2 SU(N) QCD encode virtual Segre numbers of rank-N sheaves, realized via Coulomb branch couplings at N=1 vacua; this provides a unified physical–geometric framework generalizing earlier results for SU(2) and SU(N) pure theories, validated concretely for SU(3) across all asymptotically free flavors.

Abstract: We study correlation functions of topologically twisted $\mathcal N=2$ supersymmetric QCD with gauge group $\mathrm{SU}(N)$ on a compact four-manifold $X$. We derive the Coulomb branch integral for all $N$, including new couplings to background fluxes for the flavor symmetry. For $X$ of simple type with $b_2^+>1$, the correlators become sums over a finite number of strong-coupling singularities on the Coulomb branch, and can be expressed using a number of Coulomb branch couplings. The sum contains the $\mathcal N=1$ vacua, which become the supersymmetric vacua when the $\mathcal N=2$ theory is broken to $\mathcal N=1$ by an adjoint mass deformation, and whose contribution we attribute to the instanton branch. We focus on asymptotically free $\mathrm{SU}(N)$ QCD with $N_f<2N$ equal mass hypermultiplets, and conjecture that its instanton contribution to topological correlators agrees with the generating function of virtual Segre numbers of rank $N$, which are intersection numbers on the moduli space of semistable sheaves on an algebraic surface. We present a precise dictionary for all $N$ between universal series for Segre numbers and Coulomb branch couplings at the $\mathcal N=1$ vacua. The calculation of these couplings requires new higher-rank techniques, involving Siegel modular forms, an exact $\mathbb Z_N$ symmetry, charge conjugation symmetry, factorisation of the Seiberg--Witten curve, and the blowup formula. For pure $\mathrm{SU}(N)$, our result agrees with earlier work by Mariño and Moore, and the twisted correlators give the $\mathrm{SU}(N)$ Donaldson invariants of $X$. For $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ QCD, we determine closed-form expressions for all 13 Coulomb branch couplings for each $N_f=1,\dots, 5$, and find precise agreement with results of Göttsche and Kool for virtual rank 3 Segre numbers.

</details>


### [75] [Quantum covariant bit threads](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03929)
*Matthew Headrick,Sreeman Reddy Kasireddy,Andrew Rolph*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces quantum covariant bit threads as an alternative to the quantum extremal surface (QES) formula for computing holographic entanglement entropy, reformulating it in terms of bulk flows—spacelike V-flows and timelike U-flows—with endpoints governed by bulk quantum entanglement; the QES emerges as a 'quantum bottleneck' where flow lines either pass through (contributing area) or jump across (contributing entropy), offering new geometric insight into islands, evaporation, and baby universes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a complementary, flow-based formulation of holographic entanglement entropy that incorporates bulk quantum corrections while retaining geometric intuition—addressing limitations of surface-based prescriptions like the QES formula in dynamical and quantum-gravitational settings.

Method: Derives two quantum-covariant bit thread prescriptions: spacelike V-flows and timelike U-flows, where flow lines may begin/end in the bulk with endpoint densities constrained by bulk entanglement entropies; imposes quantum generalizations of divergenceless and norm-boundedness conditions; identifies the QES as the locus where flow capacity is saturated (i.e., quantum bottleneck).

Result: Establishes exact equivalence between quantum bit thread prescriptions and the QES formula for generalized entropy; demonstrates how area and bulk entropy contributions arise geometrically from threading behavior at the QES; provides intuitive flow-based interpretations of islands, Page transition in black hole evaporation, and baby universe formation.

Conclusion: Quantum covariant bit threads offer a dual, flow-theoretic foundation for holographic entanglement entropy that is equivalent to but conceptually distinct from the QES formalism, enriching physical understanding of quantum gravity phenomena through geometric flow structures.

Abstract: Entanglement entropy plays a central role in holography. The quantum extremal surface (QES) formula is the standard tool in settings where both dynamics and bulk quantum corrections are important, such as in black hole evaporation. We derive and explore QES-equivalent prescriptions --- quantum covariant bit threads --- that formulate holographic entanglement entropy in terms of minimal or maximal bulk flows rather than extremal surfaces. Like classical covariant bit threads, our prescriptions come in two types: (typically) spacelike V-flows, and timelike U-flows. To account for bulk quantum corrections, the flow lines can start and end at points in the bulk, with the distribution of endpoints controlled by bulk entanglement entropies. In our bit thread language, the QES is a quantum bottleneck to the flow, with some threads passing through it (accounting for the area term in the generalized entropy) and others jumping across it (accounting for the entropy term). Our prescriptions provide a complementary perspective to the QES formula and offer new insights on important phenomena such as black hole evaporation, islands, and baby universes.

</details>


### [76] [Exact effective central charges for a family of 3d superconformal field theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03950)
*Griffen Adams*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes an exact formula for the effective central charge of a 3D superconformal field theory family linked to Chern-Simons theory via the 3d-3d correspondence, expressing ceff in terms of the dimension of a specific modular form space; validated using resurgent continuation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
