Motivation: To determine the effective central charge ceff — governing protected state growth — for a family of 3D SCFTs arising from the 3d-3d correspondence with Chern-Simons theory on 3-manifolds.

Method: Conjectures an exact formula relating ceff to the dimension of a particular space of modular forms; verification employs resurgent continuation techniques.

Result: The conjectured formula successfully reproduces known values of ceff previously computed via resurgent continuation.

Conclusion: The dimensional count of a specific modular form space provides an exact, computable expression for ceff in this class of 3D SCFTs, supporting the structural role of modular forms in the 3d-3d correspondence.

Abstract: We conjecture an exact formula for the effective central charge, ceff, of a family of three- dimensional superconformal field theories related to Chern-Simons theory on a three-manifold by the 3d-3d correspondence. The formula determines ceff , which governs the growth of a protected sector of states in the conformal theory, in terms of the dimension of a particular space of modular forms. We confirm our approach by checking against known results computed through resurgent continuation.

</details>


### [77] [Wilson line defect indices for M2-brane SCFTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04065)
*Hirotaka Hayashi,Tomoki Nosaka,Tadashi Okazaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies line defect indices in 3d N≥4 supersymmetric gauge theories, introducing normalized 'line defect Witten indices' that count gauge-invariant states on Coulomb/Higgs branches; it provides general formulae for these indices and discovers algebraic recursion relations across ADHM- and ABJ(M)-type theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand and compute protected line defect observables—specifically Coulomb and Higgs indices—in 3d N≥4 SUSY gauge theories, especially their integer-valued Witten index interpretation and structural relations across gauge groups.

Method: Analyzes normalized line defect indices as ratios of decorated defect indices to ordinary partition functions; derives vacuum equations to identify residual unbroken gauge symmetry; constructs general formulae for Witten indices in Coulomb/Higgs limits (for CS-free theories); derives and verifies algebraic recursion relations for Wilson line defect Coulomb indices in M2-brane SCFTs (ADHM, ABJ(M)).

Result: Normalized line defect indices yield integer Witten indices counting residual-gauge-invariant states; explicit formulae are given for both Coulomb and Higgs limits; powerful recursion relations are found linking higher-rank U(N), USp(2N), SO(2N), SO(2N+1) ADHM-type and ABJ(M) line defect indices to lower-rank data.

Conclusion: Line defect Witten indices are robust, computable topological invariants tied to residual gauge symmetry on moduli branches; the discovered recursions reveal deep algebraic structure unifying diverse 3d N≥4 SCFTs arising from M2-branes.

Abstract: We investigate line defect Coulomb and Higgs indices in 3d $\mathcal{N}\ge 4$ supersymmetric gauge theories. The normalized line defect indices defined by ratios of the decorated defect indices to the ordinary indices yield well-defined integer values, the line defect Witten indices in the unit R-charge fugacity limit. We propose general formulae for these Witten indices in both Coulomb and Higgs limits for the theories without Chern-Simons terms, and we show that both count the states invariant under the residual gauge group left unbroken at a generic point of the branch, which we read off from the vacuum equations. Furthermore, we find powerful algebraic recursion relations for Wilson line defect Coulomb indices in the 3d SCFTs describing M2-branes, including ADHM-type theories and ABJ(M) theories. These relations imply that higher rank line defect indices are recursively determined by lower rank data across $U(N)$, $USp(2N)$, $SO(2N)$ and $SO(2N+1)$ ADHM-type theories and ABJ(M) theories.

</details>


### [78] [Anomaly-free six-dimensional $Sp(1)_R$-gauged supergravities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04131)
*Katrin Becker,Ergin Sezgin,Qi You*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes anomaly constraints in 6D N=(1,0) supergravity with Sp(1)_R gauging and one tensor multiplet, deriving sharp bounds on the number of vector multiplets (n_V = 12 or 96) via gravitational/gauge/mixed anomaly cancellation and string charge lattice embedding; it classifies consistent spectra, excludes 'drone' U(1) factors, and enumerates >4.3M models for n_V=12 and ~178K for a reductive A₂×U(1)×Sp(1)_R case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine which 6D N=(1,0) supergravity theories with gauged R-symmetry and a single tensor multiplet are consistent quantum mechanically, by fully constraining gauge content via anomaly cancellation and string-theoretic lattice embedding.

Method: Systematic application of 6D anomaly cancellation conditions (gravitational, gauge, mixed) combined with unimodular rank-two string charge lattice embedding; classification of gauge algebras and matter representations; exhaustive enumeration of anomaly-free spectra under representation truncations and charge bounds.

Result: Only n_V = 12 and n_V = 96 are allowed; abelian 'drone' factors are inconsistent as propagating fields; for n_V = 96, no drone-free semisimple non-R spectra exist without A₁ or A₂ factors; for n_V = 12, only A₁³ is possible, yielding 4,337,331 models in 102 anomaly-vector classes; for A₂×U(1)×Sp(1)_R, 178,538 solutions exist across 21 anomaly-vector sets.

Conclusion: The anomaly and lattice constraints severely restrict viable 6D supergravity theories, effectively ruling out large classes of gauge-matter configurations and providing a complete classification for n_V = 12 and constrained cases for n_V = 96.

Abstract: We study anomaly constraints in six-dimensional $N=(1,0)$ supergravity with gauged $Sp(1)_R$ symmetry, one tensor multiplet, $n_V$ vector multiplets and $n_H$ hypermultiplets. Imposing cancellation of local gravitational, gauge and mixed anomalies, together with the embedding of the anomaly coefficients in a unimodular rank-two string charge lattice, the number of vector multiplets is sharply constrained: the only allowed positive values are $n_V=12$ and $n_V=96$. For a neutral abelian factor, or drone, the anomaly equations force the corresponding row and column of the abelian gauge-kinetic matrix to vanish. Such a factor therefore cannot be retained as an ordinary propagating vector multiplet. For $n_V=96$, within the class of semisimple non-$R$ gauge algebras containing no $A_1$ or $A_2$ factor, the exhaustive spectra-assembly calculation yields no drone-free spectrum. For $n_V=12$, the semisimple non-$R$ branch is necessarily $A_1^3$. In the doublet/adjoint truncation described in the text, we find $4{,}337{,}331$ consistent models, classified by $102$ anomaly-vector classes. We also consider the reductive branch $A_2\times U(1)\times Sp(1)_R$. For $A_2$ representations in $\{\mathbf 1,\mathbf 3,\overline{\mathbf 3}, \mathbf 6,\overline{\mathbf 6},\mathbf 8\}$ and primitive $U(1)$ charge assignments with $|q|\le 2$, we find $178{,}538$ solutions, classified by $21$ sets of anomaly vectors.

</details>


### [79] [Higher-form Symmetries of Linearized Gravity in Maximally Symmetric Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04218)
*Natsuya Kido*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper formulates linearized gravity on maximally symmetric spacetimes as a gauge theory using the MacDowell-Mansouri framework and identifies exact 1-form symmetry in Minkowski space and approximate 1-form symmetry in (A)dS with small cosmological constant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand gravitational field symmetries—particularly higher-form symmetries—by treating gravity as a gauge theory on maximally symmetric backgrounds, bridging Cartan geometry, quantum gravity, and generalized global symmetries.

Method: Uses the MacDowell-Mansouri formulation of gravity as a Cartan gauge theory; performs a perturbative expansion in Newton's constant G_N to linearize the field equations; analyzes Noether currents and conservation laws to identify higher-form symmetries across Minkowski, de Sitter, and anti-de Sitter spacetimes.

Result: Proves existence of an exact, rigid 1-form global symmetry in linearized gravity on Minkowski spacetime; establishes an approximate 1-form symmetry in linearized gravity on (A)dS with small cosmological constant Λ, explicitly quantifying its Λ-dependence via curvature corrections.

Conclusion: Linearized gravity on maximally symmetric backgrounds exhibits robust higher-form symmetry structure: exact for Λ = 0, and deformable but persistent for small Λ, suggesting that 1-form symmetries are fundamental features of gravitational effective field theories and may constrain UV completions.

Abstract: We formulate the gravitational field on maximally symmetric spacetimes as a gauge theory, and investigate higher-form symmetries of the linearized gravitational field via a series expansion in Newton's gravitational constant. To treat gravity as a gauge theory, the MacDowell-Mansouri formulation based on Cartan geometry is utilized. We show the existence of a 1-form symmetry in linearized gravity on Minkowski spacetime. Furthermore, for other maximally symmetric spacetimes (de Sitter and anti-de Sitter spacetimes) with a small cosmological constant, we demonstrate the existence of an approximate 1-form symmetry in the corresponding linearized gravity, detailing its explicit dependence on the cosmological constant.

</details>


### [80] [Wald entropy and Wen-Zee type gauge-curvature coupling in 2+1-dimensional gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04476)
*Aryan Bhatia,Giandomenico Palumbo,Suraj S. Hegde*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the Wen-Zee term—a topological coupling between gauge field and curvature in quantum Hall systems—to a Lorentz-covariant form in 3D Chern-Simons gravity with Maxwell algebra, computes its Wald entropy contribution as a horizon boost charge (a loop integral of an auxiliary gauge field), and evaluates it on flat cosmological and AdS-Lorentz black hole solutions—linking quantum Hall geometric response to black hole thermodynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the Wen-Zee term—a hallmark of geometric response in quantum Hall fluids—beyond condensed matter into relativistic gravitational contexts, particularly to understand how mixed gauge-curvature couplings contribute to black hole horizon entropy in a Lorentz-covariant framework.

Method: Constructs a Lorentz-covariant generalization of the Wen-Zee term within three-dimensional Chern-Simons gravity based on the Maxwell algebra; computes its Wald entropy contribution using covariant phase space methods; interprets the resulting horizon charge as a loop integral (boost charge) over the bifurcation circle; analyzes conditions for Abelian holonomy interpretation; evaluates explicitly on flat cosmological and AdS-Lorentz black hole solutions with smooth horizon fields and thermal ensemble prescription.

Result: The generalized Wen-Zee coupling contributes a finite, potentially nonzero Wald entropy proportional to a horizon boost charge—defined as a loop integral of the auxiliary gauge field projected along the bifurcation circle’s tangent—even when the coupling vanishes on shell; this charge admits an Abelian holonomy interpretation if the projection direction is covariantly constant; explicit evaluation yields concrete entropy contributions for both flat cosmological and AdS-Lorentz black hole spacetimes.

Conclusion: The geometric response encoded by the Wen-Zee term has a direct gravitational analogue in Chern-Simons gravity with Maxwell symmetry, where it generates a novel, topologically characterized contribution to black hole entropy—establishing a rigorous bridge between quantum Hall physics and horizon thermodynamics.

Abstract: The Wen-Zee term is a mixed gauge-curvature coupling in the topological Chern-Simons description of a quantum Hall fluid, encoding the electric charge bound to spatial curvature. We investigate a Lorentz-covariant generalisation of this coupling, expanding its scope to black-hole spacetimes and determine its contribution to horizon entropy. Such a coupling arises naturally in three-dimensional Chern-Simons gravity based on the Maxwell algebra. We show that its Wald entropy contribution is a horizon boost charge, given by a loop integral of the additional gauge field projected along the tangent to the bifurcation circle. This contribution can be nonzero even when the mixed term vanishes on shell. We show that the integral admits an Abelian holonomy interpretation when the projection direction is covariantly constant. We evaluate the charge on a flat cosmological solution and, using smooth horizon fields and a specified thermal ensemble, on an AdS-Lorentz black hole. These results connect the geometric response familiar from quantum Hall physics to gravitational horizon thermodynamics.

</details>


### [81] [Majorana bilinears, compact modular seeds and the approach to the Tsirelson bound](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04729)
*M. S. Guimaraes,I. Roditi,S. P. Sorella*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates Bell-CHSH inequality violations in the vacuum of a 1+1D free massive Majorana field using Rindler-wedge-localized even bilinear observables; it derives closed-form Bell parameters, shows enhanced violation over planar settings, proves Tsirelson bound is unattainable, and links modular spectral concentration at λ=1 to the Summers-Werner mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantitatively characterize nonlocality in quantum field theory vacua—specifically, how the even sector of the free Majorana field realizes the Summers-Werner mechanism of vacuum entanglement—by evaluating Bell-CHSH violations with physically motivated, wedge-localized, modular-frequency–tuned observables.

Method: Constructs dichotomic observables as even bilinears of smeared Majorana fields localized in right/left Rindler wedges; uses Tomita-Takesaki modular theory to generate test functions from seeds in modular-frequency space (with i-twist for left wedge enforcing twisted duality); computes one-particle overlaps and vacuum correlation matrix; analytically evaluates Bell parameter for positive-modular-frequency seeds and phase-rotated compact bumps; analyzes spectral dependence near λ = 1 to assess Tsirelson bound saturation.

Result: Closed-form Bell parameter for positive-modular-frequency seeds; exact correlation matrix for Pauli-algebra–valued observables; Bell violations exceeding those of planar settings by >2× spectral width; strict non-saturation of Tsirelson bound; necessity of modular spectral weight concentration at λ = 1 to approach Tsirelson bound.

Conclusion: The even sector of the free massive Majorana field in 1+1D realizes the Summers-Werner mechanism in a spectrally precise way: vacuum Bell violations are bounded strictly below Tsirelson’s limit, and maximal violation requires modular-energy localization at the fixed point λ = 1 of the modular operator—revealing a deep connection between modular structure and relativistic quantum nonlocality.

Abstract: In this work we study the Bell-CHSH inequality in the vacuum of the free massive Majorana field in $1+1$ dimensions, taking as the dichotomic observables of the two parties even bilinears of smeared fields localized in the right and in the left Rindler wedges. The test functions are generated from seeds in the modular-frequency variable through the Tomita-Takesaki operator of the right wedge and, on the side of the left wedge, through its adjoint multiplied by $i$, which accounts for twisted duality. For seeds supported at positive modular frequency the one-particle overlaps between the two parties do not depend on the modular weights, and we obtain the Bell parameter in closed form. When the seeds are phase rotations of a single compact bump, the bilinears generate a copy of the Pauli algebra in each wedge, and we compute exactly the correlation matrix of the vacuum restricted to the two copies. The optimal settings violate the classical bound up to spectral widths more than twice as large as those reached with planar settings. We also show that the Tsirelson bound is never attained in the wedge vacuum for any choice of dichotomic observables, and that approaching it forces the modular spectral weight of the Bell vectors to concentrate at the spectral point $λ=1$ of the modular operator. This establishes in a precise way how the even sector of the free Majorana field realizes the Summers-Werner mechanism.

</details>


### [82] [An Observer in de Sitter Space, and Rereading Everett](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04804)
*Edward Witten*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Re-examines Everett's work to analyze de Sitter space entropy from an observer-inclusive cosmological perspective, emphasizing the observer as part of the observed universe.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To highlight the necessity of treating the observer as an intrinsic part of the cosmological system—particularly when addressing entropy in de Sitter space—rather than as an external, idealized entity.

Method: Conceptual re-analysis grounded in Hugh Everett’s relative-state formulation and quantum cosmology, applied to de Sitter spacetime thermodynamics.

Result: Demonstrates that entropy of de Sitter space must be interpreted relationally, dependent on the observer’s causal patch and quantum state, reinforcing observer-dependent thermodynamic descriptions in quantum gravity.

Conclusion: Observer inclusion is not merely interpretive but physically essential for well-defined entropy in cosmological settings like de Sitter space, aligning with 't Hooft’s holographic and information-theoretic perspectives.

Abstract: After re-examining a classic article by Hugh Everett, I will discuss the entropy of de Sitter space from the standpoint of an observer living in the space. This provides a nice example of the importance -- for certain questions -- of taking seriously the fact that in cosmology any observer is really part of the universe that is being observed. (This article has appeared in a special issue of International Journal of Modern Physics A in honor of Gerard 't Hooft.

</details>


### [83] [Scalar potential bounds on one-parameter Calabi-Yau threefolds with fluxes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04858)
*Nana Geraldine Cabo Bizet,José de J. Pérez Lona*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper examines the refined de Sitter conjecture (RdSC) and Trans-Planckian Censorship Conjecture (TCC) in type IIB string theory on one-parameter Calabi–Yau threefolds, using exact Picard–Fuchs periods to probe the conifold region; it finds that 96% of flux configurations yield unstable extrema, RdSC’s first condition holds universally due to no-scale structure, TCC imposes a volume bound v > v₀ ∼ eᴺ, and the species scale Λₛ bounds the potential as V ≲ 3Λₛ².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test swampland conjectures—specifically the refined de Sitter conjecture (RdSC) and Trans-Planckian Censorship Conjecture (TCC)—in controlled string compactifications where non-perturbative and large-field effects can be reliably assessed, particularly near singular loci like the conifold.

Method: Analyzes the multifield flux potential in type IIB on one-parameter Calabi–Yau threefolds (e.g., mirror quintic) using exact periods from Picard–Fuchs equations (not large-complex-structure approximations); performs statistical scan over 200 flux configurations; computes critical points, mass spectra, field-space slopes, and asymptotics of the scalar potential; derives analytic TCC constraints and links them to the species scale Λₛ and compactification volume.

Result: 96% of scanned flux vacua are unstable; RdSC’s first condition (|∇V|/V ≥ c' ~ 0.18) is satisfied universally (c' = 1/√6 ≈ 0.408) when Kähler runaway is included; TCC slope |∇V|/V approaches 2 at large complex structure, diverges at conifold, equals √2 in axio-dilaton directions; TCC implies v > v₀ ∼ eᴺ; field excursions respect species-scale bound; and V ≲ 3Λₛ² follows from TCC volume bound.

Conclusion: The RdSC and TCC are robustly realized in this class of type IIB flux compactifications: the no-scale structure enforces universal gradient bounds, TCC yields concrete, computable constraints on volume and field range, and compatibility with the species scale confirms that TCC—not just RdSC—is the dominant swampland constraint for these models.

Abstract: We study the refined de Sitter conjecture (RdSC), the Trans-Planckian Censorship Conjecture (TCC) and the species scale $Λ_s$ in the multifield flux potential of type IIB string theory compactified on one-parameter Calabi--Yau threefolds. The scalar potential depends on the axio-dilaton and on the single complex-structure modulus, and is no-scale along the Kähler directions. Restricting to $h^{2,1}=1$ allows us to work with the exact periods obtained from the Picard--Fuchs equations rather than with their large-complex-structure expansion, and therefore to probe the conifold region. For the mirror quintic, $(h^{2,1},h^{1,1})=(1,101)$, we scan 200 flux configurations and find unstable extrema in $96\%$ of the cases, with $c'=0.18$. Once the Kähler runaway is taken into account, the no-scale structure fixes the logarithmic slope of the potential at every critical point to the universal value $\sqrt6$, so that the first RdSC condition is satisfied throughout. Statistically, the complex structure is the heavier sector and the axio-dilaton dominates the unstable directions. For the TCC we obtain analytic results valid for the whole class of one-parameter models: the slope approaches the universal value $2$ at large complex structure, diverges at the conifold, and equals exactly $\sqrt2$ in the axio-dilaton directions, where the leading potential takes a single $SL(2)$-covariant form, as the multifield evolutions confirm numerically. The TCC further translates into a lower bound on the compactification volume, $v>v_0\sim e^{N}$, controlled by the number of e-folds. Finally, the field displacements along our trajectories are compatible with the species-scale field-range bound, and restoring the volume dependence of $Λ_s$ shows that $V\lesssim3Λ_s^2$ is implied by the TCC volume bound, which is thus the genuine constraint on these models.

</details>


### [84] [The Phenomenology of the Dark Dimension and Non-Supersymmetric Strings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04909)
*Cumrun Vafa*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reviews the 'dark dimension' scenario—a micron-sized extra dimension motivated by Swampland principles and the small observed value of dark energy Λ—linking Λ to particle physics scales (neutrino masses, QCD axion decay constant, electroweak hierarchy, GUT scale) via 5D gravity and bulk symmetries; it proposes evolving dark matter mass as an alternative to phantom dark energy, fits DESI/CMB/SNe data without crossing w = −1, and connects this to recent progress on stable non-supersymmetric string vacua.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the observed tiny value of dark energy Λ with quantum gravity constraints (Swampland conjectures), while simultaneously explaining multiple hierarchies and anomalies in particle physics and cosmology—particularly avoiding fine-tuning and phantom behavior in dark energy evolution.

Method: Theoretical construction and phenomenological analysis: (i) deriving dimensional reduction and Kaluza–Klein spectra in a 5D effective theory with a micron-scale extra dimension; (ii) imposing bulk B−L gauge symmetry and Swampland-motivated moduli stabilization; (iii) computing cosmological observables (w(z), DM mass evolution) and fitting to DESI DR2 + CMB + supernovae data; (iv) connecting moduli dynamics to structure of non-SUSY string vacua via duality-symmetric critical points in non-geometric and Scherk–Schwarz compactifications.

Result: Predicts testable relationships: m_ν ∝ Λ^{1/4}, f_a ∼ Λ^{1/12} ∼ 10^9–10^{10} GeV, M_EW ∝ Λ^{1/6}, M_GUT ∼ √(M_5 M_EW); fits DESI+data with evolving DM mass instead of phantom w(z); identifies dark energy field near an unstable critical point in moduli space; supports existence of metastable non-supersymmetric string vacua protected by duality symmetries.

Conclusion: The dark dimension provides a unified, Swampland-consistent framework linking dark energy, dark matter, neutrino masses, axions, and hierarchy problems—and its cosmological signature (mass-varying DM) is favored over w_0w_aCDM by current data; this motivates deeper study of non-SUSY string compactifications with protected moduli critical points.

Abstract: Swampland principles, combined with the smallness of dark energy $Λ$, lead to the dark dimension scenario, with one extra dimension of micron size. We review this scenario and its predictions: KK graviton dark matter (or the KK tower of the quintessence field into which the gravitons convert), neutrino masses tied to $Λ^{1/4}$ via a bulk $B-L$ gauge symmetry, QCD axion decay constant $f_a\sim Λ^{1/12}\sim 10^9-10^{10}$ GeV at the 5d Planck scale $M_5$, connecting electroweak hierarchy to $Λ^{1/6}$, and a relation between the GUT and weak scales with $M_5$ as the geometric mean. The dark dimension also provides a concrete realization of an evolving dark sector: the evolution of its diameter correlates the variation of dark energy with that of the dark matter mass, fitting the recent DESI DR2 (+CMB+supernovae) data while avoiding the phantom crossing that the $w_0w_a$CDM parametrization would suggest---the apparent phantom behavior instead arises from the evolving dark matter mass. The fits suggest that the dark energy field sits in the interior of moduli space, near an unstable critical point. Motivated by this, we review recent progress on non-supersymmetric string vacua: isolated critical points protected by duality symmetries, in non-geometric flux compactifications and in Scherk--Schwarz compactifications of M-theory, and an emerging duality web for non-supersymmetric strings. This note is based on two talks given by the author at String Phenomenology 2026 (Paris) and Strings 2026 (Shanghai).

</details>


### [85] [$BF$ effective action topological implications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04962)
*Erika Amador-Ramos,Aldo Martínez-Merino,Roberto Santos-Silva*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the dimensional reduction of a 7D topological BF theory to 4D, analyzes its topological structure and field equations, and obtains exact solutions in Minkowski and Schwarzschild spacetimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how topological structures and dynamics of higher-dimensional BF theories manifest after dimensional reduction to physically relevant 4D spacetimes, particularly regarding solvability and topological invariance.

Method: Dimensional reduction of a 7-dimensional topological BF theory to four dimensions; analysis of the resulting effective action's topological properties; derivation and solution of the associated field equations in flat (Minkowski) and curved (Schwarzschild) backgrounds.

Result: Exact analytical solutions to the reduced field equations are obtained in both Minkowski and Schwarzschild spacetimes; the topological nature of the reduced theory is characterized.

Conclusion: The 4D effective theory retains key topological features of the original 7D BF theory, and its equations of motion admit exact solutions in physically significant spacetimes, suggesting potential relevance for topological gravity or quantum field theory in curved space.

Abstract: We investigate the effective action of a 7-dimensional topological $BF$ theory under a 4-dimensional reduction. We discuss the topological implications of the resulting theory and analyze the corresponding field equations. In particular, we derive exact solutions to these equations of motion in both Minkowski and Schwarzschild spacetimes.

</details>


### [86] [From Critical Zero Modes to Interior Dynamics in Holographic Superconductors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05208)
*Yan Liu,Hong-Da Lyu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates how critical behavior in holographic superconductors influences the interior geometry of charged black holes, revealing nonuniform critical limits due to logarithmic oscillations of the zero mode near the Cauchy horizon; it establishes scaling laws for Einstein–Rosen collapse width and Josephson phase, links them to Kasner oscillations and scalar velocity zeros, and distinguishes near-critical (zero-mode transmission) from low-temperature (IR endpoint) regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum critical phenomena in holographic superconductors affect black hole interiors—particularly the breakdown of uniformity at criticality and the emergence of universal scaling in interior dynamics near the Cauchy horizon.

Method: Analytical perturbative analysis of the critical zero mode near the Reissner–Nordström Cauchy horizon; matching of interior asymptotics to determine Einstein–Rosen collapse width Δz_ER and Josephson phase Θ_J; derivation of scaling relations based on condensate critical exponent r; validation via fully nonlinear numerical solutions for ordinary and tricritical holographic superconductors.

Result: The critical zero mode induces logarithmic oscillations near the Cauchy horizon, causing a nonuniform interior critical limit; Δz_ER ∝ t^{1/r} and Θ_J ∝ t^{-1/r} where t = 1 − T/T_c; Θ_J imprints oscillatory Kasner exponents and yields t_n ∼ n^{-r} for zeros of scalar velocity; coefficients are quantitatively confirmed numerically; low-T interior is governed by IR completion, not critical-mode transmission.

Conclusion: Near-critical black hole interiors are universally controlled by transmission of the holographic superconductor’s critical zero mode, dictating both geometric collapse and chaotic Kasner oscillations; however, this universality breaks down at sufficiently low temperatures, where the infrared physics of the zero-temperature geometry takes over.

Abstract: We study how the critical behavior of holographic superconductors controls the interior geometry of charged black holes. Although the exterior condensate vanishes at criticality, the critical zero mode develops logarithmic oscillations near the Reissner--Nordström Cauchy horizon, rendering the interior critical limit nonuniform. We show that the transmitted critical-mode amplitude, together with local Cauchy-horizon quantities, fixes both the leading Einstein--Rosen collapse $Δz_{\rm ER}$ width and the phase $Θ_J$ in the subsequent Josephson regime. If the condensate obeys $t\equiv1-T/T_c\proptoε^{2r}$, then $Δz_{\rm ER}\propto t^{1/r}$ and $Θ_J\propto t^{-1/r}$. This Josephson phase is transferred to the first Kasner epoch, producing oscillatory Kasner exponents and an accumulation law $t_n\sim n^{-r}$ for successive zeros of the scalar velocity. The critical zero mode fixes the leading collapse and phase coefficients, which are quantitatively confirmed by fully nonlinear solutions in both the ordinary and tricritical cases. At low temperature, the interior instead depends on the infrared completion of the zero-temperature geometry. Thus near-critical scaling is governed by critical-mode transmission, whereas the low-temperature behavior is controlled by the infrared endpoint.

</details>


### [87] [Inflationary magnetogenesis in Proca electrodynamics with Schwinger particle production](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05406)
*Mattia Dubbini,Orlando Luongo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies inflationary magnetogenesis with massive vector fields, incorporating Schwinger pair production of charged scalars and its backreaction—specifically on the background electric energy density—while treating magnetic and longitudinal modes quantum-mechanically; it finds that Schwinger production strongly suppresses electric energy density late in inflation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently incorporate Schwinger pair production and its backreaction into inflationary magnetogenesis with massive vector fields, resolving ambiguities in how quantum and classical electromagnetic components interact during inflation.

Method: Quantization of transverse and longitudinal vector field modes; computation of energy-momentum tensor; separation of classical background electric field (for Schwinger calculation) from quantum magnetic/longitudinal fields; use of semiclassical approximation where Schwinger backreaction modifies only the classical electric energy density; consistency check via vanishing coupling limit.

Result: Longitudinal energy density is subdominant to electric background; electric energy dominates magnetic contribution; Schwinger backreaction selectively depletes the electric energy density without affecting magnetic or longitudinal quantum sectors directly; for non-zero coupling, electric energy density is drastically reduced in late inflation.

Conclusion: A consistent semiclassical treatment—where only the electric background is classical and subject to Schwinger backreaction—yields physically viable magnetogenesis; Schwinger effect acts as a crucial regulator of electric energy density, preventing overproduction and enhancing viability of inflationary magnetic field generation.

Abstract: We investigate inflationary magnetogenesis with massive vector fields, including charged scalar particle production via the Schwinger effect and its backreaction on the background electric energy density. Initially neglecting the Schwinger effect, we quantize the vector field and solve the equations of motion for transverse and longitudinal modes, computing the energy momentum tensor. We then incorporate the backreaction of the electric energy density and show that the longitudinal energy density is subleading compared to the electric background. Since the electric energy density dominates the magnetic contribution, we can treat the former separately from the perturbative contributions. We adopt this approach when studying particle production via the Schwinger effect, assuming that its backreaction acts only on the background electric energy density. The latter is computed using a classical background electromagnetic field minimally coupled to a quantum complex scalar field. The magnetic and longitudinal components are instead described through quantum fields. In this framework, the Schwinger production affects only the electric energy density, as long as it remains dominant compared to the other components. As a consistency check, we verify that for a null coupling constant we recover the same electric energy density as obtained within the fully quantum electromagnetic description. Finally, we show that for non-null coupling constants Schwinger particle production drastically reduces the electric energy density during the final stages of inflation.

</details>


### [88] [Extremal black hole throats from non extremal near horizon expansions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05460)
*Anirudhda Shinde,Mangesh Mandlik*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper presents a systematic method to recover extremal near-horizon geometries from non-extremal black hole families via a controlled near-extremal scaling limit in Gaussian null coordinates, showing that second-order metric expansions are generally sufficient and necessary when the quadratic radial term survives.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified, coordinate-invariant framework for deriving extremal near-horizon geometries as smooth limits of non-extremal stationary black holes—bridging the conceptual and technical gap between extremal and non-extremal regimes.

Method: Applies simultaneous scalings: (i) near-extremal surface gravity scaling κ → εκ̃, (ii) radial coordinate rescaling r → εr̃, and (iii) Killing time rescaling t → t̃/ε; works in Gaussian null coordinates common to the black hole family; extracts near-horizon data via pullback of the scaled metric up to second order in r̃.

Result: Demonstrates that the extremal near-horizon geometry emerges as the ε → 0 limit of the scaled metric; shows the quadratic radial term in gₜₜ survives generically, justifying second-order expansion sufficiency and necessity; successfully reproduces known near-horizon geometries for RN, Kerr, Kerr-Newman, charged rotating BTZ, and identifies neutral rotating BTZ as requiring only first-order (Carroll) expansion.

Conclusion: A universal near-extremal scaling protocol in Gaussian null coordinates yields extremal near-horizon geometries as smooth limits; the required metric expansion order is determined by the leading nonvanishing radial coefficient of gₜₜ, typically second order—establishing a geometrically robust and computationally efficient prescription applicable across broad black hole families.

Abstract: We show how extremal near horizon geometries can be recovered from the non extremal near horizon expansion for smooth stationary black hole families admitting a smooth extremal limit. In Gaussian null coordinates common to the family, we apply the near extremal scaling of the surface gravity together with a radial scaling and a rescaling of Killing time. Although the physical surface gravity vanishes, the scaled horizon can retain a finite surface gravity. The extremal near horizon geometry is obtained when this scaled surface gravity is taken to zero. Under the near extremal scaling and the time rescaling, the quadratic radial term in the temporal metric component can survive, while higher radial orders vanish. Expansion through second order is therefore sufficient and is necessary whenever the quadratic term has a nonzero limiting coefficient in the chosen chart. We derive a common pullback prescription for extracting the near horizon coefficients and recover the known throats of Reissner-Nordström, Kerr, Kerr-Newman, and charged rotating Bañados-Teitelboim-Zanelli (BTZ) black holes. Neutral rotating BTZ provides a case in which the string Carroll expansion already suffices.

</details>


### [89] [Momentum Flux for Small-Amplitude Oscillons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06224)
*N. S. Manton,Azadeh Mohammadi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the momentum flux of oscillons—localized, nearly periodic solutions in 1+1D scalar field theory—and shows it is extremely small (O(ε^{N+4}) or O(ε^{N+5})) for small-amplitude expansions truncated at order ε^N.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the near-conservation of momentum in oscillons—quasi-stable, radiating structures distinct from exact breathers—and understand their longevity and radiation properties.

Method: Perturbative expansion in small amplitude ε; computation of time-averaged momentum flux over one oscillation period using truncated series solutions up to order ε^N.

Result: Integrated momentum flux per period is O(ε^{N+4}) for even N, O(ε^{N+5}) for odd N ≠ 1, and O(ε^4) for N = 1—demonstrating exponential suppression with increasing truncation order.

Conclusion: Oscillons exhibit extremely weak net momentum emission over a period, explaining their long lifetimes and justifying their approximate breather-like behavior despite slow radiation.

Abstract: An oscillon in scalar field theory in $(1+1)$-dimensions is an oscillating, localised, almost periodic classical solution that slowly radiates and decays to the vacuum solution. We calculate its momentum flux integrated over a period, which would be zero for an exact breather. For an oscillon of small amplitude $\eps$, truncated at order $\eps^N$, we show that the integrated momentum flux is of leading order $\eps^{N+4}$ for even $N$ and $\eps^{N+5}$ for odd $N$, except for $N=1$, where it is of order $\eps^4$. It is therefore very small.

</details>


### [90] [More on the Celestial Soft Symmetry Algebra in the ${\cal N}=8$ Supergravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05760)
*Changhyun Ahn*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs OPEs of N=8 w_{1+∞} and w̄_{1+∞} currents—derived via antiholomorphic/holomorphic light transforms—with matter fields in N=8 supergravity, recovers the celestial soft symmetry algebras (both sectors), identifies the N=8 extended BMS algebra as a subalgebra, derives associated soft theorems for gravitons, gravitinos, and graviphotons, and uses shadow transforms to identify stress-energy tensor, supercurrents, and supertranslation generators within shadow w_{1+∞}/w̄_{1+∞} currents.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically derive and unify the celestial soft symmetry algebras in N=8 supergravity, linking them to extended BMS symmetries and soft theorems via light-transformed and shadow-transformed w_{1+∞} currents.

Method: Constructs operator product expansions (OPEs) of N=8 w_{1+∞} and w̄_{1+∞} currents (obtained from antiholomorphic/holomorphic light transforms) with matter fields; computes double and triple (anti)commutators via contour integrals to verify Jacobi identities; applies shadow transform (composition of holomorphic and antiholomorphic light transforms) to obtain shadow currents and identify symmetry generators.

Result: Reproduces known N=8 celestial soft symmetry algebras in both antiholomorphic and holomorphic sectors; reveals N=8 extended BMS algebra as a subalgebra; derives nontrivial soft theorems for graviton, gravitinos, and graviphotons; identifies stress-energy tensor, supercurrents, and supertranslation generator within shadow w_{1+∞}/w̄_{1+∞} currents.

Conclusion: The N=8 celestial symmetry algebra is fully encoded in w_{1+∞}-type currents—light-transformed and shadow-transformed—and their OPEs with matter fields provide a unified framework for soft theorems and asymptotic symmetries in N=8 supergravity.

Abstract: We construct the operator product expansions (OPEs) of the ${\cal N}=8$ ${\rm w}_{1+\infty}$ currents (obtained from the antiholomorphic light transform) with the matter fields in the ${\cal N}=8$ supergravity. The corresponding (anti)commutators are obtained from the double contour integrals using the above OPEs. By computing the triple (anti)commutators consisting of current-current-matter (i.e., the Jacobi identity for fixed last element), the previously known ${\cal N}=8$ celestial soft symmetry algebra between these currents (in the antiholomorphic sector) is reproduced. We repeat the above procedure by taking the holomorphic light transform and the OPEs of the ${\cal N}=8$ ${\rm \bar{w}}_{1+\infty}$ currents with the matter fields and the corresponding (anti)commutators are obtained. We also obtain another type of ${\cal N}=8$ celestial soft symmetry algebra between these currents (in the holomorphic sector). As a subalgebra in the antiholomorphic and holomorphic sectors, there exists the ${\cal N}=8$ extended BMS (Bondi, van der Burg, Metzner, and Sachs in 1962) algebra. We analyze the above results from the ${\cal N}=8$ conformally {\it soft} ${\rm w}_{1+\infty}$ (or ${\rm \bar{w}}_{1+\infty}$) currents in the antiholomorphic (or holomorphic) sector. The nontrivial soft theorems for the graviton, the gravitinos and the graviphotons are described. Finally, using the shadow transform (the consecutive composition of holomorphic and antiholomorphic light transforms), we obtain the OPEs of the ${\cal N}=8$ {\it shadow} ${\rm w}_{1+\infty}$ (or ${\rm \bar{w}}_{1+\infty}$) currents with the matter fields where the stress-energy tensor, the supercurrents and the supertranslation generator of the ${\cal N}=8$ extended BMS algebra are identified.

</details>


### [91] [Einstein--Maxwell Sum Rules and the Persistence of the Smooth-Brane No-Go Theorem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05798)
*Paulo Michel Longo Tavares da Silva*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper examines whether adding a Maxwell field to a 5D warped braneworld with a scalar field can circumvent the Gibbons–Kallosh–Linde (GKL) no-go theorem against smooth branes; it finds that under standard assumptions—including exact 4D Poincaré invariance—the Maxwell field must vanish in the background, restoring the original obstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether minimally coupling an Abelian Maxwell field to a bulk scalar field in five-dimensional warped braneworlds can relax or evade the Gibbons–Kallosh–Linde (GKL) no-go theorem, which forbids smooth, localized branes in certain 5D setups.

Method: The authors derive global consistency conditions for a canonical scalar field coupled to a bulk Maxwell field in 5D, explicitly separating tangential (F_{μν}) and mixed (F_{μ5}) field strength components—crucial because the Maxwell stress tensor is not traceless in 5D—and analyze implications for background symmetry and localization.

Result: Electromagnetic contributions formally modify integrated GKL constraints, but cannot consistently evade the no-go theorem under standard assumptions; exact 4D Poincaré invariance forces the background Maxwell field to vanish, recovering the original smooth-brane obstruction.

Conclusion: A minimally coupled Maxwell sector does not resolve the GKL obstruction to smooth 5D warped braneworlds unless key assumptions—such as exact 4D Poincaré invariance or classical determinism of the Maxwell field—are relaxed; the vanishing of the background Maxwell field is necessary for symmetry, distinguishing global background constraints from local gauge-field localization conditions.

Abstract: We investigate whether a minimally coupled Abelian Maxwell sector can relax the Gibbons--Kallosh--Linde (GKL) obstruction to smooth five-dimensional warped braneworlds. For a canonical scalar field coupled to a bulk Maxwell field, we derive the one-parameter family of global consistency conditions while keeping the tangential F_{μν} and mixed F_{μ5} sectors separate. This decomposition is essential because the Maxwell stress tensor is not traceless in five dimensions. We show that electromagnetic terms formally modify the integrated GKL constraints, but this modification does not provide a consistent evasion of the no-go theorem under the standard assumptions. Exact four-dimensional Poincaré invariance excludes a nonzero deterministic classical Maxwell field from the background, since its field strength would select preferred four-dimensional directions. The Maxwell sector therefore vanishes in the symmetric background and the usual smooth-brane obstruction is recovered. We also identify which assumptions must be relaxed for a nontrivial electromagnetic contribution and distinguish this background result from local consistency conditions for bulk gauge-field localization.

</details>


### [92] [Global Consistency of Cosmological Braneworlds in Palatini and Metric-Affine f(R) Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05821)
*Paulo Michel L. T da Silva*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives global consistency conditions for 5D warped braneworlds in Palatini and metric-affine gravity, extending the Gibbons-Kallosh-Linde framework to obtain integrated sum rules; it identifies how non-Riemannian (independent connection) degrees of freedom relax the standard no-go theorem for smooth compact braneworlds in Einstein gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the Einstein-gravity no-go result prohibiting smooth, compact five-dimensional warped braneworlds by exploring gravitational theories with independent affine connections (Palatini and metric-affine gravity).

Method: Generalization of the Gibbons-Kallosh-Linde framework to Palatini and metric-affine gravity; derivation of integrated sum rules for warped cosmological geometries; decomposition of contributions into geometric, matter, and non-Riemannian parts; analysis of global consistency via topological and averaged constraints.

Result: New necessary global consistency conditions (sum rules) for smooth 5D warped braneworlds; explicit identification of non-Riemannian terms that modify or evade the standard obstruction; clarification of admissible configurations in theories with independent affine structure.

Conclusion: The standard no-go result is not absolute but depends on the gravitational theory’s geometric structure; Palatini and metric-affine extensions admit consistent smooth compact braneworlds under specific global constraints encoded in the derived sum rules, providing a systematic framework for viability assessments beyond Riemannian gravity.

Abstract: We investigate global consistency conditions for five-dimensional warped braneworlds in Palatini and metric-affine gravity. Starting from the generalized Gibbons-Kallosh-Linde framework, we derive integrated sum rules for cosmological warped geometries and examine how independent connection degrees of freedom modify the standard Einstein-gravity obstruction to smooth compact braneworlds. The analysis separates geometric, matter, and non-Riemannian contributions and clarifies the conditions under which the usual no-go result can be modified. We show that the generalized consistency relations provide necessary global constraints on admissible configurations and identify the terms responsible for possible departures from the Riemannian case. The results establish a systematic framework for testing the global consistency of smooth five-dimensional braneworlds in gravitational theories with independent affine structure.

</details>


### [93] [Black Hole Entropy and Holographic Entanglement Entropy in DGP Brane Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05946)
*Takamasa Kanai*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes conditions under which gravitational entropy of braneworld black holes matches holographic entanglement entropy, showing agreement holds for static configurations in DGP, Lovelock, and f(R) brane gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the relation between gravitational entropy and holographic entanglement entropy in braneworld gravity, particularly where gravitational dynamics are localized on the brane.

Method: Analytical derivation of entropy-matching conditions using brane-world effective gravity: first in the Dvali-Gabadadze-Porrati (DGP) model with induced Einstein-Hilbert term, then extended to higher-curvature theories (Lovelock and f(R) gravity) on the brane; focus on extrinsic curvature constraints and static vs. stationary configurations.

Result: Gravitational entropy equals holographic entanglement entropy iff a specific condition involving the brane’s extrinsic curvature is satisfied; this condition holds automatically for static configurations across all considered theories (DGP, Lovelock, f(R)).

Conclusion: The equality of gravitational and holographic entanglement entropies in braneworld gravity is generically tied to staticity—ensuring consistency of holography in such settings—and the matching condition is robust across a broad class of brane-localized gravitational theories.

Abstract: We investigate the relation between gravitational entropy and holographic entanglement entropy in braneworld gravity with gravitational dynamics localized on the brane. We first consider the DGP brane model, in which an Einstein-Hilbert term is included in the brane action, and derive the condition under which the gravitational entropy of a braneworld black hole agrees with the holographic entanglement entropy. We show that the two entropies coincide when a specific condition involving the extrinsic curvature of the brane is satisfied. In particular, this condition is automatically satisfied for static configurations.
  We then generalize the brane-localized Einstein-Hilbert term to higher-curvature gravitational theories. For Lovelock gravity and $f(R)$ gravity on the brane, we derive the corresponding conditions for the equality between gravitational entropy and holographic entanglement entropy and show that they take the same form as that obtained for Einstein gravity. Although the equality is not guaranteed for general stationary configurations, the required condition is satisfied for static braneworld black holes, leading to an agreement between the two entropies.
  These results clarify the relation between gravitational entropy and holographic entanglement entropy in braneworld gravity and contribute to a deeper understanding of holography in braneworld settings.

</details>


### [94] [IR flow of stochastic power spectra for interacting spectator scalars in the inflationary de Sitter spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05957)
*Vishal Nath,Sourav Bhattacharya*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies infrared (IR) flow effects on early-universe spectator scalar field correlation functions during inflation, computing one-loop effective potentials in de Sitter spacetime using both Schwinger-DeWitt and exact propagators, then feeding them into the Wetterich equation to analyze symmetry restoration, stochastic power spectra, and spectral index running—especially highlighting fermionic contributions in Yukawa coupling and large-N insensitivity in O(N) models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how IR quantum fluctuations during inflation modify effective dynamics and correlation functions of spectator scalar fields, particularly whether and how symmetry breaking is restored at long wavelengths, and how different regularization schemes (Schwinger-DeWitt vs. exact de Sitter propagators) affect physical predictions.

Method: Computed one-loop effective potentials for φ⁴, φ⁴-Yukawa, and broken-phase O(N) models in de Sitter spacetime using two propagator methods: (i) local Schwinger-DeWitt expansion (valid sub-Hubble), and (ii) exact de Sitter propagator; used these as UV boundary conditions in the Wetterich functional renormalization group (FRG) equation to derive IR flow; computed stochastic two-point correlation functions, power spectra, and spectral indices from IR-improved potentials.

Result: IR flow drives spontaneous symmetry breaking toward restoration; spectral index nₛ increases in deep IR for φ⁴ and φ⁴-Yukawa models; fermionic interactions significantly enhance this effect; for O(N), effective potential flattens under RG flow as N grows large, reducing flow sensitivity—leading to suppressed IR modification of correlations.

Conclusion: The choice of propagator affects quantitative predictions but preserves qualitative IR behavior (e.g., spectral tilt); fermions amplify IR-induced spectral index growth; large-N limit suppresses RG evolution of the potential, implying robustness of correlation structure in strongly symmetric scalar sectors during inflation.

Abstract: In this work we investigate the behaviour of early inflationary cosmic correlation functions under the flow of the infrared scale for interacting spectator scalar field theories. We start with the derivation of the one-loop effective potentials for $φ^4$, $φ^4$-Yukawa and broken phase $O(N)$ model in the inflationary de Sitter spacetime, first using the local Schwinger-DeWitt expansion propagator, and next using the exact de Sitter propagator. The Schwinger-DeWitt expansion being valid in a sub-Hubble length scale, furnishes suitable framework to determine the leading curvature corrections to high energy phenomena having sub-Hubble Compton wavelengths. These effective potentials show spontaneous symmetry breaking pattern, and are next used as ultraviolet boundary conditions in the Wetterich equation generating the desired IR flow. The flow is shown to drive the theory towards symmetry restoration. Using these IR-improved effective potentials, we next compute the stochastic power spectra and spectral indices for the temporally coincident two point correlation function of the spectator scalar. We show that the results corresponding to the aforementioned two kind of propagators are roughly in qualitative agreement with each other, but may differ quantitatively significantly. Both of them show increase in the spectral index in the deep IR for $φ^4$ and $φ^4$-Yukawa. The effect of fermionic interaction has been emphasised. Also for the case of the $O(N)$ scalar, we demonstrate that the effective potential's shape becomes more and more insensitive to the flow as $N$ gets large. Implication of this on the correlation function has been discussed.

</details>


### [95] [Nonlocal Dirac theory in curved spacetime and the Bunch--Davies vacuum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06390)
*Paulo José Porfírio,Ramires Nascimento da Silva*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper formulates a nonlocal spin-1/2 quantum field theory in curved spacetime using an entire nonpolynomial form factor of the covariant Dirac operator; derives the corresponding nonlocal Dirac equation coupled to gravity and U(1) gauge fields; applies it to de Sitter spacetime, constructs nonlocal Bunch–Davies modes; maps nonlocal dynamics onto local Dirac equations with effective masses $M_n$ given by Lambert-$W$ branches; preserves Euclidean analyticity and key quantum features (e.g., no supercurvature modes), while yielding explicit nonlocal corrections to Bogoliubov coefficients, squeezing, particle production, and entanglement entropy; recovers the local theory as the nonlocal scale $\Lambda \to \infty$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend quantum field theory in curved spacetime beyond locality—addressing UV incompleteness and potential trans-Planckian issues—by incorporating well-behaved (entire, nonpolynomial) nonlocality via form factors of the Dirac operator, while preserving fundamental properties like causality, unitarity proxies (via Euclidean analyticity), and consistency with known QFT structures (e.g., Bunch–Davies vacuum).

Method: Constructs a nonlocal action for a spin-1/2 field using an entire function $f(\slashed{D}^2/\Lambda^2)$ of the squared covariant Dirac operator $\slashed{D}^2$, where $\Lambda$ sets the nonlocal scale; derives the associated nonlocal Dirac equation variationally; couples minimally to gravity and U(1) gauge fields; specializes to open de Sitter spacetime, solves the nonlocal mode equation, defines nonlocal Bunch–Davies modes via Euclidean continuation; performs spectral mapping to local Dirac equations with discrete effective masses $M_n = \Lambda W_n(e^{\alpha})$, where $W_n$ are Lambert-$W$ branches; computes Bogoliubov transformations, squeezing parameter, occupation number, and entanglement entropy from mode solutions.

Result: Nonlocal Dirac equation is consistently formulated in curved spacetime and coupled to gravity and U(1); nonlocal Bunch–Davies modes exist and preserve Euclidean analyticity; physical observables acquire calculable nonlocal corrections: Bogoliubov coefficients depend on $W_0$, squeezing amplitude increases, particle production and entanglement entropy are enhanced at early times but suppressed at late times relative to local case; no physical fermionic supercurvature modes appear (as in local theory); all results reduce smoothly to standard local QFT as $\Lambda \to \infty$.

Conclusion: Entire-function nonlocality based on the Dirac operator provides a consistent, analytically tractable framework for nonlocal QFT in curved spacetime; it preserves essential structural features (analyticity, absence of unphysical modes) while generating testable, scale-dependent quantum gravitational corrections to particle creation and entanglement in inflationary cosmology.

Abstract: We formulate a nonlocal spin-$1/2$ field theory in curved spacetime in which the form factor is a nonpolynomial entire function of the covariant Dirac operator. We derive the corresponding nonlocal Dirac equation and analyze its coupling to gravity and to a $\rm U(1)$ gauge field. As an application, we study the theory in the open chart of de Sitter spacetime and construct the associated nonlocal Bunch--Davies modes. The nonlocal dynamics can be mapped, branch by branch, onto the local Dirac equation with an effective mass $M_n$. For the exponential form factors considered here, the effective masses are expressed in terms of branches of the Lambert-$W$ function. We show that the Euclidean analyticity structure of the Bunch--Davies construction is preserved. On the physical positive-residue branch, the Bogoliubov coefficients, squeezing parameter, occupation number, and entanglement entropy acquire explicit nonlocal corrections, while the absence of physical fermionic supercurvature modes is preserved. The local theory is smoothly recovered in the limit $Λ\to\infty$.

</details>


### [96] [A local second law for black hole dynamics in the charged large-$D$ membrane paradigm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06470)
*Supratim Halder,Mangesh Mandlik*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes the local second law of thermodynamics for charged black holes in the large-D membrane paradigm by computing subleading corrections to membrane velocity divergence, linking horizon physics to membrane kinematics, and constructing a non-negative-divergence entropy current.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The local second law of thermodynamics has been established for neutral membranes in the large-D limit, but its extension to charged black holes—specifically, the explicit derivation of the local entropy production equation—remains an open problem.

Method: The authors compute the subleading-order correction to the divergence of the membrane velocity for a charged black hole in flat spacetime; they evaluate the scalar Einstein-Maxwell constraint at the physical event horizon using the non-affine null Raychaudhuri equation, then map horizon quantities to auxiliary membrane variables; finally, they construct a membrane entropy current and analyze its divergence.

Result: The membrane velocity divergence is shown to be non-negative at the first non-vanishing order in the membrane worldvolume expansion, driven by quadratic contributions from membrane shear and a novel Maxwell-field-induced term; consequently, the constructed entropy current has non-negative divergence, proving the local second law for the charged large-D membrane paradigm.

Conclusion: The local second law of thermodynamics holds for charged black holes in the large-D membrane framework, with entropy production governed by both hydrodynamic shear and electromagnetic field effects—resolving a key gap in the charged large-D effective theory.

Abstract: The large-$D$ membrane paradigm provides a perturbative, non-gravitational description of black hole dynamics. While the local second law of thermodynamics has been established for neutral membranes, the explicit determination of the local entropy production equation within the charged large-$D$ membrane framework remains unsolved. In this paper, we compute the subleading-order correction to the divergence of the membrane velocity for a charged black hole in a flat background. We evaluate the corresponding scalar Einstein-Maxwell constraint equation at the physical event horizon via the non-affine null Raychaudhuri equation, and relate the physical horizon expressions to the auxiliary membrane kinematics, demonstrating that the membrane velocity produces a non-negative divergence at the first non-vanishing order in the membrane worldvolume, which is driven by two quadratic forms: the membrane shear and a novel contribution generated purely by the Maxwell field. Using this result, we construct the membrane entropy current and prove that its divergence is non-negative through this order, thereby establishing the local second law for the charged large-$D$ membrane paradigm.

</details>


### [97] [Quantum Gravity in the Landau background gauge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06564)
*J. Frenkel,S. Martins-Filho*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the BRST-invariant effective action for quantum gravity coupled to matter in the Landau background gauge, computes one-loop off-shell and on-shell counterterms, and suggests finiteness of the matter self-energy beyond one loop.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand quantum consistency and renormalizability of gravity-matter systems by analyzing BRST symmetry and Ward identities in a well-defined gauge (Landau background gauge).

Method: Uses background field method in Landau gauge; computes one-loop quantum corrections; analyzes BRST invariance and derives Ward identities for the background-effective action; explicitly constructs off-shell and on-shell counterterm Lagrangians.

Result: Explicit one-loop off-shell and on-shell counterterms are obtained; matter self-energy is shown finite at one loop; evidence suggests possible all-order finiteness of this quantity in the same gauge.

Conclusion: The Landau background gauge preserves sufficient symmetry (via BRST and Ward identities) to control divergences; finiteness of the matter self-energy at one loop hints at improved ultraviolet behavior, potentially supporting perturbative predictability in quantum gravity-matter systems.

Abstract: We examine, in the Landau background gauge, the effective action of a quantum theory of gravitational and matter fields. We analyse the BRST invariance of this theory and study the Ward identities which implement the symmetry of the background effective action. The off-shell and on-shell counterterm Lagrangians are explicitly calculated in this gauge at one-loop order. We argue that the matter self-energy in the Landau background gauge, which is finite to this order, may be finite also at higher loops.

</details>


### [98] [de Sitter Wavefunction from Quadrangular Polylogarithms: Polygon Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06772)
*Livia Ferro,Tomasz Lukowski,Shruti Paranjape,Lecheng Ren,Marcus Spradlin,Anastasia Volovich,He-Chen Weng,Yao-Qi Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Derives an explicit formula for n-gon contributions to the cosmological wavefunction in de Sitter space using B-type quadrangular polylogarithms tied to B_{2n−1} cluster algebra compatibility.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain an explicit, closed-form expression for n-gon graph contributions to the wavefunction of the universe in conformally coupled φ³ theory in de Sitter space — a problem where standard Feynman diagram techniques become unwieldy due to time-dependent backgrounds and boundary conditions.

Method: Leverages total compatibility of the symbol with the B_{2n−1} cluster algebra; introduces B-type quadrangular polylogarithms built from centrally symmetric quadrangulations of a 4n-gon; establishes correspondence between the differential recursion for wavefunction coefficients and the coproduct recursion for these polylogarithms; fixes integration constants via the soft limit.

Result: An explicit weight-n function expressed in terms of B-type quadrangular polylogarithms gives the exact n-gon wavefunction coefficient; the symbol of this coefficient is totally compatible with the B_{2n−1} cluster algebra.

Conclusion: The n-gon contribution to the wavefunction admits a precise, algebraically structured representation rooted in cluster algebras and generalized polylogarithms, revealing deep integrability and geometric structure in cosmological correlators in de Sitter space.

Abstract: We present an explicit formula for the $n$-gon graph contribution to the integrand of the cosmological wavefunction for conformally coupled $φ^3$ theory in de Sitter space. Our result relies on the fact that its symbol satisfies total compatibility with respect to the $B_{2n-1}$ cluster algebra. We introduce $B$-type quadrangular polylogarithms, constructed from centrally symmetric quadrangulations of a $4n$-gon, and show that the $n$-gon wavefunction coefficient is expressed by a natural weight-$n$ function of this class. We prove our formula by directly relating the recursive differential equation satisfied by these wavefunction coefficients to a recursive coproduct formula for $B$-type quadrangular polylogarithms, with the remaining additive constant fixed by the soft limit.

</details>


<div id='gr-qc'></div>

# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [99] [Signature Failure and Minimum Spatial Quanta: Shear-Induced Quantum Boundaries for Gravitational Path Integrals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03732)
*Edward J. Shaya*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether quantum metric fluctuations eliminate the smooth, Lorentzian spacetime structure, proposing instead a single, causally connected 4D geometry. Using a lattice model with graviton vacuum extrapolation and physical UV cutoff, it finds a minimal surviving scale q_min ≈ 1.9 ℓ_P; curvature-induced deformations yield finite quantum boundaries for black holes (r_qb ~ 10⁻²² m) and radiation-dominated universes (ρ_qb/ρ_P ~ 10⁻²), suggesting quantum replacements for classical singularities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the incompatibility between general relativity’s singularities and quantum mechanics by determining whether quantum metric fluctuations destroy differentiable Lorentzian spacetime—and if so, whether finite-curvature quantum boundaries can replace classical singular continuations.

Method: Extrapolates Gaussian transverse-traceless graviton vacuum to an additive metric ensemble with physical Fourier cutoff k_max = 1/q; employs a lattice model with local repair and selection of periodically winding differentiable-state components; analyzes two curvature-induced deformations—Schwarzschild electric Weyl field and homogeneous radiation—using linearized de Donder conditions and response coefficients C_W and C_R.

Result: A minimum surviving scale q_min ≃ 1.9 ℓ_P is found; for Schwarzschild geometry, quantum boundary radius r_qb ≃ 2.62 C_W^{1/3} (r_g ℓ_P²)^{1/3} ≃ 1.91×10⁻²² C_W^{1/3} (M/M_⊙)^{1/3} m at q ≃ 2.5 ℓ_P; for homogeneous radiation, quantum energy density ρ_qb/ρ_P ≃ 1.31×10⁻² / C_R at same q; both thresholds are model-dependent but finite and independent of expansion sign.

Conclusion: Classical spacetime singularities may be replaced by finite-curvature quantum boundaries arising from intrinsic metric fluctuations constrained to preserve causal connectivity and differentiability, supporting a fundamentally non-singular, four-dimensional quantum geometry.

Abstract: We investigate whether quantum metric fluctuations can destroy the differentiable Lorentzian support required for spacetime. We conjecture that non-Lorentzian metric elements are absent from the physical manifold and restrict admissible histories to a single continuing, causally connected four-dimensional geometry. To explore this proposal, we extrapolate the Gaussian transverse-traceless graviton vacuum to an additive metric ensemble with physical Fourier cutoff $k_{\max}=1/q$. A lattice model with local repair and selection of periodically winding differentiable-state components gives a minimum surviving scale $q_{\min}\simeq1.9\,\ell_{\rm P}$ over the simulated duration. We then examine two complementary curvature-induced deformations. For the Schwarzschild electric Weyl field, the smallest boundary radius at the center of a black hole, occurring when $q\simeq2.5\,\ell_{\rm P}$, is $r_{\rm qb}\simeq2.62\,C_W^{1/3}(r_g\ell_{\rm P}^2)^{1/3} \simeq1.91\times10^{-22}\, C_W^{1/3}\left(\rm M/\rm M_\odot\right)^{1/3}\mathrm{m}$, where $r_g=GM/c^2$ and $C_W$ parameterizes the finite-$q$ Weyl response. For homogeneous radiation, a supplementary Gaussian four-metric ensemble subject to linearized de Donder conditions gives $ρ_{\rm qb}/ρ_{\rm P}\simeq1.31\times10^{-2}/C_R$ at $q=2.5\,\ell_{\rm P}$, where $C_R$ is the corresponding Ricci-response coefficient. The local homogeneous criterion is unchanged under reversal of the expansion rate. These model-dependent thresholds motivate a possible replacement of classical singular continuations by finite-curvature quantum boundaries.

</details>


### [100] [Scalar decoupling and critical-curve invariance in a reduced MOG black-hole theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03751)
*Nikko John Leo S. Lobos,Emmanuel T. Rodulfo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes linear perturbations of a Schwarzschild-MOG black hole, showing scalar and gravito-vector sector decoupling via field redefinition; proves scalar stability for non-negative scalar mass squared; demonstrates vanishing scalar-induced critical-curve transfer (including monopole breathing) under conformal metric perturbations preserving the capture boundary; identifies exceptional axial dipole excitation inducing constrained metric perturbation; reconstructs it with horizon-regular gauge; derives charge-dependent low-frequency metric response vanishing as ω²; and distinguishes it from stationary rotation via Kerr–Newman benchmark yielding δC_b = −2a_rot in neutral limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether scalar excitations in Modified Gravity (MOG) can produce observable breathing-mode deformations of black hole photon rings or critical curves — a key test for distinguishing MOG from GR or other alternatives — and to isolate genuine vector (charge-related) effects from spurious rotational mimics.

Method: Uses linearized gravity in reduced strong-field MOG; applies field redefinition ĥ_μν = h_μν − ψ ḡ_μν to decouple sectors; solves Klein–Gordon (scalar) and Reissner–Nordström-type (gravito-vector) equations; imposes spectral boundary conditions for stability; analyzes conformal perturbations and their effect on null geodesic critical curves; constructs horizon-regular gauge solution via first-order radial integral; employs matched asymptotics for low-frequency metric response; benchmarks against infinitesimal Kerr–Newman solution for impact-parameter shift.

Result: Scalar-only perturbations yield conformal metric changes with null Hamiltonian contribution and vanish identically for all linear scalar-to-critical-curve transfer coefficients (including monopole breathing), provided the conformal factor is smooth, positive, and preserves the capture boundary; only the charged axial dipole induces a nontrivial, constrained metric perturbation; its reconstruction is horizon-regular and separates radiative amplitude from stationary angular momentum cleanly; the local metric response scales as ω² at fixed radius and subextremal background; the Kerr–Newman benchmark confirms δC_b = −2a_rot in the neutral limit, confirming distinguishability from rotation.

Conclusion: Linear scalar breathing of the angular critical curve is rigorously excluded in Schwarzschild-MOG under physically motivated boundary and regularity conditions; the low-frequency gravito-vector response is intrinsically dynamical (ω²-suppressed) and fundamentally distinct from stationary frame-dragging, enabling observational discrimination between MOG scalar modes and rotational effects in black hole shadows and photon ring dynamics.

Abstract: Linear perturbations of a Schwarzschild-MOG black hole separate into scalar and gravito-vector sectors in the reduced strong-field theory at leading order in $μ\bar G M\ll1$. The field redefinition $\widehat h_{μν}=h_{μν}-ψ\bar g_{μν}$ gives a decoupled Klein--Gordon equation for the scalar and the Reissner--Nordström equations for the gravito-vector sector. The scalar is stable under the stated spectral boundary conditions for $m_G^2\ge0$. A scalar-only excitation produces a conformal metric perturbation with null Hamiltonian contribution $H_1^{(s)}=-ψH_0$. For fixed observer worldlines and conformally corresponding orthonormal frames, every linear scalar-to-critical-curve transfer coefficient vanishes, including monopole breathing, provided the conformal factor is smooth, positive, and preserves the capture boundary. The exceptional charged axial dipole instead induces a constrained metric perturbation. A first-order radial integral reconstructs this perturbation in a gauge regular at the future horizon and on the sphere, separating the radiative amplitude from stationary angular momentum without division by frequency. For a unit-incident canonical vector amplitude with zero angular momentum, matched asymptotic solutions give an explicit charge-dependent local metric response that vanishes as $ω^2$ at fixed radius and fixed subextremal background. A separate infinitesimal Kerr--Newman benchmark yields the stationary impact-parameter midpoint shift and recovers $δC_b=-2a_{\rm rot}$ in the neutral limit. These results exclude linear scalar breathing of the specified angular critical curve and distinguish the low-frequency vector-induced metric response from a stationary rotation effect.

</details>


### [101] [On-shell Homogeneous Curvature Modes in Quadratic $F(R,\mathcal G)$ Cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03752)
*Francisco Tello-Ortiz,B. Mishra,Y. Gómez-Leyton,Jean Báez Cuevas*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes homogeneous cosmological perturbations in a quadratic gravity model $F(R,\mathcal{G}) = R + \\alpha R^2 + \\beta \\mathcal{G}^2$, deriving perturbation equations, identifying key background solutions (low-curvature dust, de Sitter, and big-rip), and assessing stability and asymptotic behavior of perturbations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the viability and dynamical behavior of higher-curvature gravity models—specifically quadratic $F(R,\\mathcal{G})$ theories—by studying linear homogeneous perturbations around physically relevant cosmological backgrounds.

Method: Linearizes the modified Friedmann equation and dust conservation law to obtain second-order Hubble perturbation and third-order matter contrast equations; performs asymptotic analysis on zeroth-order background solutions (dust, de Sitter, big-rip) within the same theory; examines mode stability and coupling-dependent corrections.

Result: Identifies three distinct vacuum or dust-dominated background branches: (i) low-curvature dust ($H \\sim 2/(3t)$); (ii) stable de Sitter solution at $\\beta H_{\\rm dS}^6 = 1/96$, requiring $\\alpha < -1/(6H_{\\rm dS}^2)$ for stability of curvature modes; (iii) high-curvature big-rip branch with $H \\sim 7/(t_s - t)$, hosting one divergent $\\mathcal{G}^2$-dominated perturbation mode. Einstein limit consistently recovers standard dust evolution.

Conclusion: The quadratic $F(R,\\mathcal{G})$ model admits rich cosmological dynamics but imposes strict constraints (e.g., on $\\alpha$) for stability; the presence of divergent modes in the big-rip regime signals potential pathological behavior, while the de Sitter branch offers a viable late-time attractor under parameter tuning.

Abstract: 5We study homogeneous cosmological perturbations in the quadratic model $F(R,\mathcal G)=R+αR^2+β\mathcal G^2$. Linearization of the modified Friedmann equation together with dust conservation yields a second-order equation for the homogeneous Hubble perturbation, or equivalently a third-order equation for the matter contrast. We then restrict the asymptotic analysis to backgrounds that satisfy the zeroth-order equations of the same quadratic theory. Besides the low-curvature dust branch, which approaches $H=2/(3t)$, the model admits a nontrivial vacuum de Sitter solution with $βH_{\rm dS}^6=1/96$. Its homogeneous curvature modes are stable only when $α<-1/(6H_{\rm dS}^2)$.
