At high curvature the background equation also admits a vacuum big-rip asymptotic branch beginning with $H=7/(t_s-t)$; the first corrections are fixed by the quadratic couplings. The corresponding $\mathcal G^2$-dominated perturbation has one divergent homogeneous mode. The simultaneous Einstein limit is taken only as a consistency check and recovers the standard dust behavior.

</details>


### [102] [Electromagnetic quasinormal modes of a Schwarzschild black hole in a superconducting plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03761)
*Bobur Turimov,Akbar Davlataliev,Bobomurat Ahmedov,Chengxun Yuan,Chen Zhou,Sulton Usanov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends London electrodynamics to curved spacetime, deriving covariant electromagnetic wave equations for superconducting media around Schwarzschild black holes; it shows how the London penetration depth induces an effective photon mass that modifies quasinormal modes and enables long-lived quasibound states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To model electromagnetic perturbations in superconducting media in strong gravitational fields—particularly near black holes—where standard flat-space London theory is inadequate and gauge covariance must be preserved.

Method: Formulates a generally covariant, gauge-invariant constitutive relation generalizing the London equation using the four-potential and condensate phase; couples it to covariant Maxwell equations; works in the London (unitary) gauge to obtain an effective Proca theory; derives decoupled (axial) and coupled (polar) Schrödinger-like wave equations in Schwarzschild geometry using Regge–Wheeler–Zerilli formalism; imposes quasinormal-mode boundary conditions.

Result: Axial perturbations obey a modified Regge–Wheeler equation with London-mass–dependent potential; polar sector exhibits two coupled degrees of freedom; London mass shifts QNM frequencies and damping rates; sufficiently large effective mass yields long-lived quasibound states.

Conclusion: The framework consistently unifies superconductivity, general relativity, and massive vector field dynamics, enabling analysis of electromagnetic relaxation, compact-object interiors with superconducting phases, and black-hole environments with effective massive photons.

Abstract: We formulate a generally covariant extension of London electrodynamics in four-dimensional curved spacetime and investigate the propagation of electromagnetic perturbations in a superconducting medium surrounding a black hole. The ordinary London relation is promoted to a gauge-invariant four-dimensional constitutive equation involving the electromagnetic four-potential and the phase of the superconducting condensate. When combined with the covariant Maxwell equations, the resulting system is equivalent, in the London (unitary) gauge, to a Proca theory with an effective photon mass set by the inverse London penetration depth. We derive the corresponding Schrödinger-like wave equations for electromagnetic perturbations in the axial and polar sectors of the Schwarzschild spacetime in the presence of the superconducting plasma. The axial sector is governed by a single Regge--Wheeler-type equation whose effective potential contains the London mass term, whereas the polar sector contains two coupled physical degrees of freedom. Quasinormal-mode boundary conditions are formulated for the Schwarzschild geometry. The London penetration depth shifts the oscillation frequencies, modifies the damping rates, and can generate long-lived quasibound configurations when the effective field mass is sufficiently large. This formalism provides a framework for studying electromagnetic relaxation in curved superconducting media, compact objects containing superconducting phases, and effective massive-vector fields around black holes.

</details>


### [103] [Influence of quantum decoherence on black hole shadow visibility and time domain echoes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03762)
*Sen Guo,Xin Li,Yu Liang,Kai Lin,Li-Fang Li*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes using quantum decoherence-induced horizon reflectivity to break the observational degeneracy between black holes and horizonless exotic compact objects, predicting delayed electromagnetic echoes detectable via spatial (shadow illumination, visibility changes) and temporal (ACF-based flare echo detection) signatures in current and near-future VLBI instruments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The geometric shadow of black holes is observationally degenerate with that of horizonless exotic compact objects; this work aims to break that degeneracy by leveraging the fundamental quantum decoherence of the event horizon—the Danielson-Satishchandran-Wald mechanism—as a physically grounded source of effective reflectivity.

Method: The authors map quantum horizon decoherence to a macroscopic effective reflectivity ℛ, then use Kerr ray tracing to model electromagnetic echoes from a partially reflective boundary under both specular and diffuse (Lambertian) reflection conditions; they analyze spatial-domain effects on shadow structure and visibilities, derive scaling laws for signal strength versus membrane compactness ε, and develop an Autocorrelation Function (ACF) pipeline to extract time-domain echo signatures from noisy Sgr A* flare data.

Result: Partially reflective boundaries (ℛ > 0) produce delayed echoes that illuminate the inner shadow and modify low-frequency interferometric visibilities without altering photon subrings; echo amplitude scales as ε^Γ with Γ = 0 (specular) or Γ → 1 (diffuse); detectable ℛ thresholds are ℛ ≃ 0.12 (ngEHT) and 0.04 (BHEX) for static imaging; for Sgr A*, ACF analysis enables 3σ echo detection at amplitude 𝒜 ≳ 0.3 within 15–33 hr of continuous monitoring, robust against accretion red noise.

Conclusion: Quantum-decoherence-induced horizon reflectivity provides a theoretically grounded, observationally accessible probe of black hole unitarity; the predicted spatial and time-domain echo signatures offer a directly deployable test to distinguish black holes from horizonless mimickers using existing and upcoming VLBI facilities.

Abstract: The geometric shadow of a black hole cannot observationally distinguish horizonless exotic compact objects. We break this degeneracy by mapping the fundamental quantum decoherence of the horizon (the Danielson-Satishchandran-Wald mechanism) into a macroscopic effective reflectivity $\mathcal{R}$. Utilizing Kerr ray tracing, we show that a partially reflective boundary ($\mathcal{R}>0$) generates delayed electromagnetic echoes under both specular and diffuse (Lambertian) conditions. In the spatial domain, these echoes illuminate the inner shadow and alter low-frequency visibilities, while leaving metric-governed photon subrings unchanged. The signal strength scales with the membrane compactness $ε$ as $ε^Γ$, where $Γ=0$ for specular reflection and $Γ\to1$ for diffuse re-emission due to escape-cone collapse. This brings specular reflectivities down to $\mathcal{R}\simeq0.12$ (ngEHT) and $0.04$ (BHEX) within reach of static imaging. For Sagittarius A$^\ast$, we translate these echoes into a robust time-domain signature. Using an Autocorrelation Function (ACF) pipeline, we demonstrate that single-flare echoes can be cleanly extracted from severe accretion red noise, achieving a $3σ$ detection for amplitudes $\mathcal{A}\gtrsim0.3$ within 15 to 33 hours of continuous monitoring. This framework provides a directly deployable pathway to test black hole unitarity.

</details>


### [104] [Coherent quantum state corrections in RN--black holes: Photon dynamics, shadow casts, and energy backreaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03777)
*A. Errehymy,Y. Khedif,M. Daoud,B. Turimov,S. Usanov,Z. Yasakov,F. Turaev*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a quantum-corrected Reissner-Nordström black hole using Gaussian regularization to eliminate unphysical oscillations and tame the central singularity, yielding a Ricci-regular or integrable-singularity core; it modifies horizon structure, photon orbits, and shadow properties while recovering classical behavior asymptotically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the pathological central singularity and unphysical oscillations in standard quantum-corrected RN geometries, while preserving classical large-distance behavior and ensuring mathematical consistency (e.g., finite curvature invariants or integrable singularities).

Method: Introduces a smooth Gaussian regularization replacing sharp cutoffs; derives a modified RN metric with a core radius Rs; analyzes curvature invariants (Ricci scalar, Kretschmann scalar), effective stress-energy tensor, Misner-Sharp mass, horizon conditions, and null geodesics (photon sphere, deflection, Shapiro delay, shadow).

Result: A well-behaved quantum RN geometry emerges: Ricci-regular center at critical Rs*, otherwise integrable 1/r Kretschmann divergence; anisotropic fluid interpretation of effective energy-momentum; gradual mass accumulation; horizon topology depends on α=RQ/RM and β=Rs/RM; photon sphere shifts inward; reduced light deflection and Shapiro delay; more compact and smoother black hole shadow.

Conclusion: Gaussian regularization yields a physically viable coherent quantum black hole model that tames singularities without ad hoc cutoffs, bridges quantum and classical regimes smoothly, and produces testable observational signatures in strong-field gravity.

Abstract: We revisit the Reissner-Nordström (RN) geometry within the coherent quantum black hole framework, introducing a version that remains well-behaved at short distances while preserving the expected classical behavior at large scales. The key ingredient is a smooth Gaussian regularization, which replaces the usual sharp cutoff and {suppresses} the unphysical oscillations. This construction introduces a characteristic core radius $R_{\rm s}$, constrained relative to the mass $M$ and charge $Q$ to ensure either a {Ricci-regular} center or a softened integrable singularity. Near the origin, the spacetime exhibits distinct behaviors depending on the choice of parameters. When $R_{\rm s}$ satisfies the critical relation $R_{\rm s}^* = \sqrtπ\,R_{\rm Q}^2 / R_{\rm M}$, the geometry is {Ricci-regulFar}, while deviations from this value produce a milder, integrable singularity. {We clarify that for generic parameters, the Kretschmann scalar retains a $1/r$ divergence even at $R_{\rm s}^*$, so the center is not fully regular in the sense of finite curvature invariants.} The effective stress-energy tensor can be interpreted as an anisotropic fluid, whose density and pressure smoothly interpolate between the quantum-dominated core and the classical RN region at large radii. The Misner-Sharp mass accumulates gradually from the center, recovering the ADM mass asymptotically. The quantum scale also modifies the horizon structure, giving rise to standard, extremal, or horizonless configurations depending on the ratios $α=R_{\rm Q}/R_{\rm M}$ and $β=R_{\rm s}/R_{\rm M}$. Photon motion is correspondingly affected: the photon sphere moves inward, light deflection and Shapiro delay are slightly reduced, and the resulting shadow appears more compact and smoother, reflecting the quantum-corrected geometry.

</details>


### [105] [Nonlinear completions of the Ricci coupling in black-hole scalarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03779)
*Subrat Parida*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates spontaneous scalarization of static, spherically symmetric black holes in scalar–Gauss–Bonnet gravity with a quartic Ricci coupling completion; it derives exact near-horizon regularity conditions, identifies bifurcation structure and stability exchange via radial perturbations, and shows how quartic self-interaction (parameter γ) shrinks the domain of radially stable black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how higher-order scalar self-interactions (beyond quadratic) affect spontaneous scalarization, stability, and bifurcation structure of black holes in scalar–Gauss–Bonnet gravity—particularly whether and how nonlinear completions alter critical behavior and stability domains compared to the standard quadratic model.

Method: Analytical derivation of near-horizon regularity conditions and curvature invariants; weakly nonlinear expansion to determine bifurcation direction and critical β; exact analysis of scalar field profile and mass shift at fixed horizon scalar value; derivation and spectral analysis of a radial-perturbation master equation for arbitrary couplings (β, γ).

Result: Near-horizon regularity condition degenerates at finite horizon curvature, terminating scalarized branches; bifurcation coefficient is quadratic in β, changes sign at β ≈ 1.1156, and linearly depends on γ; mass difference between completions scales linearly with γ at equal φ_h; radial stability exchanges at both bifurcation and turning points; increasing γ monotonically shrinks the domain of radially stable black holes—exponentially completed models are less stable than quadratic truncations.

Conclusion: Quartic self-interaction (γ) qualitatively preserves spontaneous scalarization but quantitatively suppresses stability: it controls mass shifts, modifies bifurcation onset, and systematically reduces the parameter space of stable scalarized black holes—demonstrating that UV-sensitive higher-order couplings have direct, observable consequences for black hole phenomenology in modified gravity.

Abstract: We study spontaneous scalarization of static, spherically symmetric black holes in scalar--Gauss--Bonnet gravity with a Ricci coupling $F=1 βφ^{2}/4+γβ^{2}φ^{4}+O(φ^{6})$ that nonlinearly completes the quadratic one. For arbitrary couplings and potential, we derive the near-horizon regularity condition and horizon curvature invariants in closed form; scalarized branches terminate where this condition degenerates, at finite horizon curvature. A weakly nonlinear expansion fixes the bifurcation direction exactly: for a massless scalar, its governing coefficient is quadratic in $β$, changes sign at $β=1.1156$, and depends linearly on a quartic self-interaction. At equal horizon values of the scalar, completions differ in mass, to leading order, in proportion to $γ$. A radial-perturbation master equation for arbitrary couplings confirms the exchange of stability at the bifurcation and turning points. Consequently, the domain of radially stable black holes shrinks as $γ$ increases over the range studied; it is smaller for the exponential completion than for the quadratic truncation.

</details>


### [106] [Dynamical thin shells in black hole ringdown](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03798)
*Han-Wen Hu,Chen Lan,Zong-Kuan Guo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how a radially oscillating thin shell near a black hole modifies gravitational wave ringdown, finding Floquet sidebands, enhanced late-time echoes for nearby shells, and an exceptional point with a second-order pole contribution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how matter dynamics (e.g., accretion shells or exotic compact objects) near black holes influence gravitational wave ringdown signatures—particularly echoes and spectral features—beyond standard BH perturbation theory.

Method: Applies linearized Israel junction conditions on a moving worldtube to derive matching conditions for polar and axial metric perturbations; employs Floquet theory to analyze scattering from periodic shell motion; computes quasinormal mode spectra and late-time waveform contributions including exceptional point analysis.

Result: 1) Periodic shell oscillation generates Floquet sidebands in scattered waves; 2) Shell proximity strongly amplifies late-time echoes (~100×) via coupling to long-lived shell modes; 3) An exceptional point emerges where two polar modes coalesce, yielding a distinct second-order pole imprint in the late-time waveform.

Conclusion: Radial shell oscillations significantly alter ringdown phenomenology—especially echo amplitude and spectral structure—highlighting the importance of dynamical boundary conditions and non-Hermitian degeneracies (exceptional points) in BH spectroscopy.

Abstract: We study how a radially oscillating thin shell outside a black hole (BH) affects gravitational wave ringdown and derive matching conditions for polar and axial perturbations from the linearized Israel junction conditions on the moving worldtube. Periodic shell motion produces Floquet sidebands in the scattered wave. For a distant shell, the motion has little effect on the prompt ringdown and only weakly modifies the late time echoes. When the shell is closer to the BH, a replica of a long lived shell mode enters the incident frequency band, increasing the late time echo amplitude by roughly two orders of magnitude. We also identify an exceptional point where two polar modes coalesce and find the contribution of its second order pole in the late time waveform.

</details>


### [107] [Thermodynamics, thermodynamic geometry, and the shadow of a Bardeen black hole surrounded by a Kiselev fluid: the role of regularity, asymptotics, and observational self-consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03801)
*Hoang Van Quyet*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reanalyzes the Bardeen black hole surrounded by Kiselev quintessence fluid, correcting prior claims about regularity and asymptotics; it provides exact horizon classification, verifies thermodynamic laws with machine-precision algebra, identifies spurious singularities in geometric thermodynamics, and critically reassesses EHT constraints on the quintessence parameter c.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To correct erroneous literature claims that the Kiselev-modified Bardeen black hole is regular at the origin and asymptotically flat for nonzero quintessence parameter c, and to rigorously assess its thermodynamic and observational viability—particularly in light of Event Horizon Telescope (EHT) constraints on Sgr A*.

Method: Analytical derivation of curvature invariants and asymptotic structure; closed-form classification of horizon topology in the (q,c) parameter space; application of Ma-Zhao prescription to obtain consistent nonlinear-electrodynamics mass and entropy; computer-algebra verification of extended first law and Smarr relation; thermodynamic geometry analysis using Quevedo and Ruppeiner metrics; analytical computation of photon sphere and shadow critical impact parameter b_c(q,c,w); consistency check of cosmological scales against EHT bounds.

Result: The spacetime is regular only for c=0; for c≠0, curvature diverges at r→0 and asymptotics are de Sitter-like with a cosmological horizon r_c; full (q,c) horizon structure is mapped exactly, including extremal and Nariai boundaries; corrected entropy satisfies extended first law and Smarr relation to machine precision; heat capacity exhibits one physical Davies-type divergence (quartic in parameters); Quevedo curvature contains an unphysical singularity from entropy’s integration constant; Ruppeiner curvature vanishes for pure Bardeen but is nonzero for c≠0; EHT-derived bound c ≲ 10⁻²/M is inconsistent with cosmological scales and must be interpreted as local (near-horizon), not global.

Conclusion: The Kiselev-Bardeen solution is physically viable only for small c near the horizon; its global structure and thermodynamics are fundamentally altered by c≠0, invalidating prior assumptions; EHT constraints cannot be naively extrapolated to cosmological quintessence density, demanding a careful separation between local strong-field phenomenology and global dark-energy models.

Abstract: We reconsider the Bardeen black hole surrounded by a Kiselev fluid, obtained by adding a Kiselev term to the Ayon-Beato-Garcia-Bardeen metric. Contrary to claims in the literature, the solution is regular at the origin only for $c=0$: for $c\neq0$ curvature invariants diverge as $r\to0$, and the spacetime is asymptotically de Sitter-like rather than flat, with a cosmological horizon $r_c$ for $0<c<c_N(q)$. We map the full $(q,c)$ domain of horizon structures in closed form, locating the extremal and Nariai-type boundaries and their cusp. Using the Ma-Zhao prescription for the mass-dependent nonlinear-electrodynamics source, we derive the corrected entropy and verify the extended first law and Smarr relation to machine precision by computer-algebra differentiation, not through the entropy's own definition. The heat capacity shows a single Davies-type divergence, located as a closed-form quartic and expanded analytically in $c$. In thermodynamic geometry, the Quevedo-metric curvature has a spurious second singularity generated by the entropy's integration constant and the non-uniqueness of its conformal factor; we isolate this artefact and show only the Davies-point divergence is physical. The Ruppeiner curvature, identically zero for pure Bardeen, becomes non-zero once $c\neq0$. For the shadow, we compute the critical impact parameter $b_c(q,c,w)$ and show the nominal bound $c\lesssim10^{-2}/M$ from the EHT image of Sagittarius A* is not self-consistent: the same $c$ places the cosmological horizon nine orders of magnitude closer than Earth's distance to Sgr A*, and the bound is fourteen orders of magnitude larger than $H_0/c$, the true cosmological dark-energy scale. We reinterpret the EHT constraint as bounding $c$ only near the horizon, not as an astrophysical or cosmological bound on the quintessence parameter.

</details>


### [108] [Four-derivative corrections to the neutral-scalar Christodoulou threshold in charged de Sitter black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03805)
*Reggie C. Pantig*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how four-derivative quantum gravity corrections modify the Christodoulou regularity criterion for a neutral scalar field near the Cauchy horizon of 4D charged de Sitter black holes, finding that cosmological scale and quasinormal mode competition govern threshold shifts — not extremality alone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how higher-derivative corrections in effective gravity affect the stability and regularity of the Cauchy horizon—specifically whether quantum-gravitational deformations preserve or violate the Christodoulou criterion for neutral scalar perturbations in charged dS black holes.

Method: Analytic derivation of corrected static black hole solutions and cold extremality relations within an Einstein-Maxwell effective theory with leading parity-even four-derivative terms; computation of near-horizon geometry, surface gravities, and quasinormal spectra; reduction of the scalar wave equation to an AdS₂ throat problem near cold extremality; comparative analysis of spectral gaps and regularity ratios across cosmological scales.

Result: The Christodoulou regularity threshold shifts non-monotonically with cosmological scale: positive effective coefficients move it toward larger horizon separation at small/intermediate Λ, but reverse direction near large Λ; this reversal coincides with a switch in the dominant quasinormal family supplying the spectral gap; extremality conditions do not universally fix the threshold correction due to distinct coefficient dependencies for mass, throat geometry, and regularity ratio.

Conclusion: Cosmological scale and spectral-family competition—not extremality—are decisive for Cauchy-horizon regularity of neutral scalars under effective gravity corrections; thus, extremal limits cannot generically predict stability behavior in de Sitter black holes.

Abstract: We study leading parity-even four-derivative corrections to the linear Christodoulou regularity criterion for a neutral, massless, minimally coupled spectator scalar in four-dimensional charged de Sitter black holes. We derive the corrected static solution, cold extremality relation, near-horizon geometry, Cauchy-horizon surface gravity, and neutral scalar quasinormal spectrum within Einstein-Maxwell effective theory. Near cold extremality, the scalar equation reduces analytically to the finite-temperature anti-de Sitter two-dimensional throat problem. We find that the dominant nonstationary spherically symmetric mode retains its cold-limit regularity ratio through first order, while higher angular sectors respond to the deformation of the relative throat radii. The finite-distance neutral-scalar Christodoulou boundary nevertheless shifts because the relevant exterior scalar spectrum and the unequal horizon surface gravities enter the regularity ratio. At smaller and intermediate cosmological scales, positive effective coefficients move the Christodoulou threshold to larger normalized separation of the Cauchy and event horizons. Toward the larger cosmological-scale part of the cold branch, the same coefficient directions move that threshold back toward the cold endpoint. The reversal accompanies a transition in the quasinormal family that supplies the spectral gap. We further show that the coefficient combination governing the fixed-charge extremal mass parameter differs generically from those governing the cold throat and the finite-distance neutral-scalar boundary. Extremality therefore does not determine a universal direction for the neutral-scalar Christodoulou-threshold correction. Our results identify cosmological scale and spectral-family competition as essential elements in relating effective gravity, black-hole extremality, and Cauchy-horizon regularity in this spectator sector.

</details>


### [109] [Optical Signatures of Black Holes Surrounded by a Generalized Cloud of Strings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04173)
*Marcos V. de S. Silva,Ednaldo L. B. Junior,Carlos F. S. Pereira,Diego Sáez-Chillón Gómez*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies black hole optical signatures in the Letelier--Alencar spacetime (a Schwarzschild black hole with a generalized cloud of strings), analyzing null geodesics, shadow radius constraints from Sgr A* and M87*, weak-field light deflection (via Gauss–Bonnet and perturbative methods), Shapiro delay with asymptotic conical contribution, and ray-traced images of thin accretion disks—revealing non-uniform lensing effects beyond simple rescaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe how a generalized string cloud (non-asymptotically flat, conical asymptotics) modifies black hole optical observables—shadow size, light bending, time delay, and disk imaging—and to constrain string parameters using EHT shadow measurements.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of null geodesics; Gauss–Bonnet theorem and perturbative expansion for weak-field deflection angle; Shapiro delay computation in conical asymptotics; backward ray tracing for accretion disk imaging; comparison with Schwarzschild baseline and EHT shadow bounds.

Result: String parameters alter photon sphere radius and critical impact parameter non-monotonically; shadow radius differs from critical impact parameter due to non-flat asymptotics, enabling observational constraints; weak-field deflection includes conical + gravitational terms; Shapiro delay acquires distance-dependent asymptotic contribution; disk images show non-uniform mapping—e.g., smaller shadows can still yield outward Einstein ring shifts.

Conclusion: The Letelier--Alencar geometry produces rich, nontrivial optical signatures distinct from Schwarzschild, demonstrating that different observables (shadow radius, Einstein ring position, time delay) encode complementary information about the asymptotic and local spacetime structure—enabling multi-messenger tests of exotic matter models around black holes.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the optical signatures of black holes surrounded by a generalized cloud of strings, described by the Letelier--Alencar spacetime. We first analyze null geodesics, showing that the string parameters can either increase or decrease the photon sphere radius and the critical impact parameter relative to their Schwarzschild values. Since the spacetime is not asymptotically flat, the critical impact parameter does not directly coincide with the physical shadow radius. We use this distinction to derive constraints on the model parameters from the shadow radius bounds of Sgr A* and M87*. In the weak-field regime, we calculate the light deflection angle using both the Gauss--Bonnet method and a perturbative expansion of the null geodesic equations, obtaining consistent results that contain a contribution from the asymptotically conical geometry in addition to the local gravitational deflection. We also examine the Shapiro time delay and identify a distance dependent contribution associated with the non Minkowskian asymptotic background. Finally, we study the optical appearance of geometrically and optically thin accretion disks and construct celestial sphere images through backward ray tracing. The transfer functions and lensing images show that the generalized cloud of strings modifies the null geodesic mapping in a nonuniform manner rather than producing a simple rescaling of the Schwarzschild image. In particular, configurations with a smaller shadow may still produce an outward displacement of the main Einstein ring image, demonstrating that different optical observables probe distinct aspects of photon propagation in this geometry.

</details>


### [110] [Exact Solutions for Colliding Neutrino Waves and the Absence of Mutual Scattering for a Single Grassmann Mode](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04321)
*Yorgo Senikoglu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper exactly solves the head-on collision of two plane neutrino waves in the Einstein-Weyl system, showing they pass through each other without scattering due to nilpotency of the Grassmann-mode bilinear.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether and how neutrino waves scatter during gravitational interaction in a fully coupled relativistic field-theoretic setting.

Method: Exact analytical solution of the coupled Einstein-Weyl equations for head-on colliding plane waves; treatment of the Weyl spinor as an odd Grassmann-valued field; use of O'Brien-Synge junction conditions for matching across wave regions.

Result: The two counter-propagating waves must share a single Grassmann mode; the bilinear ε = ηη* is nilpotent; the full system is solved exactly in the interaction region; metric functions decouple the waves; Ψ₂ = 0; Ψ₀ and Ψ₄ are proportional to individual energy fluxes; no mutual scattering occurs.

Conclusion: Nilpotency of the Grassmann-mode bilinear enforces wave transparency—neutrino waves traverse each other unscattered in this exact solution; cross-coupling arises only beyond this strict nilpotent, single-mode assumption.

Abstract: We study the head-on collision of two plane neutrino waves in the coupled Einstein-Weyl system, treating the massless Weyl spinor as an odd Grassmann valued field. We show that the mixed components of the Einstein equations force the two counter propagating waves to be built on a single Grassmann mode, whose bilinear $\varepsilon=ηη^{*}$ is nilpotent. With this structure the full coupled system is solved exactly in the interaction region for arbitrary amplitude and phase profiles, and the metric and spinor fields are matched to the single wave regions by the non-impulsive O'Brien-Synge junction conditions. The metric functions contain no terms coupling the two waves; the Weyl scalar $Ψ_2$ vanishes, and $Ψ_0$, $Ψ_4$ are proportional to the neutrino energy fluxes. The waves therefore traverse each other without mutual scattering; we trace this to the nilpotency of the single mode bilinear. We discuss the limits of this result and indicate where cross couplings can arise.

</details>


### [111] [Bouncing Dust Collapse and Black-to-White Hole Transition on the Brane](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04353)
*Rikpratik Sengupta,Chiranjeeb Singha*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes spherical collapse of a dust cloud on a timelike-extra-dimension braneworld (Shtanov–Sahni model), deriving exact interior dynamics, matching to a charged Vaidya exterior, and showing geodesic completeness, bounce conditions, white-hole re-emergence, and recovery of Oppenheimer–Snyder in the GR limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand gravitational collapse and singularity avoidance in braneworld cosmologies with timelike extra dimensions, particularly whether bounce, geodesic completeness, and nonsingular evolution are possible under modified Friedmann dynamics with negative quadratic density terms.

Method: Exact integration of the brane-world Friedmann equation for a closed homogeneous dust cloud; Israel junction conditions applied across a comoving boundary to match to a spherically symmetric exterior; derivation of the exterior geometry as a charged Vaidya spacetime with tidal charge; construction of a global $C^{1,1}$ metric by patching advanced/retarded Vaidya regions with a static wedge; verification of energy conditions and curvature bounds.

Result: Interior collapse exhibits a nonsingular bounce if comoving density satisfies a simple condition; all curvature invariants remain bounded by $\lambda$; worldlines are geodesically complete; exterior is necessarily nonstatic charged Vaidya with positive tidal charge; trapped region forms only above a critical mass; bounce persists near boundary even when trapped; full cyclic evolution (contraction–bounce–re-expansion) is realized via a white-hole emergence into a second asymptotic region.

Conclusion: The Shtanov–Sahni braneworld with timelike extra dimension permits singularity-free, geodesically complete, cyclic gravitational collapse—unlike GR—due to the negative quadratic density term in the Friedmann equation; the model reduces to Oppenheimer–Snyder in the $\lambda \to \infty$ (GR) limit, confirming consistency.

Abstract: We study the spherical collapse of a homogeneous dust cloud on a three-brane in the Shtanov--Sahni braneworld, in which the extra dimension is timelike. In the high-energy regime the brane Friedmann equation acquires a negative quadratic term in the matter density, controlled by a critical density $\rhoc=2λ$, where $λ$ is the magnitude of the negative brane tension. For a spatially closed interior we integrate the dynamics exactly. Every turning point of the scale factor lies above $\astar=\sqrt{3/4πλ}$, a bounce exists if and only if a simple condition on the comoving density holds, and all curvature invariants stay below bounds fixed by $λ$ alone along the entire cycle. Comoving worldlines are geodesically complete. We then match the cloud across a comoving boundary to a spherically symmetric exterior with a trace-free effective source and solve the Israel conditions in closed form. The exterior is a charged Vaidya geometry whose mass function and tidal charge are fixed by the boundary radius. The tidal charge is positive, opposite in sign to the Randall--Sundrum case, and the exterior cannot be static. A trapped region forms on the boundary if and only if the mass exceeds $2/(3\sqrt{πλ})$, and in that case the bounce still takes place in an untrapped neighbourhood of the boundary. For trapped clouds we construct an exterior over the full contraction, bounce and re-expansion by joining an advanced and a retarded charged Vaidya patch through a static wedge. The matched metric is of class $C^{1,1}$, the effective energy conditions hold, and the cloud re-emerges through a white hole region into a second asymptotic region. In the general relativistic limit $λ\to\infty$ the Oppenheimer--Snyder solution is recovered.

</details>


### [112] [On the stability of spherically symmetric relativistic configurations with scalar field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04360)
*Anatolii Tugay,Valery Zhdanov,Yurii Taistra*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper investigates radial stability of static, spherically symmetric, asymptotically flat scalar-field configurations in General Relativity with power-law potentials; it finds them unstable to monopole perturbations for small scalar charges using a novel shooting method applied to a regularized master equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether static, spherically symmetric, asymptotically flat scalar-field configurations — which generically host naked singularities — are physically viable, i.e., stable against radial perturbations.

Method: Develops and applies a new shooting method to solve the eigenvalue problem for exponentially growing radial (monopole) perturbations; reduces the perturbation dynamics to a single second-order 'master' equation in divergent form with a regularized effective potential.

Result: Identifies unstable (exponentially growing) modes for sufficiently small scalar charges of the background configurations, confirming prior instability findings.

Conclusion: The static scalar-field configurations under study are linearly unstable against radial perturbations when the scalar charge is small, undermining their physical viability despite asymptotic flatness and spherical symmetry.

Abstract: Static spherically symmetric asymptotically flat configurations in General Relativity with a minimally coupled scalar field typically possess naked singularities. We study a linear stability of these configurations against radial (monopole) perturbations in case of power-law scalar field potentials. We employ a new shooting method that extracts the eigenvalues corresponding to the exponentially growing modes of the perturbation. The problem is reduced to one second order ``master'' equation in a divergent form with a regularized effective potential. We confirm our previous results showing that instability modes exist for sufficiently small scalar charges of the initial static configurations.

</details>


### [113] [Bias-aware Fisher forecasts for stochastic gravitational-wave background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04459)
*Bo-Qiang Lu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes Whittle-likelihood forecasting for stochastic gravitational-wave background detection, identifying significant amplitude error inflation (up to 10×) in a commonly used two-stage composite estimator due to its implementation—not the underlying gauss_D likelihood approximation—and provides calibrated bias and MSE corrections for power-law templates under strong-signal, stationary assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify and correct systematic amplitude error inflation in Whittle-likelihood-based stochastic gravitational-wave background forecasting, particularly for mHz interferometers (LISA, Taiji, TianQin), where the standard composite two-stage estimator introduces substantial bias not attributable to the gauss_D likelihood itself.

Method: Compares exact Whittle likelihood (control) with gauss_D (working model); performs frequentist calibration of bias vector and mean-squared-error matrix for power-law and smooth broken-power-law templates; evaluates error inflation R across segment durations (30-min to 11.5-d), signal sharpness (32-point sound-shell scan), and SNR regimes; assesses limitations of single-parameter Whittle-bias diagnostics.

Result: Composite estimator inflates marginal-Fisher amplitude error by up to R = 10.2 at 11.5-d baseline (median ~3); inflation scales as R²−1 ∝ T_seg² for short segments; irreducible efficiency gap C_b up to 18% in spectral index α_out; single-parameter bias diagnostic fails for multi-parameter inflation; inflation negligible on 30-min segments (R−1 ≤ 1.5×10⁻³) and vanishes below sensitivity floor; strong correlation corr[ln SNR, ln(R−1)] = 0.91.

Conclusion: Joint maximisation of gauss_D is unbiased and recommended as baseline; the calibrated bias and MSE matrices enable robust amplitude inference for strong, stationary signals under matching noise models; results apply only in locally Gaussian posterior regimes for Bayesian interpretation.

Abstract: Whittle-likelihood forecasting of the stochastic gravitational-wave background underpins planned mHz interferometers (LISA, Taiji, TianQin). A composite two-stage estimator, per-bin amplitude estimation then unweighted log-space template fitting, inflates the marginal-Fisher amplitude error bar on resolved-bin count by up to an order of magnitude at \(11.5\)-d baseline. This inflation arises from the composite implementation, not the determinant-approximate \texttt{gauss\_D} likelihood it approximates. Jointly maximised, \texttt{gauss\_D} is unbiased (\(R=1.000\) within \(10^{-4}\)) and is recommended baseline with exact-Whittle likelihood. With exact Whittle as control and \texttt{gauss\_D} as working model, we calibrate bias vector and mean-squared-error matrix for power-law and smooth broken-power-law templates. First, for sharp signals of a \(32\)-point sound-shell scan, the composite estimator inflates amplitude error bar by \(R=10.2\) at \(11.5\)-d baseline (median \(\simeq3\)), follows \(R^2-1\propto T_{\mathrm{seg}}^2\) at shorter segments, and has a non-removable efficiency gap \(\mathbf{C}_b\) up to \(18\%\) in \(α_{\mathrm{out}}\). Second, the usual single-parameter Whittle-bias diagnostic does not track multi-parameter inflation and errs both ways. Third, inflation is negligible on \(30\)-min segments, \(R-1\le1.5\times10^{-3}\) over measured bias-exponent range, vanishes below sensitivity floor, and tracks resolved signal strength with \(\mathrm{corr}[\ln \mathrm{SNR},\ln(R-1)]=0.91\). Results are frequentist; interval diagnostics transfer to flat-prior credible intervals only in the locally Gaussian regime. The calibration is a strong-signal, stationary statement transferable only to a matching noise model.

</details>


### [114] [Magnetic-field-induced chaos of charged particles around a rotating black hole in a King-type dark matter halo](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04521)
*Yu Wang,Meilin Liu,Haiguang Xu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies orbital dynamics of charged particles around a rotating black hole embedded in a King-type dark matter halo and immersed in a uniform magnetic field; it finds that magnetic coupling can induce chaos, but regular orbits persist depending on initial conditions and halo density.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how dark matter halos and external magnetic fields jointly influence the chaotic or regular nature of charged particle orbits near rotating black holes—relevant for astrophysical plasma environments and gravitational wave modeling.

Method: Uses an effective rotating spacetime metric constructed via a modified Newman--Janis algorithm from a static King-type dark matter halo; employs a test electromagnetic field with supporting currents; applies Hamilton–Jacobi analysis (yielding Carter constant in zero-coupling limit); diagnoses chaos via Poincaré sections, fast Lyapunov indicators (FLI), and finite-time Lyapunov exponents (FTLE), with mass-shell monitoring and numerical convergence checks.

Result: Magnetic coupling induces transition from regular to chaotic motion for some initial conditions; higher-density halos amplify FTLEs for chaotic families; weak-halo effects are numerically unresolved; certain initial conditions yield persistent regular motion (closed Poincaré curves, decaying FTLE) even with nonzero coupling.

Conclusion: The King-type dark matter halo modulates magnetic-field-induced chaos in a non-universal, initial-condition-dependent way; integrability is broken by magnetic coupling but not fully destroyed—regular orbits coexist with chaotic ones in the magnetized, halo-embedded system.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamics of charged test particles around a rotating black hole embedded in a King-type dark matter halo and subjected to a prescribed, asymptotically uniform magnetic field. We adopt an effective rotating geometry constructed from a static halo seed through a modified Newman--Janis prescription and a test electromagnetic field that generally requires supporting currents in the nonvacuum background. In the absence of magnetic coupling, the timelike Hamilton--Jacobi equation separates and admits a Carter-type constant, providing an integrable reference system. We characterize the orbital dynamics using Poincaré sections and tangent-vector growth diagnostics, including fast Lyapunov indicators and finite-time Lyapunov exponents, with mass-shell monitoring and numerical refinement checks. For the sampled initial conditions, increasing the magnetic coupling can produce a transition from regular motion to scattered Poincaré sections with sustained positive finite-time growth rates. At fixed magnetic coupling, the two higher-density halo cases yield larger finite-time exponents for a representative chaotic orbit family, whereas the weak-halo correction is not resolved consistently under numerical refinement. A different initial condition yields a closed Poincaré curve and a decreasing finite-time exponent over an extended integration, supporting regular motion despite the nonzero magnetic coupling. These results show that the King-type halo modulates magnetic-field-induced orbital instability in an initial-condition-dependent manner, while regular motion persists within the magnetized system.

</details>


### [115] [Exact solutions of Maxwell vacuum equations in null homogeneous Petrov spaces with solvable motions groups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04522)
*V. V. Obukhov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies null homogeneous Petrov spaces V*_4(N) by leveraging the geometry of associated 3D homogeneous Riemannian spaces V_3(N), classified via Bianchi types I–VII. Using canonical frames, it constructs metrics and electromagnetic vector potentials invariant under G_3(N) motions, derives Maxwell vacuum equations solely in terms of group structure constants and wave-variable derivatives, and obtains all non-equivalent exact solutions explicitly in terms of arbitrary functions of the wave variable—thereby reducing the Einstein-Maxwell system to ODEs for subsequent integration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and classify exact solutions of the Maxwell vacuum equations in null homogeneous Petrov spacetimes V*_4(N), exploiting symmetry (Bianchi-classified 3D isometry groups) to simplify the Einstein-Maxwell system.

Method: Uses the canonical (non-holonomic) frame of 3D homogeneous Riemannian space V_3(N) to derive the metric tensor of V*_4(N) and the invariant electromagnetic vector potential; formulates Maxwell vacuum equations purely in terms of Bianchi group structure constants, non-holonomic metric components, vector potential components, and their derivatives with respect to the wave variable; solves them completely for solvable Bianchi types I–VII.

Result: All non-equivalent exact solutions of Maxwell vacuum equations in V*_4(N) for Bianchi types I–VII are obtained explicitly as arbitrary functions of the wave variable; the Einstein-Maxwell equations thereby decouple, leaving only ODE systems for the Einstein equations involving those functions.

Conclusion: The symmetry-based frame method enables complete integration of Maxwell vacuum equations in null homogeneous Petrov spaces for solvable Bianchi groups, reducing the full Einstein-Maxwell problem to tractable ODEs—providing a foundational classification and solution framework for aligned electromagnetic fields in such spacetimes.

Abstract: Null homogeneous Petrov spaces $V^*_4(N)$ are considered. The geometry of $V^*_4(N)$ is determined by the geometry of the three-dimensional homogeneous Riemannian space $V_3(N)$, which is invariant under the action of the three-parameter motions group $G_3(N)$ ($N$ corresponds to the group number in the Bianchi classification). The canonical frame of the space $V_3(N)$ is used to construct the metric tensor of the space $V^*_4(N)$ and the components of the vector potential of the invariant electromagnetic field. Using this reper, we obtain Maxwell vacuum equations containing only the structure constants of the groups, the non-holonomic components of the contravariant metric tensor and vector potential, and their derivatives with respect to the wave variable. All non-equivalent exact solutions of Maxwell vacuum equations are found for the case of solvable motions groups $G_3(I)-G_3(VII)$.
  All solutions of Maxwell vacuum equations obtained in this article are written explicitly in terms of arbitrary independent functions of wave variable. Therefore, when integrating the Einstein-Maxwell equations, it has to consider only the remaining systems of Einstein equations which reduce to systems of ordinary differential equations that include only these independent functions.

</details>


### [116] [Dynamic Quantum Wormholes: Path-Integral State Selection and the Quantum Throat Trajectory in an Exactly Solvable Thin-Shell Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04577)
*Rikpratik Sengupta,Paulo Vargas Moniz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a pilot-wave minisuperspace quantization of a spherically symmetric thin-shell wormhole, showing that in asymptotically flat spacetimes the throat is quantum-mechanically frozen due to vanishing Bohmian current; however, with Schwarzschild–de Sitter exteriors, a dynamic, metastable wormhole emerges via tunneling through a classically forbidden barrier, with exact analytic treatment of self-adjointness, path integral regularization, and thimble structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve quantum dynamics of thin-shell wormholes beyond semiclassical approximations, clarify the role of boundary conditions (Euclidean vs. Lorentzian), address self-adjointness ambiguities near the throat, and determine whether quantum effects permit dynamical wormhole evolution—especially in cosmologically relevant settings like de Sitter backgrounds.

Method: Minisuperspace quantization using the throat radius $a(τ)$ as the sole degree of freedom constrained by Israel junction conditions; exact solution of the Wheeler–DeWitt equation (reducing to Whittaker and modified Bessel equations); Bohmian trajectory analysis via quantum potential and current; rigorous resolvent analyticity and Picard–Lefschetz thimble decomposition of the Lorentzian path integral; Whittaker and Airy connection formulas for asymptotic matching and self-adjoint extension; WKB and exact tunneling rate computation.

Result: In asymptotically flat exteriors: unique bounded, real wavefunction → zero Bohmian current → frozen throat, independent of equation of state, operator ordering, or signature convention. In Schwarzschild–de Sitter exteriors: potential diverges to $-\infty$ at large $a$, enabling a purely outgoing, normalizable, nonzero-current state; exact tunneling through classically forbidden region yields metastable wormhole with lifetime consistent with WKB; self-adjointness resolved analytically via Whittaker connection formula; thimble calculation matches closed-form solution to 48 digits; residual prefactor $κ_∞ = 0.814±0.004$ remains unexplained but physically irrelevant.

Conclusion: Quantum wormhole dynamics critically depend on asymptotics: flat spacetimes enforce staticity via quantum freezing, while de Sitter-like expansion permits genuine quantum-driven expansion via tunneling; the framework provides an exactly solvable, non-perturbative pilot-wave model that bridges canonical quantum gravity, path integral methods, and quantum cosmology.

Abstract: We construct a minisuperspace quantization of a dynamic, spherically symmetric thin-shell wormhole in the spirit of pilot-wave quantum cosmology, treating the throat radius $a(τ)$ as the single degree of freedom of the Israel junction-condition constraint. For a pressureless shell of mass $M$ the Wheeler--DeWitt equation reduces exactly to Whittaker's equation, and to the modified Bessel equation as $M\to0$. When the exterior is asymptotically flat, boundedness of the wave function as $a\to\infty$ selects a unique state that can always be chosen real. Since any real solution has a vanishing Bohmian current, the throat is frozen for any linear equation of state and any operator ordering, independently of the choice between Euclidean and Lorentzian boundary conditions. This state is the exact $ε\to0^+$ limit of the regularized Lorentzian path integral, as shown by resolvent analyticity and by an exact Picard--Lefschetz thimble calculation that agrees with the closed-form solution to $48$ digits. The near-throat self-adjointness ambiguity is resolved in closed form through the Whittaker connection formula. Replacing the exterior by Schwarzschild--de~Sitter removes the obstruction: the potential tends to $-\infty$ at large $a$, and the equation admits a genuinely dynamic, purely outgoing state. Propagated exactly through the classically forbidden barrier, it carries a conserved, nonzero Bohmian current everywhere, including the inner pocket. It describes a long-lived metastable wormhole that tunnels into unbounded expansion at a rate consistent with the WKB estimate. Airy connection formulas leave a residual prefactor constant $κ_\infty=0.814\pm0.004$ for sub-critical coupling, which we do not trace to its analytic origin, and the physical results do not depend on it.

</details>


### [117] [Quasinormal-Mode Collider Spectroscopy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04595)
*Mian Zhu,Jun Tao,Peng Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Introduces quasinormal-mode collider spectroscopy (QCS) to study nonlinear interactions among black-hole quasinormal modes, establishing factorization and reconstruction properties of cubic strain residues for consistency tests and waveform prediction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To move beyond linear BH spectroscopy and probe how quasinormal modes interact nonlinearly during ringdown—revealing intermediate states and testing strong-field gravity in the nonlinear regime.

Method: Develops QCS as a theoretical framework inspired by cosmological collider physics; derives structural properties of cubic strain residues at isolated nondegenerate intermediate poles, including residue factorization and horizon/infinity-based reconstruction of intermediate pole contributions.

Result: 1) Cubic strain residue factorizes into excitation amplitude and quadratic conversion coefficient at nondegenerate intermediate poles, yielding rank constraints across output channels and second-order initial data; 2) Intermediate pole residue is fully reconstructible from horizon and asymptotic radiation, enabling prediction of cubic waveform differences under varied second-order initial data.

Conclusion: QCS provides a consistent, predictive framework for nonlinear black-hole ringdown, linking lower-order observables to higher-order responses and enabling new tests of mode coupling and strong-field gravity.

Abstract: Black-hole (BH) quasinormal modes encode the properties of merger remnants and provide powerful tests of strong-field gravity. The BH spectroscopy has predominantly treated them as independent linear oscillations. Nonlinear ringdown can additionally reveal how these modes interact and which intermediate states mediate their interactions. Inspired by cosmological collider physics, we introduce quasinormal-mode collider spectroscopy (QCS), a framework for organizing and predicting nonlinear BH responses. We establish two structural results. First, at an isolated, nondegenerate intermediate pole, a cubic strain residue factorizes into an excitation amplitude and a quadratic conversion coefficient. This factorization imposes rank constraints across output channels and choices of second-order initial data, providing consistency tests of the intermediate-mode description. Second, the intermediate pole residue can be reconstructed from its horizon and infinity radiation. With the first-order field and third-order data held fixed, this reconstruction extends to the full cubic waveform difference generated by changing the second-order initial data. QCS thus connects lower-order radiation to higher-order observables and provides a framework for probing the interaction structure of nonlinear ringdown.

</details>


### [118] [Covariance scaling and a dual-mode torque diagnostic in measurements of the gravitational constant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04707)
*Vineet Kumar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes inconsistencies in laboratory measurements of the gravitational constant G, using a proportional covariance-scale model on the CODATA-2018 dataset; it finds that the 2026 NIST torsion-balance result is statistically consistent once historical extra dispersion is accounted for, identifies a common absolute torque offset in NIST’s free-minus-servo data, and provides diagnostic tools for torsion-balance consistency without recommending a new G value.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing discrepancy among laboratory determinations of G — which exceed quoted uncertainties — by rigorously auditing reported covariances, accounting for apparatus lineage (e.g., BIPM/NIST torsion balances), and testing physically motivated systematic models (absolute vs. fractional torque offsets).

Method: Applies a proportional covariance-scale model to the 16 CODATA-2018 G determinations, excluding BIPM-14 due to apparatus lineage with the 2026 NIST redetermination; performs χ²-based consistency tests under scaled vs. unscaled covariance; fits a common absolute torque offset ΔN across four NIST free-minus-servo configurations; compares absolute-offset vs. fractional-scale torque models via Δχ² contrast under independent Type-A uncertainty treatment.

Result: The 2026 NIST result is inconsistent under unscaled covariance (low p-value) but acceptable after applying historically inferred dispersion (F-test p = 0.382); a common absolute torque offset ΔN = 1.918 ± 0.215 pN·m explains NIST’s four-configuration differences significantly better than a fractional-scale model (Δχ² = 11.041); full-archive and lineage-excluded fits yield comparable conclusions.

Conclusion: Discrepancies in G measurements stem partly from underreported dispersion tied to apparatus lineage; the analysis validates the NIST 2026 result within an extended uncertainty framework, establishes a robust diagnostic for dual-mode torsion balances, and underscores the need for covariance-aware metrological audits — but deliberately refrains from proposing a revised G value.

Abstract: Laboratory determinations of the Newtonian gravitational constant $G$ disagree by more than their quoted uncertainties allow. We examine the sixteen determinations comprising the CODATA-2018 archive using a proportional covariance-scale model that retains the full reported covariance matrix. Because the 2026 NIST torsion-balance redetermination shares apparatus lineage with the BIPM Mark II balance, our primary historical fit excludes the BIPM-14 determination, giving $\hatλ = 2.967$ for the remaining fifteen measurements. Evaluated against this historical model under the declared uniform covariance-scaling convention, the correlated four-configuration NIST result is highly unlikely under the archive's reported, unscaled covariance but is statistically unremarkable once the historically inferred extra dispersion is applied ($Q_λ= 4.840$; finite-sample $F$ tail $p = 0.382$); a mean-only scaling sensitivity leaves the vector discrepant. Retaining the complete archive also gives a comparable result. We further examine four matched free-minus-servo torque differences reported by NIST. They are consistent with a single common absolute torque offset across the four tested configurations, $\widehat{ΔN} = 1.918 \pm 0.215$ pN m, and poorly described by an equally simple fractional-scale alternative under the stated independent-Type-A treatment, with a chi-square contrast of $Δχ^{2} = 11.041$ between the two torque models. The paper provides a covariance audit of the historical archive, a lineage-qualified consistency check, and an experimentally testable diagnostic for dual-mode torsion balances, without proposing a revised value of $G$.

</details>


### [119] [Polyakov backreaction in large D discretely self-similar collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04960)
*Sheng-Hong Lai,Wen-Yu Wen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates quantum backreaction effects—specifically from the trace anomaly—on discretely self-similar Einstein–Klein–Gordon solutions in high spacetime dimensions, revealing that anomaly-induced corrections prevent generic smooth, self-similar completions of the classical singularity; instead, they produce a critical dilaton surface whose crossings are generically singular, and only a fine-tuned, high-curvature throat matches smoothly to the exterior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether quantum corrections—particularly from the conformal (trace) anomaly—can resolve or regularize the classical singularity in discretely self-similar gravitational collapse models, especially in the large-D limit where analytic control is enhanced.

Method: Analyzes semiclassical backreaction using the Polyakov effective action (auxiliary field formulation) coupled to spherical Einstein–Klein–Gordon gravity; imposes horizon regularity to fix a Floquet exponent and select a unique quantum state; exploits analyticity, discrete self-similarity, and large-D scaling; constructs and solves a nonlinear inner expansion near the center, identifying a critical dilaton surface via matching constraints.

Result: The Polyakov anomaly induces affine-periodic (not periodic) auxiliary field behavior with nontrivial monodromy; spherical reduction generates a position-dependent quantum coupling that breaks uniformity of the first-order expansion; a critical dilaton surface emerges where generic trajectories become singular; only a narrow subset of solutions—characterized by high curvature and zero-mass exterior matching—avoids singularity; anomaly also decouples effective null-energy conditions from classical matter and curvature loci.

Conclusion: The minimally coupled Polyakov anomaly model does not generically yield smooth, exactly self-similar extensions of the classical central singularity; smoothness requires extreme fine-tuning, implying that quantum backreaction alone—within this framework—fails to robustly resolve the singularity.

Abstract: We study anomaly-induced semiclassical backreaction on an analytic family of discretely self-similar solutions of Einstein--Klein--Gordon theory at large spacetime dimension. Horizon regularity fixes a null-coordinate Floquet exponent and selects a unique regular quantum state. The Polyakov auxiliary field is affine-periodic rather than periodic, with a monodromy that is determined independently by a global zero-mode condition and by the conformal-coordinate scaling. Spherical reduction introduces a quantum coupling that grows toward the center, so the periodic first-order expansion is nonuniform. We construct the resulting nonlinear inner problem and find a critical dilaton surface. Generic crossings of this surface are singular, whereas matching to the regular zero-mass exterior selects a narrow, high-curvature throat. The anomaly also separates the effective null-energy boundaries from the classical matter flux and zero-curvature curves. These results show that the minimally coupled Polyakov model does not yield a generic smooth, exactly self-similar completion of the classical center.

</details>


### [120] [Thermodynamic Volumes of Extended Black Hole Thermodynamics with Auxiliary Gauge Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05022)
*Zili Mou,Yu Tian,Hongbao Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives the first law of extended black hole thermodynamics using the Iyer-Wald formalism applied to a modified gravitational Lagrangian with coupling constants promoted to dynamical scalar fields via auxiliary gauge fields; it confirms that thermodynamic volumes conjugate to these couplings are independent of the auxiliary fields, match prior brute-force results, and can alternatively be expressed in terms of those fields evaluated at the horizon.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously derive the first law of extended black hole thermodynamics—particularly thermodynamic volumes conjugate to coupling constants (e.g., cosmological constant)—within a covariant, field-theoretic framework, avoiding ad hoc parameter variation.

Method: Applies the Iyer-Wald formalism to a modified gravitational Lagrangian where coupling constants are promoted to dynamical scalar fields by introducing auxiliary gauge fields; computes the Noether charge and Wald entropy; identifies thermodynamic potentials and conjugate variables via boundary integrals and on-shell variation.

Result: The thermodynamic volumes conjugate to coupling constants are shown to be independent of the auxiliary gauge fields and identical to those obtained by direct parameter variation; additionally, these volumes admit an equivalent expression solely in terms of the auxiliary gauge fields evaluated at the black hole horizon.

Conclusion: The Iyer-Wald approach consistently reproduces extended black hole thermodynamics without relying on external parameter variation; the horizon-based expression of thermodynamic volume in terms of auxiliary fields validates prior geometric proposals and strengthens the physical interpretation of coupling constants as thermodynamic variables.

Abstract: By applying the Iyer-Wald formalism to the modified Lagrangian form for the gravitational system, where the coupling constants appearing in the original Lagrangian form, such as the cosmological constant, are promoted to the dynamical scalar fields by introducing the corresponding auxiliary gauge fields, we derive the first law of the extended black hole thermodynamics, where the resulting thermodynamic volumes as the conjugate thermodynamic variables to the coupling constants are nevertheless independent of the auxiliary gauge fields and exactly the same as those obtained before via varying the coupling constants by brute force. In addition, it is further shown that such volumes can also be expressed in terms of the auxiliary gauge fields and evaluated on the black hole horizon, validating the proposal made in the previous literature.

</details>


### [121] [Thermodynamic Stability of a Viscous Variable Generalised Chaplygin Gas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05361)
*D. Panigrahi,K. Kanjilal,Saibal Ray*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a viscous variable generalized Chaplygin gas (VVGCG) model with volume-dependent parameter B and square-root bulk viscosity, analyzing its cosmological dynamics and thermodynamic consistency in a flat FLRW universe; it finds a physically viable branch (ξ₀ < 0) that yields early deceleration, late-time phantom-like acceleration, and full thermodynamic stability—including positive heat capacity, third-law-compliant entropy, and fundamental identity satisfaction—while fitting Hubble-57 data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a thermodynamically consistent dark energy/dark fluid model that unifies early decelerated expansion and late-time phantom-like acceleration, overcoming instabilities and inconsistencies of standard Chaplygin gas variants.

Method: Analytical cosmological dynamics in flat FLRW geometry using the VVGCG equation of state P = −B/ρ^α with B = B₀V^(n/3), coupled to bulk viscosity Π = −3ξH where ξ = ξ₀ρ^(1/2); full thermodynamic analysis including thermal/caloric equations of state, entropy derivation, heat capacity sign, stability criteria (isothermal/adiabatic), third law verification, and fundamental identity check; statistical constraint via Hubble-57 observational data.

Result: A physically viable branch exists for ξ₀ < 0 and n < 0: early positive viscous pressure causes deceleration; effective pressure changes sign at V_c, acceleration begins at larger V_f; w → −∞ (phantom-like) as V → ∞ for n < 0; thermodynamic stability (c_V > 0 and stability conditions) holds simultaneously only for n < 0 and ξ₀ < 0; entropy → 0 as T → 0; finite minimum volume and maximum temperature exist; fundamental identity is satisfied; Hubble-57 constraints yield α = 0.0024, n = −0.0182, ξ₀ = −0.0015 — all within stable region.

Conclusion: The VVGCG model provides a unified, thermodynamically consistent description of cosmic evolution from radiation/matter-dominated deceleration to late-time phantom-like acceleration, generalizing prior Chaplygin models and satisfying all major thermodynamic laws and observational constraints.

Abstract: We investigate the cosmological dynamics and thermodynamic properties of a viscous variable generalized Chaplygin gas (VVGCG) model in a spatially flat Friedmann--Lema^ıtre--Robertson--Walker universe, described by $P=-B/ρ^α$, where $B=B_0V^{n/3}$. The model includes a bulk-viscous pressure $Π=-3ξH$, with $ξ=ξ_0ρ^{1/2}$. We find that the physically viable branch corresponds to $ξ_0<0$, for which the positive viscous pressure dominates at early times, producing decelerated expansion. As the volume increases, the effective pressure changes sign at a finite volume $V_c$, while the transition to acceleration occurs at a larger volume $V_f$. At large volume, the equation-of-state parameter approaches a phantom-like regime for $n<0$. A detailed thermodynamic analysis of the thermal and caloric equations of state, entropy, heat capacity, and isothermal and adiabatic stability shows that the stability conditions and $c_V>0$ are simultaneously satisfied for $n<0$ and $ξ_0<0$. The entropy vanishes as $T\rightarrow0$, consistent with the third law, while the model admits a finite minimum volume and corresponding maximum temperature. Thermodynamic consistency is further verified through the fundamental thermodynamic identity. The framework also contains several existing Chaplygin-gas models as special cases. Constraints from the Hubble-57 data yield $α=0.0024$, $n=-0.0182$, and $ξ_0=-0.0015$, consistent with the thermodynamically stable parameter region. Thus, the VVGCG model provides a consistent description of cosmic evolution from an early decelerating phase to late-time phantom-like acceleration while remaining thermodynamically stable throughout the evolution.

</details>


### [122] [Ellis-Bronnikov wormholes with NUT charge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05656)
*Masato Nozawa,Tomohiro Harada,Keisuke Nakashi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs exact NUT-charged wormhole solutions in Einstein-phantom-scalar gravity—both asymptotically locally flat and AdS variants—showing regularity, geodesic completeness, finite conserved charges, and an emergent ambi-Kähler structure after Euclideanization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend known wormhole geometries (e.g., Ellis-Bronnikov) by incorporating NUT charge while preserving regularity and physical viability—addressing open questions about consistent coupling of NUT charge with traversable wormholes and their thermodynamic/gravitational charges in scalar-gravity theories.

Method: Exact solution construction in the Einstein-phantom-scalar system; analysis of global geometry (asymptotics, singularities, geodesic completeness); covariant phase space formalism for Hamiltonian charges; double Wick rotation and conformal classification to identify ambi-Kähler structure.

Result: 1) An exact stationary, singularity-free, asymptotically locally flat NUT–Ellis-Bronnikov wormhole with Dirac-Misner string and CTCs but no Killing horizons → geodesically complete. 2) A generalization with nontrivial scalar potential yields an asymptotically locally AdS wormhole interpolating between distinct AdS vacua. 3) Finite, well-defined Hamiltonian charges at both asymptotic ends under suitable scalar boundary conditions. 4) Euclideanized metric admits an ambi-Kähler conformal structure.

Conclusion: NUT-charged wormholes can be consistently realized in phantom-scalar gravity without curvature singularities or event horizons; their asymptotic structure supports finite gravitational charges, and their Euclidean counterparts exhibit rich geometric structure (ambi-Kähler), suggesting deeper connections between wormhole physics, NUT geometry, and supersymmetric or twistorial frameworks.

Abstract: We construct an exact NUT-charged generalization of the Ellis-Bronnikov wormhole in the Einstein-phantom-scalar system. The solution is stationary, asymptotically locally flat at both ends and free of curvature singularities. It inherits characteristic features of the Taub-NUT geometry, including the Dirac-Misner string and closed timelike curves, while the absence of Killing horizons eliminates the horizon-induced infinite winding of geodesics and renders the spacetime geodesically complete. By allowing the phantom field to have a nontrivial scalar potential with an infinite sequence of AdS vacua, we also obtain an exact asymptotically locally AdS wormhole with a NUT charge, which interpolates between different vacua at its two asymptotic ends. Using the covariant phase space formalism, we show that the Hamiltonian charges associated with the two asymptotic regions are finite under appropriate scalar boundary conditions. By performing a double Wick rotation to Euclidean signature, we demonstrate that the conformal class of the resulting metric admits an ambi-Kähler structure.

</details>


### [123] [Exact black hole solutions in Palatini Kalb-Ramond gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05799)
*Lin-Yi She,Rui-Jie Niu,Ke Yang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies static, spherically symmetric black holes in Palatini Kalb-Ramond gravity—a Lorentz-violating extension of GR where the Kalb-Ramond field acquires a vacuum expectation value. It derives exact black hole solutions for all Λ (positive, zero, negative), computes conserved mass via Iyer-Wald formalism, confirms first law and Smarr relation for Λ ≤ 0 (identifying mass as thermodynamic enthalpy), and constrains Lorentz violation using solar system tests—most tightly via Mercury’s perihelion precession, bounding q = ℓ₂/(1−ℓ₁) to [−7.4×10⁻¹², 3.7×10⁻¹¹].


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore consistent Lorentz violation in gravity via Palatini formulation of Kalb-Ramond theory, where spontaneous Lorentz symmetry breaking arises from a nonzero vacuum expectation value of the Kalb-Ramond field, and to assess phenomenological viability through black hole solutions and solar system tests.

Method: Palatini variational principle with independent metric and torsion-free affine connection; derivation of modified field equations; exact integration for static, spherically symmetric solutions; Iyer-Wald formalism for conserved mass; thermodynamic analysis (first law, Smarr relation) for Λ ≤ 0; post-Newtonian comparison with Mercury perihelion precession, light deflection, and Shapiro delay.

Result: Exact black hole solutions for arbitrary Λ; nonzero ℓ₁ and ℓ₂ simultaneously allowed (unlike metric formulation); conserved mass identified as thermodynamic enthalpy for Λ ≤ 0; solar system tests constrain only the combination q = ℓ₂/(1−ℓ₁); Mercury’s perihelion yields |q| ≲ 10⁻¹¹.

Conclusion: Palatini Kalb-Ramond gravity provides a consistent, phenomenologically viable framework for Lorentz violation, with tight empirical bounds on the dimensionless Lorentz-violating parameter q from precision solar system observations.

Abstract: We investigate static and spherically symmetric black holes in Palatini Kalb-Ramond gravity, where a nonzero vacuum expectation value of the Kalb-Ramond field spontaneously breaks local Lorentz symmetry. Treating the metric and the torsion-free affine connection as independent variables, we derive the modified field equations and obtain an exact family of solutions encompassing positive, vanishing, and negative values of the cosmological constant $Λ$. Unlike the corresponding purely metric formulation, the Palatini theory consistently allows both Lorentz-violating parameters $\ell_1$ and $\ell_2$ to be nonzero. Using the Iyer-Wald formalism, we determine the conserved mass of the black hole solutions. For the $Λ\leq0$ sector, we further verify the first law and the Smarr relation, identifying the conserved mass as the thermodynamic enthalpy. We then confront the $Λ=0$ solution with Mercury's perihelion precession, solar light deflection, and the Shapiro time delay. Once the central mass parameter is calibrated by planetary dynamics, these observables constrain the Lorentz-violating parameters only through $q\equiv\ell_2/(1-\ell_1)$. Among these tests, Mercury's perihelion precession yields the most stringent constraint, $-7.4\times10^{-12} \leq q \leq 3.7\times10^{-11}$.

</details>


### [124] [Gravitational-wave Echoes Beyond the Hubble Time from Quantum Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05886)
*Che-Yu Chen,Naritaka Oshita,Yuki Yokokura*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes gravitational-wave echoes from a horizonless compact object model that satisfies the entropy-area law; it finds that odd-parity waves are trapped inside due to extreme redshift, yielding echo delays longer than the age of the Universe—rendering echoes effectively unobservable and consistent with current null detections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test quantum-gravity-inspired horizonless alternatives to black holes by examining their gravitational-wave echo signatures, especially in light of null observational results.

Method: Uses a non-perturbative semi-classical Einstein solution modeling a horizonless compact object with anisotropic fluid interior in classical GR; analytically studies odd-parity gravitational wave propagation and reflection at the surface under strong redshift effects.

Result: Odd-parity gravitational waves are not reflected but exponentially redshifted and trapped inside the object, leading to echo-delay times vastly exceeding the age of the Universe—thus making echoes undetectable.

Conclusion: The absence of observed gravitational-wave echoes is naturally explained by this quantum-corrected horizonless model, and distinguishing it from classical black holes requires probes beyond echo timing—e.g., tidal deformability, quasi-normal mode spectrum, or late-time ringdown deviations.

Abstract: We investigate gravitational-wave echoes in a horizonless compact object model, a non-perturbative solution of the semi-classical Einstein equation that reproduces the entropy-area law from the bulk structure. By employing a model of anisotropic fluids in classical general relativity (GR), we show that odd-parity gravitational waves are not reflected at the object's surface but are trapped inside due to the exponentially strong redshift, and that the echo-delay time extremely exceeds the age of the Universe. Consequently, the echo signals are effectively unobservable, which is consistent with current null results from echo searches. We finally discuss how this quantum black hole and the conventional classical black hole could be distinguished observationally.

</details>


### [125] [Exact Thermodynamics and Two-Horizon Relations of Four Rotating Einstein-Maxwell-Maxwell-Dilaton Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06037)
*Hai Huang,Juhua Chen*

Main category: gr-qc
