TL;DR: This paper develops a complete thermodynamic framework for rotating black hole solutions (Kerr–Newman and Kaluza–Klein embeddings) in 4D Einstein–Maxwell–Maxwell–dilaton gravity without scalar potential, confirming the first law, Smarr formula, and scalar-shift identity, and uncovering a novel quadratic relation between horizon thermodynamic quantities involving the long-range-force parameter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend black hole thermodynamics to rotating, charged, dilatonic black holes in a consistent, asymptotically flat setting—specifically, to establish well-defined outer-horizon thermodynamics including magnetic patches and extremal limits where standard treatments often break down.

Method: Uses exact rotating solutions embedded from Kerr–Newman and Kaluza–Klein seeds in 4D Einstein–Maxwell–Maxwell–dilaton theory; employs constant-coefficient field redefinitions to fix couplings and charge variations; constructs single-valued mass functions; verifies thermodynamic identities on regular branches and finite-area extremal boundaries, incorporating magnetic patch contributions.

Result: Derives fully consistent outer-horizon thermodynamics: validated first law, Smarr relation, and scalar-shift identity; identifies a universal quadratic relation linking $2T_\pm S_\pm$ and $S_\pm \Omega_\pm / \pi$ across both horizons, with coefficients determined by asymptotic mass, electromagnetic charges, and scalar charge.

Conclusion: The thermodynamic structure of these rotating dilatonic black holes is robust and unified—even across distinct regular horizons and extremal boundaries—revealing a hidden algebraic constraint governed by the long-range-force parameter, strengthening the universality of black hole thermodynamics beyond Einstein–Maxwell.

Abstract: We establish complete outer-horizon thermodynamics for four exact rotating embeddings of Kerr--Newman and Kaluza--Klein seeds in four-dimensional, asymptotically flat Einstein--Maxwell--Maxwell--dilaton theory without a scalar potential. Constant-coefficient field maps determine the allowed couplings and charge variations. We obtain single-valued mass functions and verify the first law, Smarr relation, and scalar-shift identity on the stated regular branches and finite-area extremal boundaries, including magnetic patch contributions where required. For two distinct regular horizons with positive finite areas, the horizon combinations $2T_\pm S_\pm$ and $S_\pmΩ_\pm/π$ satisfy a common quadratic relation with the long-range-force parameter determined by the asymptotic mass, electromagnetic charges, and scalar charge.

</details>


### [126] [Black Hole Horizon Fluctuations from Unruh Quantum Noise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06223)
*Kashif Ammar Yasir*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a stochastic geometric reconstruction of Schwarzschild black holes using the full Unruh Wightman function to quantify how quantum stress fluctuations—beyond mean Hawking flux—affect horizon radius and surface gravity, revealing distinct sensitivities governed by covariance derivatives.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond the mean Hawking flux and capture how quantum field correlations—including vacuum contributions—influence black-hole geometry perturbations, which cannot be inferred from luminosity alone.

Method: Uses the complete exterior Unruh Wightman function; retains incoming vacuum and both outgoing frequency branches; computes connected stress-energy covariance before angular projection; propagates its monopole via density-driven radial response; applies shared Gaussian stochastic reconstruction (2,200 realizations) to lapse function value and derivative; separates quantum-state input from gravitational closure and spatial sampling assumptions.

Result: Quantifies relative statistical widths: 0.0185 for local horizon radius and 0.0483 for surface-gravity temperature parameter; demonstrates unequal sensitivity arises from covariance derivatives; validates consistency across histograms and reconstruction checks.

Conclusion: Field correlations—not just mean flux—govern geometric observables; the lapse value controls horizon position, its derivative controls surface gravity; the framework provides a reproducible, finite-resolution description of quantum-induced horizon and surface-gravity statistics.

Abstract: The mean Hawking flux does not determine how quantum stress fluctuations perturb a black-hole geometry. That information resides in field correlations, including vacuum contributions absent from the outgoing luminosity. Here we connect the complete exterior Unruh Wightman function to a finite-band stochastic reconstruction of Schwarzschild geometry. We retain the incoming vacuum and both outgoing frequency branches, form the connected stress covariance before angular projection, and propagate its monopole through a density-driven radial response. A shared Gaussian reconstruction then resolves how the value and derivative of the lapse control different geometric observables. For the specified covariance and noise amplitude, 2,200 realizations give relative widths of 0.0185 for the local horizon radius and 0.0483 for the surface-gravity temperature parameter, consistently across histograms and reconstruction checks. Covariance derivatives explain this unequal sensitivity. The construction separates the quantum-state input from the gravitational closure and spatial sampling assumptions, providing a reproducible finite-resolution description of how Hawking-field correlations affect horizon positions and surface-gravity statistics.

</details>


### [127] [Scalaron Quasinormal and Quasi-Bound Modes of Schwarzschild Black Holes in the Bondi-Sachs Formulation of Metric $f(R)$ Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06370)
*Bishop Mongwane,Didam G. A. Duniya,Seturumane Tema,Sanele Ziqubu,David F. Mota*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes scalaron quasinormal modes and quasi-bound states of Schwarzschild black holes in metric f(R) gravity using the Bondi-Sachs formalism, combining frequency-domain (Leaver’s method) and time-domain (characteristic evolution + matrix-pencil analysis) techniques; results confirm consistency between methods and show spectral dependence only on the dimensionless parameter μM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously study black hole perturbations in metric f(R) gravity—specifically the scalar degree of freedom (scalaron)—within a well-posed, characteristic framework (Bondi-Sachs), avoiding coordinate artifacts and enabling robust time-domain validation of frequency-domain predictions.

Method: Derives linearized scalar and tensor master equations from the Bondi-Sachs formulation of metric f(R) gravity; computes quasinormal and quasi-bound spectra via Leaver's continued-fraction method; performs fourth-order convergent characteristic time evolution of scalaron perturbations; applies matrix-pencil method to extracted waveforms for mode identification without boundary spectral assumptions.

Result: Scalar spectra depend solely on the dimensionless combination μM; both quasinormal and quasi-bound frequencies are computed and cross-validated across frequency- and time-domain methods; fundamental QNM frequencies agree within numerical uncertainty; for a finite-mass case, both oscillation and damping rates of the fundamental QNM and quasi-bound state are extracted from distinct time windows of a single waveform at one radius, without imposing boundary conditions tied to either branch.

Conclusion: The Bondi-Sachs framework provides a consistent, stable, and physically transparent setting for studying scalaron dynamics in f(R) gravity; time-domain evolution with matrix-pencil analysis is a reliable, assumption-light alternative to traditional spectral boundary conditions for identifying both QNMs and quasi-bound states.

Abstract: We study scalaron quasinormal modes and quasi-bound states of Schwarzschild black holes directly within the Bondi-Sachs formulation of metric $f(R)$ gravity, combining frequency-domain analyses with characteristic time evolution. Starting from the linearised characteristic equations, we obtain the massive scalar master equation and show that the tensor master equation takes the same form as in general relativity. For the analytic models considered here, the linear scalar spectra depend on the $f(R)$ theory only through the dimensionless combination $μM$. We compute both quasinormal and quasi-bound spectral branches using Leaver's continued-fraction method and compare them with independent literature benchmarks. To complement the frequency domain analysis, we evolve the scalaron perturbation equations using a fourth order convergent characteristic scheme. Applying matrix-pencil stability scans to the resulting waveforms, we independently recover fundamental quasinormal frequencies consistent with the continued-fraction results within the measured extraction-stability scales. Finally, for a selected finite-mass case, we demonstrate the extraction of both oscillation frequencies and damping rates of the fundamental quasinormal mode and quasi-bound state from separate time windows of the same waveform at one extraction radius, without imposing either spectral branch at the finite outer boundary.

</details>


### [128] [Time-domain analysis of scalar perturbations of Kerr--Newman black holes with Chern--Simons and Maxwell couplings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06393)
*Yan-Ming Chen,Hyat Huang,Meng-Yun Lai,De-Cheng Zou,Yun Soo Myung,Ming Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies scalarization onset in Kerr-Newman black holes within Einstein-Chern-Simons-Maxwell-scalar theory using hyperboloidal Rácz–Tóth evolution and zero-mode analysis, identifying threshold structures in coupling parameters η and γ̂.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the onset conditions for spontaneous scalarization of Kerr-Newman black holes in a modified gravity framework incorporating Chern–Simons and Maxwell–scalar couplings.

Method: Linear scalar perturbation analysis around Kerr–Newman black holes; hyperboloidal Rácz–Tóth time-domain evolution; stationary axisymmetric zero-mode computation; comparison of threshold curves with dynamical response.

Result: Two asymmetric real threshold branches in Maxwell coupling η emerge when γ̂ = 0; when γ̂ = η, branches have opposite signs and unequal magnitudes; for γ̂ ≠ η, branches merge as spin or CS coupling increases.

Conclusion: Scalarization thresholds exhibit rich coupling-dependent structure—merging behavior under unequal CS/Maxwell couplings signals a transition to complex (unstable) modes, implying critical dependence on both spin and coupling ratios.

Abstract: To study the onset of scalarization, we investigate linear scalar perturbations around Kerr--Newman black holes in the Einstein-Chern-Simons-Maxwell-scalar theory with quadratic Chern--Simons (CS) coupling $γ$ ($\hatγ=γ/M^2$) and Maxwell coupling $η$. For this purpose, we employ hyperboloidal Rácz--Tóth evolution and a stationary axisymmetric zero-mode computation. The resulting thresholds are compared with the time-domain response at representative points. For $\hatγ=0$, we find two asymmetric threshold branches in $η$, while for $\hatγ=η$, the two branches have opposite signs and unequal magnitudes. For unequal CS and Maxwell couplings, the two real threshold branches merge as the spin or CS coupling increases.

</details>


### [129] [Dyonic Simpson Visser black-bounce spacetime surrounded by an exotic Einstein cluster in general relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06523)
*Pedro Cañate,Joseph Sultana,Demosthenes Kazanas*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a dyonic (electric + magnetic) Simpson-Visser black-bounce spacetime as an exact GR solution sourced by Maxwell fields and an anisotropic Einstein cluster fluid with zero radial pressure—providing the exotic matter needed to maintain a regular, traversable throat.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a physically motivated, regular black-bounce spacetime that avoids singularities and satisfies energy conditions as much as possible, using a well-defined matter source—specifically, an Einstein cluster (for exotic support) and Maxwell electrodynamics (for charge).

Method: Solving the Einstein–Maxwell field equations exactly with an anisotropic fluid stress-energy tensor corresponding to an Einstein cluster (i.e., vanishing radial pressure, non-zero tangential pressure from circular geodesic motion), while imposing the dyonic Simpson-Visser metric ansatz.

Result: Derivation of an exact dyonic SV-BB solution where the Einstein cluster supplies the necessary negative radial pressure (effectively exotic matter) to sustain the bounce throat, and the electromagnetic field contributes both electric and magnetic charges; the resulting spacetime is singularity-free and asymptotically Reissner–Nordström.

Conclusion: The dyonic SV-BB spacetime is a viable regular black-bounce model grounded in physically interpretable matter content—combining classical electromagnetism and a kinetic (collisionless) cluster fluid—demonstrating that singularity resolution need not rely solely on ad hoc scalar fields or quantum gravity.

Abstract: In this work, we present the Simpson-Visser black-bounce (SV-BB) spacetime with both electric and magnetic charges (the dyonic SV-BB) as an exact solution of general relativity coupled to Maxwell electrodynamics and an anisotropic fluid with vanishing radial pressure. We assume the anisotropic fluid represents an Einstein cluster, which consists of a dense system of particles moving in circular orbits that generate only tangential pressure and zero radial pressure. We show that the dyonic SV-BB metric, which provides a regular black hole spacetime, can be obtained as an exact solution to the Einstein field equations sourced by a combination of an Einstein cluster (which is responsible for providing the exotic matter required to keep a black-bounce throat open) and an electromagnetic field in the framework of Maxwell electrodynamics.

</details>


### [130] [Cherenkov Bursts are Memory Steps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06528)
*Jann Zosso,Guangzi Xu,Vitor Cardoso*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper reinterprets the Cherenkov radiation from a finite superluminal charge track as interference between two electromagnetic memory transitions induced by endpoint accelerations; this explains the finite burst via cancellation of divergent memory contributions and suggests the Askaryan effect as a probe for resolving macroscopic memory transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a new physical interpretation of the finite Cherenkov burst—beyond standard wavefront arguments—by linking it to electromagnetic memory effects and exploring implications for infrared physics.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic analysis of the transverse vector potential generated by a finite superluminal charge track, focusing on memory steps induced by endpoint accelerations and their interference in the radiation zone.

Result: Cherenkov radiation emerges from destructive interference of two opposite, temporally separated memory steps in the vector potential; their amplitude divergence and temporal overlap near the Cherenkov angle yield a finite, directional burst.

Conclusion: Cherenkov radiation is a memory-interference phenomenon, and the Askaryan effect offers a viable experimental platform to resolve individual macroscopic electromagnetic memory transitions, bridging classical radiation and infrared structure.

Abstract: We show that the familiar Cherenkov burst from a finite superluminal charge track can be understood as a pair of interfering electromagnetic memory transitions. Endpoint accelerations of the track produce opposite memory steps in the transverse vector potential, such that the corresponding finite-frequency radiation is universally fixed, below the endpoint-acceleration-scale, by the memory amplitude and the time for which the temporary memory offset persists. As the Cherenkov direction is approached, the memory amplitudes grow while their separation in observer time shrinks, until the two vector-potential steps overlap and their divergent contributions cancel into a finite Cherenkov burst. This perspective opens the door to using the Askaryan effect as a setting in which individual macroscopic memory transitions may be resolved, providing a new experimental arena for memory-linked infrared physics.

</details>


### [131] [Puncture Evolution with Physics-Informed Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06776)
*Estuti Shukla,Ashwin Suriyanarayanan,David Radice,Romit Maulik,Sebastiano Bernuzzi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper applies physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) to solve the vacuum Einstein equations in spherical symmetry under the moving-puncture gauge, demonstrating interpolation, extrapolation, and reconstruction of black-hole spacetimes—including recovering Schwarzschild interior from exterior data without explicit gauge constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore PINNs as a novel, differentiable, data-efficient alternative to traditional numerical relativity (NR) methods—particularly for solving ill-posed or boundary-limited problems in general relativity, such as reconstructing spacetime geometry from sparse or incomplete data.

Method: Designs a PINN architecture encoding lapse, shift, and Z4c conformal variables (χ, ̃γₙᵣ) that remain regular at the puncture; minimizes a composite loss comprising NR data fidelity, Ricci tensor residuals (enforcing vacuum Einstein equations), and moving-puncture gauge condition residuals.

Result: PINNs successfully interpolate NR data, evolve black-hole spacetimes forward from early-time/outer-boundary data, and—remarkably—recover the interior of Schwarzschild spacetime from exterior-only data by implicitly learning a coordinate transformation to Kerr–Schild form, despite the inverse problem being ill-posed.

Conclusion: PINNs offer a promising, compact, and differentiable framework for spacetime reconstruction and surrogate modeling in NR, though limitations include sensitivity to loss balancing, gauge ambiguity, and scalability beyond spherical symmetry; future work includes extension to higher dimensions and binary systems.

Abstract: We investigate the application of physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) to solve the vacuum Einstein equations in spherical symmetry. Our neural network architecture is designed to solve the 1+1D moving-puncture evolution, by minimizing a loss that combines a data term, the Ricci tensor of the reconstructed spacetime, and the moving-puncture gauge conditions. It represents the lapse, the shift and the Z4c conformal variables $χ$ and $\widetildeγ_{rr}$, which remain regular at the puncture. We consider the evolution of a single, non-spinning black-hole in the puncture gauge and show that PINNs can interpolate numerical relativity (NR) data, predict its evolution, and reconstruct spacetime in regions where no data is provided. As an interpolant, the network provides a compact, differentiable representation of the NR data. Given only early-time and outer-boundary data, it evolves the puncture forward in time and reproduces the main features of the NR solution. Given only exterior data and no gauge condition, an ill-posed inverse problem, it recovers the interior of a Schwarzschild spacetime in coordinates of its own choosing, which we verify by learning the coordinate transformation to the Kerr--Schild form. Finally, we assess the strengths and limitations of PINNs for NR and outline their prospects for future applications.

</details>
